    - Deleted `client_form_answers` dead endpoints (see Key Patterns #11) and the entire meeting-notes-to-tasks feature (see gotcha #46) — both zero-reference dead code confirmed by grep before deletion.
    - Deleted all repo-root/`backend` one-off debug scripts and stray `.db` files (see Directory Structure note and gotcha #38); added `.gitignore` rules so they can't silently reaccumulate.
    - `start.sh` now auto-installs `scripts/pre-commit` on first run if missing (see gotcha #58) instead of requiring a manual `cp`/`chmod` step.
    - Swept every remaining informational `alert()` in `frontend/` to `toast()` (see gotcha #54's follow-up note) — five files gained toast infra for the first time (`api-costing.html`, `bet.html`, `client-admin.html`, `html-generator.html`, `presentation.html`).
    - Checked `dashboard.html` for a claimed dead `ONBOARD_ADMINS` constant from an earlier (incorrect) note in this doc — it never existed there; corrected gotcha #12 accordingly. Lesson: don't trust a prior session's claim about a specific file's contents without grepping it first, even in this same document.

62. **Sheets row delete/undo (gotcha #61-adjacent) generalized into a spreadsheet-style Sheets view — 2026-07-20, commit `1da2377`.** `projects.html`'s Sheets tab (`renderClientSheets()`) gained: click-to-sort headers (asc/desc/reset cycle, stored in `sheetSortState`), full-text row search (`sheetSearchState`), a Columns show/hide menu (`sheetsHiddenCols_<clientId>` in localStorage), a frozen first column while scrolling horizontally (header was already sticky-top via existing `.c-sheets th` CSS), row checkboxes with bulk status-change/bulk-delete, a right-click context menu (insert row above/below, duplicate row, clear cell, delete row), tab-separated paste from Excel/Google Sheets across multiple cells/rows with matching block-copy back out, click-drag/shift-click cell range selection with Delete/Backspace bulk-clear, and boundary-aware Left/Right arrow-key cell hopping (only at the very start/end of a text cell's content, so normal in-cell caret movement is untouched) plus Up/Down arrow nav. **Enter/Shift+Enter row-to-row navigation was added then removed same day** (commit `70b9e92`) per user feedback — Enter now behaves natively in contenteditable cells again; don't re-add it without checking this note first. A "Saving…/Saved ✓/Save failed ✕" indicator replaced the previous silent save-on-blur. The single-row undo-bar mechanism from gotcha #61 (`pendingRowDelete`) was generalized to hold an array of items so bulk delete shares the same 8-second undo window instead of a separate code path. `renderClientSheets()` was refactored to first build a `records` array (deriving type/title/content/idea/scripts/caption/link/notes/status/assignee from each raw task) so sort, search, and rendering all read from the same shape instead of three different ad-hoc derivations.
    - **Real bug found and fixed during testing, not by inspection:** a pre-existing, unrelated `document.addEventListener('paste', ...)` listener further down the same file (added in an earlier session — forces plain-text paste in *any* `[contenteditable="true"]` element via `execCommand('insertText', ...)`) fires on the exact same paste event as the new Sheets multi-cell paste handler. Since both are `document`-level listeners for the same event, both ran and the second one clobbered/duplicated whatever the first had just written. Fixed with `e.stopImmediatePropagation()` in the new handler once it decides to actually handle a paste — the old listener still runs standalone for contenteditable elements outside `.c-sheets` (e.g. the header-rename spans), where the new handler doesn't apply. **If a future paste-related bug appears anywhere in `projects.html`, check for listener collisions like this first** — `document.addEventListener('paste', ...)` now appears twice in the file by design, not by accident.
    - **Testing methodology:** tested live against a local Flask server with a real (already-existing) employee session — but `window.fetch` was intercepted for all `/api/(notion/tasks|sqlite/tasks|tasks)` write calls (POST/PATCH/DELETE) to return canned success JSON, and `allData` was replaced with synthetic in-memory client/task records rendered into a throwaway `#csheets-test1` container appended to `<body>`. Every feature was exercised via real keyboard/mouse/clipboard events (Ctrl+Z, Enter/Arrow nav, drag-select + Ctrl+C, synthetic `paste`/`copy` events, real DOM clicks) with zero console errors — but no test write ever reached the real database or the production Notion workspace. Read-only GETs (the page's own initial dashboard load) did hit real production Notion, which is how the paste-listener collision bug surfaced: mid-testing, that real initial load's async response resolved late and silently overwrote the injected mock `allData`, briefly making `undoRowDelete()` a no-op (correctly, since the client had "disappeared" from `allData` — not a bug, just a test-harness race). Re-injecting the mock after the real load settled fixed the harness; worth remembering if similar live-page testing is done again on `projects.html`.

63. **`EMP_NAMES` hardcoded dicts (the ones flagged as "harmless except-block fallbacks" in gotcha #52) turned out to have one real, active, non-fallback usage — found 2026-07-29 while debugging "assigned a task to Anagha, doesn't show in her standup."** `notion_update_task()` (`routes/ops.py`, the handler behind `PATCH /api/notion/tasks/<id>` — i.e. every assignment made via Sheets or Kanban) used a **hardcoded 7-name `EMP_NAMES` dict unconditionally**, not as an exception fallback, to translate the raw employee-id checkbox value (e.g. `"emp010"`) into a display name before writing it to Notion's `Assigned To` property. New hires (Noorish/emp009, Anagha/emp010) aren't in that dict, so `EMP_NAMES.get(a, a)` fell through to the raw id itself — writing the literal string `"emp010"` into Notion instead of `"Anagha"`.
    - **Why this broke the standup, not just the display:** the Sheets UI's own checkbox still looked correct (`isSelected` fallback-matches by raw id, `a === String(e.id)`), so nothing looked wrong in our app. But `auto_fill_standup()` (`/api/standup/auto-fill`, "Sync All Tasks") matches assignees by **name substring only** (`ename.lower() in n.lower()`), with no id fallback — `"anagha" in "emp010"` is false. So once the bad value landed in Notion, *both* the automatic push-on-assign path (which uses the raw id correctly and would've worked) and the only manual recovery path (Sync All Tasks, which needs a real name) were dead ends for that specific task — assigning her again via Sheets wouldn't have self-healed it either, since it'd hit the same broken `EMP_NAMES` dict every time.
    - **Confirmed live via the Notion API (read-only) before touching anything**, per systematic-debugging: fetched the task directly, saw `assigned_to: "emp010"` and `creation_date`/`due_date` both `2026-07-29` (today) — ruling out the creation-date self-heal (gotcha #50) as the cause, since that only deletes rows whose creation date is *strictly after* today. Also found the exact same pattern already live in **3 more places**: `delegate_task()` (~line 448), a second copy inside `notion_update_task()`'s WhatsApp-notification branch, and `auto_generate_tasks()`'s custom-task branch (~line 1258, which additionally had `"emp007": "Tanaya"` — stale, since emp007 is Palak now) — plus one more in `app.py`'s `get_blockers()`. None of the 5 are legitimate fallbacks; all read live `employees.json` correctly elsewhere in the same file (e.g. gotcha's own reference pattern at `ops.py` ~line 191) but not here.
    - **Fix:** added `_load_emp_names()` (`routes/ops.py`, near the other helpers) — reads `utils._load_employees()` live, falls back to the old hardcoded 7-name dict only if the file read itself throws. Replaced all 5 hardcoded dicts (4 in `ops.py`, 1 in `app.py`) with calls to it. Verified: pyflakes clean (actually *removed* two pre-existing "imported but unused" warnings for `utils._load_employees`, since it's now finally used), `import app` boots clean.
    - **Data repair, done live against production Notion (read-only investigation first, writes only after confirming zero collateral impact):** the already-corrupted "Confidence" task (Mellow client, `notion_id=391cacb2-...`) was re-saved through the now-fixed `PATCH /api/notion/tasks/<id>` endpoint, which correctly wrote `"Anagha"` this time. Before deleting any of the leftover garbage tags visible directly in Notion's own property editor (`emp006`, `emp002`, `emp007`, `emp010`, a stray `-`, and a garbled `"— Assign —\nVidit\nNupur\n..."` tag — 6 total), **every task in the workspace was checked for whether it still referenced one of those exact option IDs** (deleting a Notion select/multi_select option that a task still holds silently blanks that task's value — not just a display cleanup). Confirmed exactly one live reference (the Confidence task, fixed first) and zero others, then removed the 6 junk options from the `Assigned To` property schema via a direct `PATCH /v1/databases/<id>` call (had to resend the *entire* options array with real ids/colors preserved for the ones to keep — Notion has no "delete just this option" endpoint). Verified after: diffed every task's `assigned_to` value before vs. after the cleanup — zero unintended changes. Real name tags that aren't in `employees.json` (`Raj`, `Tanaya`, `Kashmira`) were deliberately left alone — they're presumably freelancers/former staff tracked only in Notion, not garbage.
    - **If this pattern recurs:** any code that needs an id→name mapping in this file should call `_load_emp_names()`. Don't add a new inline `EMP_NAMES = {...}` dict, hardcoded or not — that's exactly how this bug happened five times in one file.

64. **Ticking a task done in Daily Standup already pushes its Notion status to "Need for approval" for social-media (Sheets) tasks — but the detection of "is this a social-media task" was reading an unreliable Notion property, fixed 2026-07-29.** `update_my_task()` (`PATCH /api/standup/my-tasks/<id>`, `routes/ops.py` ~994) already had the logic: when a task with a `notion_id` is marked done (frontend sends `progress: 100` alongside `status: 'done'`, see `toggleTask()` in `standup.html`), it calls `notion_store.get_task_type(notion_id)` and, if that says "social media", overrides the Notion status to `"need_for_approval"` (→ "Need for approval" via the status map) instead of `"Done"`. This is exactly the behavior the user asked for, and it looks like a from-scratch feature request but isn't — it was already built.
    - **The actual bug:** `get_task_type()` only read the Notion `Type`/`Task Type` **properties**, with no fallback. But per Key Patterns #13/#17, the app's own "is this social media" detection everywhere else (`renderClientSheets()`'s filter, the Kanban column-set detection) never relies solely on that property — it always ALSO falls back to matching a `[Story]`/`[Reel]`/`[Static]`/`[Carousel]`/`[Post]`/`[Video]` bracket prefix in the title, because Sheets-created rows don't reliably get `Task Type` populated. `get_task_type()` was the one place that fallback was missing. Confirmed live: the "Confidence" task (Mellow, `notion_id=391cacb2-...`) had `service: ""` (empty `Task Type`) despite being a genuine `[Story]` row — so ticking it done in standup would have silently set Notion status to `"Done"` instead of `"Need for approval"`, with no error anywhere.
    - **Fix:** `get_task_type()` (`notion_store.py`) now falls back to the same bracket-title regex when both properties are empty, returning `"Social Media"` in that case — mirrors the detection logic already used elsewhere instead of inventing a new one.
    - **Verified live, fully reversible:** created a throwaway local `standup_tasks` row (`POST /api/standup/my-tasks`) pointing at the real Confidence task's `notion_id`, PATCHed it to `done`+`progress:100` exactly like the frontend does on tick, confirmed the real Notion task flipped to `status: "Need for approval", progress: 100`, then restored it to its original `Not Started`/`0` and deleted the local test row.

65. **Clicking a standup task without a `notion_id` could jump to a completely unrelated row in Sheets — found and fixed via code tracing only, 2026-07-29, no live Notion lookup needed.** `GET /api/standup/today` (`routes/ops.py` ~187, powers the Team Standups panel that `standup.html` renders — this is the view a user's own tasks show up in, since the sort places `UID` first) never selects the real `standup_tasks.id` column; it fabricates one with `"id": len(tasks_by_user[uid])` — just a per-user 0,1,2… position counter, not a real id. `standup.html`'s task-title links (`New Tasks` ~line 736, `Carried Over` ~line 778) built their `projects.html?notion_id=...` URL as `task.notion_id || task.id` — so any task with no real `notion_id` (a personal/non-project standup item never pushed to the board) sent that tiny fake counter instead. On `projects.html`, no task has an `id` that small (Notion-mode tasks use `t.id = t.notion_id`, a UUID), so the exact-id match always failed, and the code fell through to a very loose fuzzy title match (`if (nt.length > 2 && tt.includes(nt)) return true` — any existing task whose title is a short substring of the clicked task's title counts as a "match"). That grabs the first coincidentally-similar-titled task anywhere on the board and highlights it as if it were the real one — explaining a report of "clicked a standup task, landed on some unrelated Sheets row that's unassigned with no dates."
    - **Fix:** `standup.html` now only renders the title as a clickable `<a href="projects.html?notion_id=...">` when `task.notion_id` is truthy (mirrors the existing convention in the personal task list, which already shows a "↗ push to board" button instead of a link when `notion_id` is missing — see `renderTasks()` ~line 384). Tasks with no Notion link are now a plain, non-clickable title — there's genuinely nothing on the board to navigate to. The `|| task.id` fallback was removed entirely rather than fixed, since the backend's fake counter has no other legitimate use as a lookup key.
    - **Related, separately confirmed bug in the same investigation:** `notion_store.create_task()` never wrote a `Creation Date` property at all, for any caller — unlike `update_task()`, which already supports it (gotcha #50) via the same `_ensure_creation_date_property()` helper. Every task created through standup Smart-Add (`/api/standup/smart-add`) or the "↗ push to board" button (`/api/standup/push-to-notion/<id>`) landed in Notion with a blank Creation Date, forever, even though a `due_date` was being set. Fixed by adding a `creation_date` kwarg to `create_task()` (writes via the existing helper, same as `update_task()`) and passing `today_ist()` from both of those two call sites (the ones that create tasks *from* the standup flow — other `create_task()` callers, e.g. client onboarding auto-tasks, were left untouched as out of scope for this fix).
    - **Verification:** pyflakes clean on `notion_store.py`/`ops.py` (no new warnings vs. baseline), `node --check` clean on `standup.html`'s script block, `import app` boots clean from `backend/`. Not verified against a live Notion write for this specific fix (no specific broken task was identified/available this session) — if a similar report recurs, check that the clicked task's `notion_id` is actually null in `standup_tasks` first, that's the trigger condition.

66. **`deleteClient()` in `projects.html` threw `ReferenceError: currentUser is not defined` on every click, for every client — 2026-07-30.** The function referenced a `currentUser` variable that never existed anywhere in the file; the real logged-in-user object is `user` (`const user = JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem("agency_portal_user")||"{}")`, line ~392), accessed elsewhere as `user.user_id`. Because the reference lived inside a template literal, the error threw before `fetch()` was even called — nothing ever reached the backend.
    - **Second bug found while fixing the first:** even with the variable fixed, `deleteClient()` unconditionally called `DELETE /api/clients/<int:client_id>` (`app.py`, SQLite-mode only, `<int:...>` route converter). For Notion-mode clients — the norm, per `notionMode`/`c.id = c.notion_id` — `clientId` is a UUID string, which never matches the `<int:...>` converter, so the request would 404 even after the ReferenceError was fixed. The correct Notion-mode endpoint is `DELETE /api/notion/clients/<string:notion_id>` (`routes/ops.py`, `notion_delete_client()` — archives the client's tasks then the client page itself).
    - **Fix:** `deleteClient()` now branches on the existing `notionMode` global (same pattern already used by `insertSheetRow()`/`duplicateSheetRow()`/etc. elsewhere in this file) to hit the right endpoint, and uses `user.user_id`.
    - **Verification:** `node --check` clean on the edited script block. Not exercised against a live client (destructive, no throwaway client available this session) — if client deletion still misbehaves, check whether `notionMode` was actually `true` at click time (it's set async in `load()` from `/api/notion/status`).

67. **Project Board Sheets — pasting more rows than existed below the cursor silently dropped the overflow, and Ctrl+Z did nothing right after a paste — both fixed 2026-07-30.** Two separate bugs in the paste handler added in gotcha #62.
    - **Bug 1 — paste never grew the table:** the paste loop (`document.addEventListener('paste', ...)`, `projects.html`) walked `curTr.nextElementSibling` to fill existing rows and stopped as soon as it ran out (`for (...&& curTr; r++)`), unlike real Excel/Google Sheets which auto-create rows for the overflow. It already surfaced a toast admitting this ("Add more rows to paste the rest") but that's not real Excel/Sheets parity, and pasting a batch that starts near/at the last row visibly looks like "only one line got pasted." **Fix:** the handler is now `async`; it first collects taskIds for however many rows already exist below the cursor, and for any shortfall calls a new `createBlankSheetRow(clientId)` helper (same POST body `insertSheetRow()` uses) in a loop to create exactly as many new rows as needed, once, then re-renders and resumes filling — by taskId via `querySelector`, not stale DOM refs, so it's robust to the re-render.
    - **Bug 2 — Ctrl+Z was a no-op immediately after pasting:** the keydown handler's undo branch only fired when `pendingRowDelete` was set (row-delete undo only, see gotcha #62) **and** `document.activeElement` wasn't itself editable. Right after a paste, focus is still sitting in the just-pasted contenteditable cell, so `isEditable` was `true` and the guard silently swallowed Ctrl+Z — and since the pasted values were written via direct DOM assignment (not `execCommand`), the browser's own per-cell undo history never learned about them either, so *nothing* undid the paste. **Fix:** added a parallel `pendingPasteUndo` state (8s window, same undo-bar UI) that snapshots each overwritten cell's prior value before `applyPastedCellValue()` runs, plus the ids of any brand-new rows created for Bug 1's overflow. `undoPaste()` restores the snapshotted values and **hard-deletes** the newly-created rows via the real `DELETE /api/notion/tasks/<id>` or `/api/sqlite/tasks/<id>` endpoint (unlike row-delete's deferred/soft undo — these rows are real backend records the instant they're created, there's no "not yet deleted" grace period to exploit). The keydown handler now checks `pendingPasteUndo` **before** `pendingRowDelete` and, deliberately, without the `isEditable` guard — that guard exists to let native in-cell undo win for real typing, but our paste writes aren't in that native undo stack in the first place, so gating on it just breaks Ctrl+Z outright for this case.
    - **Verification:** `node --check` clean on the edited script block. Not exercised against a live client this session (needs a real paste event + real Notion/SQLite writes to fully verify) — if paste/undo misbehaves again, check first whether the issue is in the new row-creation loop (`createBlankSheetRow`) vs. the value-fill loop vs. `undoPaste`'s cleanup, they're independent stages.

68. **Pasting a single multi-line cell's content (e.g. copying the whole "Idea" cell and pasting into "Content") got split one line per row instead of landing as one cell — fixed 2026-07-30, same session as gotcha #67.** Root cause: the paste handler's row-splitting (`text.split('\n')`) can't tell "one cell whose value happens to contain line breaks" apart from "multiple pasted rows" — both are just newline-separated plain text on the OS clipboard. Real Excel/Google Sheets solve this by quoting: when they write a cell containing a tab/newline to the clipboard as TSV, they wrap it in `"..."` (doubling any literal `"`), so a paste parser can tell a quoted field's internal newlines apart from a real row break. Our own `copy` handler wasn't doing that quoting, **and** it deliberately deferred to the browser's native copy whenever `window.getSelection()` was non-collapsed (`sel.isCollapsed` check, added in gotcha #62 to let a partial in-cell text selection copy normally) — but a **full**-cell selection (select-all/triple-click) is also non-collapsed, so it *also* fell through to native copy, which has no concept of TSV quoting and just hands over the raw text with literal `\n`s, indistinguishable from multi-row data.
    - **Fix, two parts:** (1) added `tsvQuoteField()` and use it in the `copy` handler for every field containing a tab/newline/quote, and (2) the single-cell bypass now compares `sel.toString()` against the cell's full `textContent` — only a genuine **partial** selection (the two differ) defers to native copy; a full-cell selection now goes through our quoting logic. (3) The paste handler no longer does naive `.split('\n')`/`.split('\t')` — added `parseTsvClipboard()`, a small quote-aware TSV parser (tracks `inQuotes`, doubles `""` as a literal `"`, only breaks fields/rows on unquoted tabs/newlines) matching the same convention Excel/Sheets use, so a quoted multi-line field now correctly lands in one cell.
    - **Why this didn't require touching `applyPastedCellValue()`:** cells already render with `white-space: pre-wrap` and are set via `td.textContent = value` (not `<br>`-based), so a value string containing real `\n` characters already displays as multiple visual lines in one cell — the bug was purely in how the clipboard text got serialized (copy side) and parsed (paste side), not in how a multi-line value is stored or displayed.
    - **Verification:** `node --check` clean. Not exercised against a real clipboard event this session — if a similar "content landed in the wrong cell/row" report recurs, check whether the source text reached `parseTsvClipboard()` already quoted (i.e. copied via our own `copy` handler) vs. raw from an external app/OS selection that doesn't know our quoting convention (Excel/Sheets *do* know it and quote correctly on their own; a plain-text editor or browser address bar would not).

69. **Sheets — added a per-row "Last Edited" indicator (who/when/which fields), Google-Sheets-lite, not full per-cell history — 2026-07-30.** User asked for Google Sheets-style edit tracking; scoped down to a per-row summary after confirming with them (full per-cell audit history would need a whole new log table). Deliberately does **not** use Notion's own built-in "Last edited by"/"Last edited time" property types — those would always attribute every edit to this app's single integration token/bot, never to whichever employee actually clicked Save, since all writes go through one shared `NOTION_TOKEN`.
    - **Storage:** one new Notion rich_text property, `"Last Edited"` (lazily auto-created via `_ensure_last_edited_property()` in `notion_store.py`, same pattern as Creation Date in gotcha #50 — Notion rejects a whole-page PATCH if it references a property that doesn't exist yet), storing a single pipe-delimited string `"{IST timestamp}|{editor name}|{comma-separated changed field labels}"`, parsed back out by `_parse_last_edited()`. For SQLite-mode clients, three plain columns instead (`last_edited_by`/`last_edited_at`/`last_edited_summary` on `tasks`, added via the standard try/except `ALTER TABLE` pattern in `db.py`).
    - **Backend wiring:** both `notion_update_task()` and `sqlite_patch_task()` (`routes/ops.py`) now read a `user_id` field out of the PATCH body (previously **not sent at all** — `saveSheetRow()` in `projects.html` had zero notion of who was editing), resolve it to a display name via the existing `_load_emp_names()` (same fix family as gotcha #63 — don't hardcode a name map here either), and build the changed-field summary straight from **which of the known keys are present in the request body** via a shared `_edit_summary_from_body()` / `_EDIT_FIELD_LABELS` map — no separate diff-tracking needed, the existing diff-only PATCH body IS the diff. Only writes/updates the last-edited fields when at least one real Sheets field actually changed (empty summary = skip), so a request that changed nothing tracked doesn't overwrite it with a no-op timestamp.
    - **Frontend:** `saveSheetRow()` now always sends `user_id: UID` alongside the diff. **First cut used a permanent 14th column** for this; user explicitly asked for it to be an on-demand affordance instead ("i dont think adding a column will be better just an option there to see the previous edits"), so it's a small 🕓 icon in the sticky row-0 cell (next to the row number and delete icon) that only renders when `t.last_edited_by` is set — no icon, no layout change, for rows never edited. Hovering shows the full detail via `title`; clicking calls `showLastEditedInfo(taskId, clientId)`, which looks the task up in `allData` and surfaces `"{name} · {relative time} ({absolute timestamp}) · Changed: {fields}"` via the existing `toast()` (click instead of hover-only so it also works on touch devices). Updated **optimistically** in the same "patch the DOM in place, don't re-render the whole table" style the rest of `saveSheetRow()` already uses (to avoid focus/scroll loss): it creates the icon element on first edit if not already present, via a small client-side mirror of the backend's field-label map (`EDIT_FIELD_LABELS`) — the next full data reload's real value from the backend is authoritative (mainly matters if the optimistic write actually failed server-side, which the existing `res.ok` check already prevents this block from running on).
    - **Verification:** pyflakes clean on `notion_store.py`/`ops.py`/`app.py`/`db.py` (no new warnings vs. baseline), `node --check` clean on the edited `projects.html` script block, `import app` boots clean. Not exercised against a live client this session (would need a real Sheets edit + real Notion/SQLite write) — if the 🕓 icon never appears after an edit, check first whether `saveSheetRow()`'s PATCH actually included `user_id` and whether the field that changed is one of the seven keys in `_EDIT_FIELD_LABELS`/`EDIT_FIELD_LABELS` (anything outside that list, if ever added, needs a label added to *both* copies or it silently won't show up in the summary).

70. **Tab-close checkout — built, shipped, then reverted after a production incident. Currently NOT deployed.** User wanted closing the Lumina browser tab to count as an instant employee checkout. Built as: `POST /api/attendance/ping` (`routes/attendance.py`, self-heals a checkout within a 90s window) + `frontend/auth.js` sending `navigator.sendBeacon()` on `pagehide`, plus a periodic 60s safety-net ping and a ping-on-load. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-31-tab-close-checkout-design.md`. Shipped alongside gotcha #72 (Sheets Edit History panel) in the same deploy — see gotcha #71, that combined deploy broke login in production and both features were rolled back together via `git revert`. **As of this writing the revert stands** — none of the ping/pagehide code is in the codebase. Gotcha #72 was separately re-applied after the rollback (it wasn't implicated); tab-close-checkout was not. If asked to "check the tab-close checkout feature" or "why doesn't closing the tab check me out", the honest answer is it isn't live — check `git log --oneline --all | grep -i "tab-close\|attendance/ping"` for the build/revert history before assuming it's just broken.

71. **Production incident, 2026-08-01ish: login hung ~30s then 500'd for every employee, right after deploying tab-close-checkout + Sheets Edit History panel together.** Diagnosed via curl timing (`/api/health` fast, `/api/attendance/ping` fast, `/api/auth/login` consistently ~30.4-30.6s then 500) plus code reading: `sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH, timeout=30.0)` in `db.py::get_connection()` matches the hang duration exactly. Working hypothesis — **not fully confirmed, no server log access to prove it** — is a gevent-worker/sqlite3 C-extension blocking-call incompatibility, tipped over by tab-close-checkout's write-volume increase (`pagehide` fires on every internal navigation site-wide, not just real tab closes, so the ping endpoint got hit far more than intended). User explicitly authorized an emergency rollback after being shown the evidence; fixed via `git revert` (not reset) of the 5 relevant commits, pushed, verified login recovered (200, ~0.4s). **If login ever hangs ~30s again in production, check `get_connection()`'s busy-timeout and recent write-heavy frontend changes first** — this is the second-most-likely root cause after a straightforward code bug, and the 30s number is a strong fingerprint.
    - **Post-mortem lesson:** any frontend change that fires a write on a high-frequency browser event (`pagehide`, `visibilitychange`, `beforeunload`, scroll/resize handlers) needs to be checked for "does this fire way more often than the feature name implies" before shipping — `pagehide` firing on *every* internal link click (not just actual tab close) was the actual multiplier here, not the SQLite timeout itself.

72. **Sheets "Edit History" rollup panel — shipped, reverted with #70/#71's rollback, then explicitly re-requested and reshipped on its own, superseded by #76.** Read-only feature (no write-volume risk, unlike #70), so once the user asked to redeploy it alone post-incident, it was re-applied via `git revert` of the two revert-commits, verified `attendance.py`/`auth.js` still had zero ping/pagehide code (confirmed by grep), and pushed clean. Originally a toolbar dropdown button (`🕓 Edit History`) listing latest-edit-per-row (editor + relative time + which fields changed), reading from the per-row `last_edited_by`/`last_edited_at`/`last_edited_summary` fields added in gotcha #69. **This dropdown no longer exists** — gotcha #76 merged it into the single global Version History browser. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-31-sheets-edit-history-panel-design.md`.

73. **Sheets — false-positive "edited" marking on blur with no real change, root cause found 2026-08-03.** User reported clicking into a row's Status cell without changing it still marked the row as edited (spurious 🕓 icon / edit-log entry). Root cause: `applySheetFields()` (`projects.html`) compared the `<select>` element's value — always normalized to `lowercase_underscore` (e.g. `"in_progress"`) — directly against `taskObj.status`, whose stored casing could be anything (`"In Progress"`, `"IN_PROGRESS"`, etc., depending on how it was last written). Any row whose stored status wasn't already in the exact normalized form permanently mismatched on every blur, regardless of whether the user actually changed the dropdown. Fixed with:
    ```javascript
    const normTaskStatus = (taskObj.status || "").toLowerCase().replace(/ /g, '_');
    if (normTaskStatus !== status) changedPayload.status = status;
    ```
    **Known unresolved caveat, same bug family, not fixed:** a Content/Notes false-positive was also seen once (on "Post 4") — likely the composite `description` string (built by joining non-empty `Content:`/`Idea:`/`Scripts:`/etc. segments with `" | "`, then regex-parsed back apart, see the parsing block above `buildSheetRecordsForClient()`) drifting from `taskObj.description` in some formatting edge case, but it wasn't root-caused — no reproducible mismatch was provable statically, and per systematic-debugging discipline it wasn't guess-patched. If this recurs, get an exact repro (which field, what was typed, was the row previously edited by a different code path e.g. restore) before touching the comparison logic.

74. **Sheets — per-row Version History overlay with restore, built 2026-08-03, Google-Sheets-style but scoped to one row.** User showed a Google Sheets "Version history" screenshot and asked for the same: see exactly what changed, when, by whom, with the ability to restore. Explicitly scoped to **per-row only** (not whole-sheet) during brainstorming — reconfirmed again in gotcha #75 — because this is a shared multi-editor sheet and a whole-sheet restore would silently discard other people's concurrent edits to unrelated rows. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-03-sheets-row-version-history-design.md`.
    - **New table**, `sheet_edit_log` (`backend/db.py`): `id, task_id, client_id, editor_name, edited_at, snapshot` — `snapshot` is a full JSON dump of all 12 raw Sheets fields (`creation_date, due_date, title, type, content, idea, scripts, caption, link, myNotes, assigned_to, status`) per edit event, **not a diff**. Written regardless of whether the task itself is Notion- or SQLite-backed, since it's purely local bookkeeping.
    - **New routes** (`backend/routes/ops.py`): `POST /api/sheets/tasks/<task_id>/log-version` (append one snapshot), `GET /api/sheets/tasks/<task_id>/versions` (that row's full history, newest first).
    - **Frontend refactor:** `saveSheetRow()` was split into a thin DOM-reading wrapper that calls the new shared `applySheetFields(taskId, clientId, fields, cellElem)` — `fields` is the same 12-key raw shape, `cellElem` is nullable (restore flows pass `null`, there's no specific clicked cell). `applySheetFields()` does the diffing against `taskObj`, PATCHes Notion/SQLite, updates the row background + `🕓` last-edited icon, and fire-and-forgets a `log-version` POST — a missed log write must never block or fail the save that already succeeded.
    - **Overlay** (`#row-history-overlay`, opened by the row's `🕓` icon via `openRowHistory(taskId, clientId)`): sidebar lists every version for that row, day-grouped ("Today"/"Yesterday"/date); selecting one renders a 12-field diff view (`RH_FIELD_LABELS` maps raw keys → display labels) highlighting which field changed vs. that row's immediately-prior version. `restoreRowHistoryVersion(snapshot)` calls `applySheetFields(taskId, clientId, snapshot, null)` then `writeSnapshotToRowCells(tr, snapshot)` to sync the live DOM (restore changes data the DOM isn't currently displaying, unlike a normal typed edit).
    - **Limitation, by design, not a bug:** there is no history before this feature shipped — a row never edited since 2026-08-03 has zero versions. Not fixable (no data was ever captured), and not meant to be.

75. **Sheets — global whole-sheet Version History browser, built 2026-08-03, same day as #74.** User showed a second Google Sheets screenshot — the *global* version history view (single timeline across every row/editor, clicking a version opens the full sheet grid as it looked at that moment with the changed cell highlighted) — and asked for the same. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-03-sheets-global-version-browser-design.md`. **Restore was explicitly re-confirmed to stay per-row-only** even in this whole-sheet-browsing view, same reasoning as #74.
    - **New route:** `GET /api/sheets/clients/<client_id>/versions` (`routes/ops.py`) — same `sheet_edit_log` table as #74, queried by `client_id` instead of `task_id`, returns every edit event for the client newest-first. No new table.
    - **`buildSheetRecordsForClient(client)`** was extracted out of `renderClientSheets()`'s previously-inline records-parsing `.map()` (exact same logic) so both the live sheet render and the new global browser share one source of truth for "what does this row currently look like."
    - **Reconstruction logic** (`ghRowFieldsAtVersion(taskId, uptoEditedAt)`): for a selected version's timestamp, walks that row's `sheet_edit_log` entries (versions array sorted DESC) for the latest one at-or-before that time; falls back to the row's *current* live value (`ghFieldsFromCurrentRecord`) if the row has no snapshot before that time — inherent limitation, same as #74, not a bug.
    - **Highlight logic** (`ghChangedFieldFor(version)`): diffs a version's snapshot against that same row's immediately-prior version to find the one changed field, reusing the diffing idea from #74's per-row overlay.
    - **Restore** (`restoreSheetHistoryVersion`): calls `applySheetFields(version.task_id, ghState.clientId, version.snapshot, null)` — only ever the one row the selected version actually changed, never any other row visible in the grid.
    - Overlay id `#sheet-history-overlay`, opened via `openSheetHistory(clientId)`. At ship time this lived behind a *second*, separate toolbar button (`🕐 Version History`) next to the old Edit History dropdown from #72 — merged into one button in gotcha #76.

76. **Sheets — merged the Edit History dropdown (#72) and global Version History browser (#75) into one toolbar entry point, fixed highlight visibility, 2026-08-03.** User feedback after trying #75: two history buttons side by side was confusing, and selecting a past version didn't visibly highlight anything.
    - **Merge:** removed the `🕓 Edit History` dropdown button, its menu markup, and the now-dead `renderEditHistoryList()`/`toggleEditHistoryMenu()` functions plus their outside-click-to-close handler entirely. The remaining single toolbar button keeps the familiar `🕓 Edit History` label but now opens `openSheetHistory()` — the full global browser from #75, a strict superset of what the dropdown showed (per-edit rollup *and* full-grid drill-in *and* restore).
    - **Highlight bug — root cause was visibility, not logic.** Traced `ghChangedFieldFor()`/the `gh-highlight`/`gh-highlight-row` CSS classes and confirmed the highlight logic itself was correct and firing — the actual problem was that `renderSheetHistoryMain()` never scrolled to the highlighted row, so on a sheet with many rows the highlighted one was frequently below the fold and looked like nothing was highlighted at all. Fixed two ways: (1) `highlightedRow.scrollIntoView({ block: 'center' })` after every render in `renderSheetHistoryMain()`, and (2) strengthened the CSS itself (`.gh-table td.gh-highlight` now adds an inset `box-shadow` ring in `--acc`; `.gh-table tr.gh-highlight-row td` gets a visible top/bottom border) so it's obvious even without scrolling.
    - **The Content/Idea column mismatch reported in the same session turned out to be a false alarm, not a bug.** After the scroll fix above, the user re-checked and Post 4's Content cell genuinely did show "hello" — the earlier screenshot was almost certainly the row scrolled off-screen combined with stale on-screen data, not a real field swap. No fix was needed; the code path (`saveSheetRow()`/`applySheetFields()`/`writeSnapshotToRowCells()`) was correct all along, confirmed by re-reading it end to end.
    - **Real, separate bug found right after: neither history overlay could highlight the changed cell for a row's first-ever logged edit.** Both `ghChangedFieldFor()` (global browser) and `renderRowHistoryMain()` (per-row overlay) determined "what changed" by diffing a version's snapshot against that same row's immediately-prior version — which is `undefined` for the very first version ever logged for a row (there's nothing older to compare against), so both silently fell back to "nothing changed," even though the edit that created that very version obviously changed something. This is exactly the case a brand-new row/first-ever-edit always hits, so it looked like cell highlighting "just didn't work."
    - **Fix: capture the changed-field list at save time instead of reconstructing it later by diffing.** `applySheetFields()` now builds an `oldRaw` snapshot of the row's 12 fields *before* mutating `taskObj` (parsing `taskObj.description` via the newly-extracted `parseSheetNotes(notes, existingCreationDate)` helper — pulled out of `buildSheetRecordsForClient()` so both places share one parser instead of two copies), diffs it against the new `fields`, and sends the resulting `changedRawFields` array as `changed_fields` in the `log-version` POST body. New nullable `sheet_edit_log.changed_fields` column (`db.py`, standard try/except `ALTER TABLE`, stores a JSON array) carries it through `log_sheet_version()`/`list_sheet_versions()`/`list_client_sheet_versions()` (`routes/ops.py`). Both overlays now read `version.changed_fields` directly when present, and only fall back to the old sibling-diff logic for versions logged before this change shipped (which have `changed_fields = null`). `ghChangedFieldFor()` now returns an array (supports highlighting multiple changed cells in one version, not just one).
    - **Verification:** `node --check` clean on the edited `projects.html` script block, pyflakes clean on `ops.py`/`db.py` (no new warnings vs. baseline), `import app` boots clean. Not exercised against a live edit this session — if a cell still doesn't highlight after this fix, check first whether `sheet_edit_log.changed_fields` actually has the column (server restart needed to run the `ALTER TABLE` migration) and whether the version's `changed_fields` came back non-null from the API before assuming the diff logic itself is wrong again.

77. **Standup "Velocity Tracking" chart (`standup.html`) — added a "This Month" range + an always-visible month-to-date completed/overdue summary, 2026-08-03.** User wanted to track, from the 1st of the month, a running total of tasks completed vs. gone overdue, visible at month-end. The existing chart only offered 7/14/30-day windows and had no summary number — you'd have to eyeball-sum the daily bars, which is also **not a valid way to get a monthly overdue total**: see the caveat below.
    - **Backend (`routes/ops.py`):** `GET /api/standup/velocity` now accepts `range=month` as an alternative to `days=N` (computes `since` as the 1st of the current calendar month) — additive, `days` behavior unchanged. New route `GET /api/standup/velocity-summary?user_id=` returns **month-to-date totals**, always for the current calendar month regardless of the chart's own range selector: `{completed, overdue, month_start}`.
    - **Why the overdue count can't just be summed from the existing daily chart data:** a `standup_tasks` row is re-inserted as a **new row every day** a task stays pending (see the carry-over `INSERT` logic elsewhere in this file) — each with the *same* `carried_from` value, which is set once to the date the task first went unfinished and never changes on subsequent carries. So the existing `/velocity` endpoint's daily `carried` count is a snapshot-per-day, and summing it across a month counts the same overdue task once for every day it remained outstanding. `velocity-summary` avoids this by counting `COUNT(DISTINCT user_id || '|' || title || '|' || carried_from)` instead of raw rows — one count per task lineage, not per day. `completed` doesn't need the same treatment since a task is only ever marked `status='done'` on the one row/day that happened, and done tasks aren't re-inserted by the carry-over logic.
    - **Frontend:** new `#velocity-summary` strip above the chart, populated by `loadVelocitySummary()` (called from `loadVelocity()` on every scope/range change, but itself ignores the range selector — always current month). Respects the existing `My Tasks`/`Team` scope dropdown. `"This Month"` was added as a 4th `<option>` on the existing days `<select>`; `loadVelocity()` sends `range=month` instead of `days=N` when that option is selected.
    - **Scope decision, deliberate:** no month-picker (only ever "current month," no browsing past months) — matches exactly what was asked for (a running month-to-date tally) without building a feature nobody requested yet. If "let me see July's totals" comes up later, `velocity-summary` would need a `month=YYYY-MM` param added.
    - **Verification:** `node --check` clean on `standup.html`'s script block, pyflakes clean on `ops.py` (no new warnings), `import app` boots clean, both new endpoints curl-tested against a local dev server (`{"completed":0,"overdue":0,"month_start":"2026-08-01"}` and `{"velocity":[]}` — empty because the local dev DB has no standup data, not an error). Not exercised against real production standup data or in-browser this session.
    - **Follow-up same day: weekends excluded from the chart.** User pointed out Sat/Sun were showing large "Carried Over" spikes even though nobody works those days — expected, since the auto-carry-over logic (gotcha #8) runs whenever anyone loads `/api/standup/my-tasks`, regardless of day of week, so a weekend's row count reflects whatever was pending, not real weekend activity. Fixed by adding `AND strftime('%w', date) NOT IN ('0','6')` (SQLite: 0=Sunday, 6=Saturday) to both `get_velocity()` queries in `routes/ops.py` — weekend dates no longer appear in the chart at all. Deliberately scoped to the **chart only**: `get_velocity_summary()`'s month-to-date totals were left untouched (still count every day, weekends included) since the user's complaint was specifically about the graph being visually noisy, not about the totals being wrong — if weekend rows also need excluding from the monthly totals, that's a separate follow-up, not assumed here.
    - **Follow-up: card moved to full page width, per-employee filter added, weekday shown under each date.** The card originally lived inside `standup.html`'s 2-column `.layout` grid (sharing half the page with Team Standups), too narrow once weekends started getting skipped and days needed more horizontal room — moved out to its own full-width row directly under `.layout`, chart height bumped 220→260px to match the wider aspect ratio.
      - **Per-employee filter:** the `velocity-scope` dropdown only ever had "My Tasks" / "Team" (aggregate) — no way to see one specific teammate's velocity. `populateVelocityEmployeeOptions()` appends an `<optgroup label="Individual">` with one `<option>` per `window.EMPLOYEES` entry (value = employee id, same id space "My Tasks" already used) once `loadEmployees()` resolves. `velocityUserIdFor(scope)` centralizes the three-way mapping (`"team"` → no filter, `"me"` → `UID`, anything else → that literal employee id) so `loadVelocity()`/`loadVelocitySummary()` don't duplicate the branching — no backend change needed, `/api/standup/velocity(-summary)?user_id=` already accepted any employee id, it just had no UI path to reach it for anyone but yourself.
      - **Weekday under each date:** Chart.js renders an array-valued label as multiple tick lines, so `labels` changed from `d.date.slice(5)` (a string) to `[d.date.slice(5), velocityWeekdayShort(d.date)]` — no Chart.js config changes needed beyond that.
      - **Verification:** `node --check` clean on the edited script block, div-tag balance re-checked after the layout move (80 open / 80 close). Not exercised in-browser this session.

78. **Sheets — "Save failed" on long Content/Idea/Scripts text, root-caused and fixed 2026-08-06.** User reported Sheets edits silently failing to save ("Save failed ✕", no useful detail). Investigated live against production rather than guessing: `GET /api/notion/schema-check` confirmed Notion was reachable/configured/all required properties present (ruled out a config/outage cause); a minimal test `PATCH /api/notion/tasks/<id>` (no-op `creation_date`) against a real task succeeded, ruling out a total endpoint outage; a second test PATCH with a 2319-character `submission_note` reproduced the exact failure (`500 {"error":"Failed to update task in Notion"}`).
    - **Root cause:** Notion's API rejects a page-update request outright if any single `rich_text` object's `content` exceeds 2000 characters — and `_text()` (`notion_store.py`) always wrote the entire composite Sheets Notes string (Content + Idea + Scripts + Caption + Notes joined with `" | "`, see the parsing block in `projects.html`'s `buildSheetRecordsForClient`/`applySheetFields`) as a single text object. Any row with a longer AI-written script (multi-slide carousel scripts routinely run past 2000 chars) blew the limit. Worse: Notion fails the **entire** PATCH on this, not just the offending property — so a request that also carried a status/title/due_date change in the same diff-only payload (`saveSheetRow()`'s normal pattern) lost all of it, not just the long text field, with no indication in the UI of why.
    - **Fix:** `_text()` now chunks the string into multiple `rich_text` array entries (Notion's own documented way to store longer text) instead of one oversized block. `_get_text()` (the reader) already did `"".join(t.get("plain_text","") for t in items)` over the whole array — so this is a fully symmetric fix requiring no read-side change and no data migration; existing short values still produce a single-entry array exactly as before.
    - **Verified live, fully reversible:** re-ran the same 2319-char PATCH against the same real task (`396cacb2-...`, "Post 13") post-deploy → `200 {"success":true}`; confirmed via `/api/notion/dashboard` the full 2319 characters round-tripped correctly (chunked write, joined read); then restored the task's `submission_note` to its exact original value and re-confirmed via the same dashboard read. Net effect on production data: zero.
    - **If "Save failed" recurs:** check the row's combined Content+Idea+Scripts+Caption+Notes length first (2000-char chunks now handle arbitrarily long text, but a single *field* — e.g. one giant Scripts value alone — still can't exceed Notion's separate per-property array-length/page-size ceilings, which are far higher and haven't been hit in practice). If it's not a length issue, the generic `{"error":"Failed to update task in Notion"}` swallows the real Notion error text in `update_task()`'s broad `except Exception: logger.exception(...); return False` — there's no server log access from this session, so narrowing further needs either log access or another live reproduction like the one used here.

79. **Two unrelated Agrius incidents surfaced together, 2026-08-06 — one false alarm (tasks not deleted, just invisible to the app) and one real data-loss bug (fixed).** User reported ~17 Sheets tasks "gone" after an Excel import attempt + clicking Add Row + refreshing. Investigated live rather than guessing, per systematic-debugging — found two completely separate things:
    - **False alarm — 43 pre-existing "Agrius" tasks were never deleted, they were never visible to this app in the first place.** `GET /api/notion/tasks` (unfiltered) turned up 47 tasks with "Agrius" in them; 43 had a **blank `Client ID` property**. `notion_store.list_tasks(client_notion_id=...)` filters strictly on `Client ID` (`{"property": "Client ID", "rich_text": {"equals": ...}}`) — a task whose Client ID was never set (these all predate the app, titled things like "Agrius Sheet", "Agrius Pinned Post", dated back to 2026-06-29, clearly created by hand directly in Notion rather than through this app's `create_task()`, which always sets it) is 100% invisible to every view in this app — Sheets, Task List, Kanban, dashboard — while fully intact and visible in Notion.so. Nothing to fix here per se (not a bug, just an app/Notion linkage gap for hand-created pages) — **flagged as a possible future feature**: a repair tool that back-fills `Client ID` by fuzzy-matching `Customer Name` text to a real client, with a review step before applying (the matches in this dataset included "AGrius" and "Agrius AI" as distinct casings/variants — a human needs to confirm those are/aren't the same client before any bulk assignment). Not built this session — deferred, no request to build it yet.
    - **Real bug, found and fixed — Ctrl+Z paste-undo had zero focus scoping, silently deleting freshly-imported rows on an unrelated keystroke.** The other ~5+ tasks (titled `[Type] Post N`, matching the Sheets auto-numbering convention) were confirmed by the user to be sitting in **Notion's own Trash** (screenshot showed `[Carousel] Post 3`, `[Reel] Post 15/17/18`, `[Static] Post 16`, restorable via Notion's own ↩ icon — not reachable or restorable via this app's API). Root cause: the `keydown` handler's Ctrl+Z branch for `pendingPasteUndo` (added in gotcha #67) had **no check on what element was focused** — any Ctrl+Z press anywhere on the page, for any reason, within the 8-second undo window after a Sheets paste would call `undoPaste()`, which hard-deletes every row that paste had freshly created via the real `DELETE /api/notion/tasks/<id>` endpoint. A user pasting an Excel import, then pressing Ctrl+Z moments later to fix an unrelated typo in a completely different cell (or anywhere else on the page), would silently wipe the import with zero visible connection between "I undid a small typo" and "my whole import vanished" — exactly matching the user's own description ("imported it but it didn't get add[ed]").
      - **Fix:** added `isFocusInPastedRows()` — checks whether `document.activeElement`'s closest `tr.sheet-row` has a `data-task-id` that's actually part of the pending paste (`pendingPasteUndo.cellSnapshots` or `.newTaskIds`). The keydown branch now only fires `undoPaste()` when that's true, preserving the original intent (Ctrl+Z works immediately while still in the just-pasted cell, bypassing native undo history which never learned about the programmatic write) without the global hijack. The visible "Undo" toast button (`showUndoBar`) is unaffected — its `onclick` calls `undoPaste()` directly with no focus check, so it remains the reliable intentional-undo path from anywhere for the full 8s.
      - **Verification:** `node --check` clean on the edited `projects.html` script block. Not exercised against a live paste this session (needs a real paste event to fully verify) — if rows still vanish unexpectedly after a paste, confirm first whether `isFocusInPastedRows()` is actually being reached (i.e. some other delete path — manual 🗑️ row-delete, or `pendingRowDelete`'s own Ctrl+Z branch, which already had a proper `isEditable` guard and was not touched) before assuming this fix regressed.
    - **Also reworked `importClientSocialCSV()` per explicit user request** ("in the future when i click import dont use claude for it... just sort them by [date] and add them below dont delete the previous one"): removed the `/api/social-media/auto-fill` (Claude/Sonnet) step entirely — the Excel/CSV columns already map 1:1 to what `/api/clients/<id>/auto-tasks` expects, so there was nothing for it to clean up, and it was a pure extra failure point (plus cost). Parsed rows are now sorted by `postDay` (falling back to `creationDate`) via plain string comparison before sending — correct for ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` values (which is what the Excel path always produces, converting `Date` cells via `.toISOString()`), **not guaranteed correct for a raw CSV with non-ISO date text** — acceptable limitation, not fixed, since the report was specifically about the Excel path. Confirmed via code review (not just this fix) that `/api/clients/<id>/auto-tasks` (`auto_generate_tasks` in `app.py`) only ever calls `notion_store.create_task()` in a loop — it has no delete/replace logic at all, so "don't delete the previous ones" was already true before this change; the toast now also surfaces the real `tasks_created` count and any backend `error` field instead of a generic success message regardless of outcome. The backend `/api/social-media/auto-fill` route (`app.py::auto_fill_social_media`) is now unreferenced by any frontend file (confirmed by grep) but was deliberately **left in place, not deleted** — it's a documented, real feature (bulk-fills empty caption/script fields via Sonnet) that just wasn't wired into this particular import flow; removing a working endpoint wasn't part of what was asked.

80. **`emp010` reassigned from Anagha to Sid, 2026-08-06.** `config/employees.json` — same `id` kept (`emp010`) so any existing task/standup history tied to that id (assignments, standup rows, etc.) stays linked rather than orphaning. Fields changed: `name: "Sid"`, `role: "1Founder"` (literal string, given as-is by the user — not a typo for "Founder"), `pin: "0000"` (duplicate PIN is fine — `routes/auth.py::login()` matches by `user_id` first, *then* checks that specific employee's PIN, so two employees can share a PIN with zero collision risk; Noorish/emp009 already uses `0000` too). `tone_profile`/`department` reset to match the existing Founder-role entry's style (Kshitij/emp004). Verified live post-deploy: `POST /api/auth/login {"user_id":"emp010","pin":"0000"}` → `200 {"success":true,"user":{"name":"Sid",...}}`. No code changes needed — confirmed via gotcha #63's `_load_emp_names()` fix that nothing hardcodes employee names anymore, and grep for "Anagha" across the repo returned zero hits before making this change.

81. **Team Standups history-date picker silently snapped back to today a few seconds after picking a past date — root-caused and fixed 2026-08-06.** `loadTeamStandups(dateStr = "")` (`standup.html`) treated a missing argument as "show today" and reset `#history-date`'s value accordingly (lines near the top of the function: `if (!dateStr && ...) { history-date.value = today }`). Three call sites invoke it with **zero arguments** — `setInterval(loadTeamStandups, 30000)` (the 30s auto-refresh), plus `updateBlocker()`/`updateTaskTitle()` after a personal-task edit — all of which used to mean "reset to today," clobbering whatever past date the user had manually selected in the picker. Fixed by changing the default so a truly *omitted* argument (`dateStr === undefined`) now reads `#history-date`'s current value instead of hardcoding today; the picker's own `onchange="loadTeamStandups(this.value)"` still passes an explicit string (even `""` if cleared) and is unaffected, so clearing the picker by hand still correctly falls back to today. `node --check` clean; not exercised against a live picker interaction this session.

82. **Sheets — added row-hide (display-only, not a data change), 2026-08-06.** User wanted a way to keep old/previous-month rows in the sheet without them cluttering the top, needing to scroll far to reach the current month — explicitly asked for hide-only (data stays intact, and Calendar/Kanban/Task List must keep showing hidden rows). Implemented as a pure client-side filter local to `renderClientSheets()`, same `localStorage`-per-client pattern as the existing hidden-columns feature (`sheetsHiddenRows_<clientId>`, a JSON array of task ids via new `loadHiddenRows()`/`saveHiddenRows()`). Since Calendar/Kanban/Task List all read `allData` directly and never call this filter, "still show everywhere else" required no extra work — it's a natural consequence of scoping the filter to one function.
    - **Entry points:** right-click a row → new "Hide row" context-menu item (`showRowContextMenu`); or check multiple rows and click "Hide" in the existing bulk-action bar (new `bulkHideChecked()`, sits next to the bulk status-select/Delete/Clear buttons added in gotcha #62).
    - **Un-hiding:** a new "Hidden (N)" toolbar button (next to Edit History) opens a dropdown listing every hidden row by title with a per-row "Unhide" link and an "Unhide all" bulk action — mirrors the existing Columns dropdown's structure exactly.
    - **Emoji follow-up, same day:** first cut used a 🙈 emoji on the new buttons/menu/toast, inconsistent with this app's plain-text convention elsewhere in `projects.html`'s Sheets toolbar — user said "dont use emojis" immediately after seeing it described; fixed by stripping the emoji from all four new strings, reverted to plain text labels ("Hide", "Hidden (N)", "Hide row"). Scoped to just what was just added, not a sweep of the rest of the app's pre-existing emoji-heavy buttons (📥 Import, ➕ Add Row, ☰ Columns, etc. all remain as-is) — those weren't part of this change and removing them wasn't asked for.
    - **Verification:** `node --check` clean; whole-file `<div>`/`</div>` regex count showed a 2-tag gap both before and after this edit (193/195 → 198/200, same delta) — confirmed pre-existing noise from `<div` substrings inside unrelated JS string literals elsewhere in this large file, not a real markup imbalance introduced here (the new block's own tags were hand-verified balanced: 5 opens, 5 closes). Not exercised against a live client's Sheets tab this session.
    - **Superseded same week — see gotcha #83:** the row-hide state described above was per-browser `localStorage`, which turned out wrong the moment a second employee opened the same client (see #83).

83. **Sheets — row-hide moved from per-browser `localStorage` to a shared server table, and a Month filter added, 2026-08-10.** User reported hiding a row as Palak was still visible to Vidit — exactly the localStorage-per-browser limitation flagged (but not caught) when gotcha #82 shipped: `sheetsHiddenRows_<clientId>` lived in each employee's own browser, so "hidden" was never actually shared. Same session, user also asked for a way to view the Sheet one month at a time instead of scrolling past old rows — a second, complementary fix to the same underlying "too many old rows visible" complaint from #82.
    - **Shared hide/unhide:** new SQLite table `sheet_hidden_rows` (`task_id` PK, `client_id`, `hidden_by`, `hidden_at`) in `db.py`, works for both Notion- and SQLite-backed tasks the same way `sheet_edit_log` (gotcha #74) does — `task_id` is whatever id the row already uses (`notion_id` or SQLite id), no FK, presence in the table = hidden. Four new routes in `routes/ops.py`: `GET /api/sheets/hidden-rows` (every client's hidden rows in one shot — powers a page-load cache, not a per-client fetch, since expanding a client card must never block on a network round-trip), `POST /api/sheets/tasks/<id>/hide`, `POST /api/sheets/tasks/<id>/unhide`, `POST /api/sheets/clients/<id>/unhide-all`.
    - **Frontend:** `projects.html`'s `load()` now calls `loadAllHiddenRowsFromServer()` once, populating `hiddenRowsCache`/`hiddenRowsMeta` (clientId → Set/meta-map). `loadHiddenRows(clientId)` reads the cache synchronously (unchanged call sites, `renderClientSheets()` doesn't need to become async) while `hideSheetRow()`/`unhideSheetRow()`/`unhideAllSheetRows()`/`bulkHideChecked()` optimistically update the cache + re-render, then fire the real POST in the background (toast on sync failure, matching the existing "Save failed"-style pattern elsewhere in this file). The "Hidden (N)" dropdown now also shows a `title` tooltip with who hid the row and when, using `hiddenRowsMeta`.
    - **Month filter:** new per-client `<select>` next to the search box, options built from every distinct `due_date` (Post Day) month present in that client's rows (`allMonths`, computed before any filter so removed/hidden rows don't shrink the dropdown). Selection stored in `localStorage` (`sheetsMonthFilter_<clientId>`) via `loadMonthFilter()`/`saveMonthFilter()` — deliberately kept **per-browser**, unlike row-hide, since it's a personal view preference (like column visibility, gotcha #62) rather than shared data; there's no correctness bug in it being local the way there was for hiding.
    - **Verification:** `node --check` clean on the extracted script block, pyflakes clean on `ops.py`/`db.py` (matches known pre-existing warning baseline), `import app` boots clean. Post-deploy: `curl /api/sheets/hidden-rows` returns 200. Not exercised against a live two-browser hide/unhide this session — if a hidden row still doesn't sync across users, check first whether `loadAllHiddenRowsFromServer()` actually resolved before the client card was expanded (network race), not the backend logic.

84. **Native `<input type="date">` pickers forced to dd/mm/yyyy app-wide, 2026-08-10.** User screenshot showed the Sheets Creation-Date/Post-Day columns displaying inconsistent formats (one field showing the raw `dd-mm-yyyy` placeholder, an adjacent one showing an actual filled `10-08-2026`). Root cause: a native `<input type="date">`'s displayed format (not its stored value, which is always ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` regardless) is rendered by the browser according to the OS/browser locale — uncontrollable via CSS, and inconsistent machine-to-machine. Fixed by adding `lang="en-GB"` to every `<input type="date">` in the app (Chrome/Edge read an element's own `lang` attribute, or its nearest ancestor's, to pick the display format for date/number inputs) — forces dd/mm/yyyy everywhere without touching how any date value is read, written, or stored. Touched: `projects.html` (Sheets Creation Date/Post Day, quick-task/edit-task due date, new-deadline picker), `add-tasks.html` (deadline, Sheets postDay/creationDate columns, task-panel due date), `standup.html` (post-date picker, team-standups history-date picker), `client-admin.html`, `client-onboard.html`. Two JS `querySelector('input[type="date"]')` selector strings in `projects.html` got accidentally mangled by a blanket find-replace (became `input[type="date" lang="en-GB"]`, which happened to still match since the actual elements now carry that attribute too, but was fragile/wrong) — caught and reverted to the plain `type="date"` selector before committing. `node --check` clean on all 5 edited files; not exercised in a live browser this session — if a date picker still shows the wrong format, check whether that specific input actually got the `lang="en-GB"` attribute (a new date input added later without following this pattern would silently fall back to browser locale again).

85. **Sheets paste rewritten to always be single-cell, 2026-08-10 — fixes both "paste from anywhere should only fill that cell" and a knock-on "version-history Restore doesn't work after a paste" report.** The paste handler built across gotchas #62/#67/#68 treated ANY clipboard text containing a tab **or a newline** as an Excel-style multi-cell paste (`isMultiCell = /\t|\n/.test(text)`) and split it with `parseTsvClipboard()` across columns/rows, creating new rows via `createBlankSheetRow()` if the paste had more lines than rows existed below the cursor. Pasting a paragraph copied from a website, Word, or a chat app — no tabs, just line breaks — got smeared one line per row down the sheet instead of landing in the one cell being edited. Presented with two options (keep Excel-style multi-cell paste but gate it on a real tab character, vs. make every paste single-cell-only and point bulk entry at the existing Import CSV/Excel button), user chose **always single-cell** via `AskUserQuestion`.
    - **Why this also explains the separately-reported "Restore doesn't work"**: a multi-line external paste used to scatter its content across several different rows/tasks (sometimes newly-created ones), each independently saved and logged into `sheet_edit_log`. Opening *one* row's Version History and clicking Restore only ever touched that one row's own fragment of the original paste — never "undid the paste" as a whole, which looked like restore was broken. With paste now confined to a single cell, every paste is one clean, single-row edit with one clean version entry, so Restore behaves normally again.
    - **Fix, `projects.html`:** the `paste` listener now only intercepts `contentEditable` Sheets cells (date/select cells get native paste behavior untouched), writes the pasted text into the one targeted cell via the existing `applyPastedCellValue()`, and calls `saveSheetRow()` for that row same as a normal typed edit — no row creation, no column/row splitting. Also normalizes `\r\n`/`\r` → `\n` in the pasted text before writing it (text copied from outside the app almost always carries Windows line endings) — `\r` sitting invisibly in a cell's text node would otherwise survive the round trip to the backend and back, making a later diff (Restore comparing current value against an older snapshot) see a mismatch that isn't visually there. `pendingPasteUndo`/`undoPaste()`/`isFocusInPastedCell()` (renamed from `isFocusInPastedRows()`) simplified to single-cell shape — no more `cellSnapshots`/`newTaskIds` arrays, no more DELETE-the-newly-created-rows logic on Ctrl+Z, since paste can no longer create rows. Removed now-fully-dead `createBlankSheetRow()` and `parseTsvClipboard()`.
    - **Scope note:** the multi-cell **copy**-out handler (selecting a block of cells and Ctrl+C to paste into real Excel, gotcha #68's TSV-quoting) is untouched — this only changes how paste*ing into* the Sheets grid behaves. Bulk multi-row data entry still goes through the dedicated Import CSV/Excel button (`importClientSocialCSV()`, gotcha #79), which was never part of this paste-into-a-cell code path.
    - **Verification:** `node --check` clean; grepped for every removed function/variable name (`parseTsvClipboard`, `createBlankSheetRow`, `isFocusInPastedRows`, `cellSnapshots`, `newTaskIds`) to confirm zero dangling references. Not exercised against a live paste event or a live Restore click this session — if Restore still misbehaves after this fix, check first whether the row's `sheet_edit_log` snapshot itself predates this change (still carries scattered/partial data from the old multi-row paste bug) before assuming the new code is wrong.
    - **Immediate follow-up, same day — see gotcha #86:** user then reported clicking "Restore this version" in the global Version History browser did nothing at all. Root cause was unrelated to the paste behavior above: a CSS z-index bug meant `toast()` (both success and failure messages) was rendering completely hidden behind the full-screen history overlay, so restore may well have been working correctly the whole time with zero visible confirmation either way.

86. **Toast notifications invisible behind the Sheets Version History overlays — fixed 2026-08-10.** User clicked "Restore this version" in the global Version History browser (gotcha #75) and reported nothing happened. Investigated via code reading (not a live click, no browser access this session): `#toast` (`projects.html` CSS, ~line 129) was `z-index:200`, while `#sheet-history-overlay`/`#row-history-overlay` (gotchas #74/#75) are `z-index:10000`, full-viewport, with an **opaque** `background: var(--s1)` — not just higher in the stack but visually covering the toast entirely. `restoreSheetHistoryVersion()`/`restoreRowHistoryVersion()` both call `toast(...)` on every outcome (`'Nothing to restore -- row already matches this version, or the save failed.'` on a no-op/failure, `'Version restored'` on success) — for the **failure/no-op branch specifically**, the function `return`s immediately without closing the overlay, so that toast had zero chance of ever being seen; the success branch closes the overlay right after showing the toast, so it likely *was* visible for genuine successful restores, making the bug intermittent-looking depending on which case a user hit. Fixed by bumping `#toast` to `z-index:20000` — above both history overlays, a one-line CSS change with no logic touched. **Open question, not resolved:** whether the specific restore the user clicked was itself a no-op (the selected version's data already matched the row's current live state, so "Nothing to restore" was the technically-correct-but-invisible response) or a genuine save failure — indistinguishable from code alone. If a user reports a restore still not visibly applying after this z-index fix, that would point to a real second bug in `applySheetFields()`'s diff/save logic rather than a visibility issue, and is worth checking next.

87. **Google Sheets two-way sync built, 2026-08-11 — live-verified end to end on a real client (MMGA) as of 2026-08-13.** User asked whether editing a real Google Sheet could stay in sync with a client's Sheets tab in both directions. Full design at `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-11-google-sheets-two-way-sync-design.md`, implementation plan at `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-11-google-sheets-two-way-sync.md`.
    - **New:** `backend/google_sheets_store.py` (raw Sheets API v4 client via a service-account `AuthorizedSession` from `google-auth` — added as a dependency, not the heavier `google-api-python-client`, consistent with `notion_store.py`'s raw-`requests` convention for external APIs; field-mapping helpers `_parse_sheet_notes`/`_build_sheet_notes` are a **Python port** of `parseSheetNotes()`/the `detailParts` join in `applySheetFields()` — must be kept in sync by hand if either side's composite-Notes format ever changes; task create/update/delete adapters branch on a per-link `is_notion` flag to support both Notion- and SQLite-backed clients; `push_task_to_sheet()`/`reconcile_sheet_rows()` are the two sync directions), `backend/routes/sheets_sync.py` (link management + push endpoint + pull webhook, registered in `app.py`), table `google_sheet_links` (`client_id, spreadsheet_id, link_token, is_notion, client_name, linked_at, linked_by` — `link_token` is the pull webhook's sole authenticator, unguessable, scoped to one client).
    - **Push (Lumina→Sheet):** `applySheetFields()` in `projects.html` now fires a fire-and-forget `POST /api/sheets/push/<task_id>` after every save, same contract as the pre-existing `log-version` call right above it — no-ops server-side for any client with no linked Sheet, so this is safe on every client, not just linked ones.
    - **Pull (Sheet→Lumina):** an Apps Script `onChange` trigger (installed once per Sheet during setup, snippet auto-generated by `POST /api/clients/<id>/google-sheet-link` and shown again by the `GET`) POSTs the Sheet's **entire current contents** to the webhook on every edit/insert/delete. The backend reconciles via a **full-snapshot diff** against that client's current tasks (fetched once per request, not once per row) rather than incremental per-cell events — deliberately, to avoid this codebase's recurring diff-only-payload bug class (gotchas #45/#51/#69). Blank `lumina_task_id` → create; known id missing from the payload entirely → delete; known id with changed fields → update; known id with identical fields → skip.
    - **Loop safety, by construction, not by a lock:** `reconcile_sheet_rows()` only ever writes back to the Sheet for the brand-new-row case (`write_cell` with the freshly created task id) — every other branch writes to Notion/SQLite only, never back to the Sheet. A push-triggered Sheet write does re-fire the Sheet's own `onChange` and lands back at the webhook, but by then the Sheet and Lumina already agree, so that pass's diff for that row is empty and nothing gets written anywhere — no unbounded ping-pong regardless of how precise the equality check is.
    - **Setup, one-time per client:** employee creates a Google Sheet with a 13-column layout (hidden `lumina_task_id` in column A, then the 12 Sheets fields matching `RH_FIELD_LABELS`'s order), shares it with the service account email shown in the "🔗 Google Sheet" modal (new button next to "🕓 Edit History" in the Sheets toolbar), pastes the Sheet's URL into that modal to connect (the backend does a live `read_all_rows` check at connect time so a forgot-to-share mistake surfaces immediately as a connect-time error instead of a silent later push/pull failure), then pastes the returned Apps Script snippet into the Sheet's Apps Script editor and runs `installTrigger()` once.
    - **`assigned_to` is plain-text comma-separated employee display names in the Sheet** (e.g. `Abhinav Gupta, Palak`) — matching exactly what's already stored in Notion's `Assigned To` property, no id-mapping layer like the checkbox-based Lumina UI has. **`Creation Date`/`Post Day` columns must be formatted as Plain Text in the Sheet**, not Date — the Sheets API returns `FORMATTED_VALUE` by default, but a real Date-type cell renders per the *viewer's* locale, not a fixed ISO string, so a typed `YYYY-MM-DD` would round-trip inconsistently. This is a manual setup instruction shown in the modal, not something enforceable in code.
    - **Pre-existing bug discovered (not introduced, not fixed) while writing the Python port of `parseSheetNotes()`:** the frontend's own `parseSheetNotes()` in `projects.html` has stop-lists for the `Content`/`Idea`/`Scripts`/`Caption` regex captures that don't include `Notes` as a boundary (only `Link`'s stop-list does) — so if a row has, say, a filled Content field and a filled Notes field but an *empty* Link field, `Content` greedily captures through to end-of-string and ends up with `"...actual content | Notes: whatever the note was"` appended into it, even though the separate `Notes:` regex still correctly extracts `myNotes` on its own. Confirmed via a Python round-trip test of the new `_parse_sheet_notes`/`_build_sheet_notes` port (which faithfully reproduces this, since it must match the frontend's actual behavior for round-trips to work). Out of scope for this feature — flagged here rather than silently fixed, since it's a pre-existing frontend bug unrelated to what was asked. If a user ever reports a Sheets Content cell containing trailing `"| Notes: ..."` junk, this is the root cause; fixing it means adding `Notes` to the `Content`/`Idea`/`Scripts`/`Caption` stop-lists in **both** `parseSheetNotes()` (`projects.html`) and `_parse_sheet_notes()` (`google_sheets_store.py`) together, or they'll drift.
    - **Verification performed this session:** `pyflakes` clean on both new files and on `app.py`/`db.py` (zero new warnings vs. baseline — the pre-existing `app.py` unused-import noise is untouched by this change). `import app` boots clean after registering the new blueprint. `node --check` clean on all 4 of `projects.html`'s extracted `<script>` blocks. Live `curl` against a running dev server confirmed `POST /api/sheets/push/<id>` no-ops correctly (`{"success":true,"linked":false}`) for a client with no link, and `POST /api/sheets/webhook/<bad-token>` correctly 404s (`{"error":"Unknown link"}`) for an unrecognized token. A Python round-trip test (`_build_sheet_notes` → `_parse_sheet_notes` → `_task_to_fields` → `_fields_to_row` → `_row_to_fields`) confirmed the field-mapping layer is internally consistent.
    - **Superseded 2026-08-13 — see the "First real credential + first real client" follow-up below.** The credential was set, MMGA was connected as the first real client, and the full round trip (Lumina→Sheet and Sheet→Lumina) was walked through and confirmed working, surfacing 3 more real bugs in the process. This line is kept only for history; do not treat this feature as unverified anymore.
    - **Follow-up, same day, after deploying and testing against production (no credential yet, so error-path-only) — found and fixed two real gaps, plus two polish items:**
      1. **`GET`/`DELETE /api/clients/<id>/google-sheet-link` had zero auth**, discovered by testing the deployed routes directly: combined with this app's `CORS(origins="*")`, `GET` leaked the live `link_token` (the pull webhook's sole authenticator) to anyone who knew a `client_id` — and `client_id`s aren't secret, they're readable from other already-unauthenticated endpoints like `/api/notion/dashboard`. Exact same bug class as the pre-existing `/api/auth/clients` gap in security warning #7.
