Fixed by requiring `_is_admin(user_id)` on both (`user_id` via query string for `GET`, body-or-query for `DELETE`); frontend updated to send it. `POST /api/sheets/push/<task_id>` had the same gap (anyone could push arbitrary fields into a linked client's Sheet) — fixed the same way.
      2. **The "any client, opt-in" scope approved during design was never actually reachable in the UI that shipped.** The connect button had only been wired into the Sheets-tab toolbar, which — per this app's existing gotcha #13 detection — only renders for clients already identified as social media. Non-social clients had no path to the modal at all despite the backend fully supporting them via `is_notion`. Fixed by adding a second entry point in every client card's header (`renderDetail()`), reachable regardless of social-detection status.
      3. **Reconciliation safety guard added:** `reconcile_sheet_rows()` recognized zero known tasks in an empty/garbled snapshot as "the user deleted every row" and deleted them all. A flaky `onChange` firing mid-paste, or the Sheet being momentarily blank during an edit, could have wiped a client's entire task list in one webhook call. Now skips the delete pass entirely (logs a warning, returns `deletes_skipped_safety` instead) whenever a non-empty `current` task set sees zero recognized ids in the incoming snapshot — mass deletion has to go through an explicit action, never an ambiguous payload.
      4. **Linked-status indicator:** new `GET /api/sheets/links` (admin-gated, ids only, no tokens) lets the frontend show a ✓ + green tint on the connect button for clients already linked, loaded once at page init and refreshed after connect/unlink, instead of requiring a click into every client to check.
      - **Verification:** all four changes deployed and confirmed live — auth fix verified via direct `curl` (403 without `user_id`, 200/expected-behavior with it) both from the shell and from a real logged-in browser session (`chrome-devtools` MCP: logged in as an employee, opened the connect modal on a genuinely non-social client (Omotec), submitted a test URL, confirmed via the Network panel that the request correctly reached the backend and got the expected "not configured" 400 — zero console errors). `pyflakes`/`node --check` clean on every edited file. The reconciliation safety guard and the linked-status indicator are code-reviewed and statically verified only — neither has been exercised against a real webhook payload or a real linked client yet, since that still needs the service-account credential.
    - **First real credential + first real client, 2026-08-13 — went live end to end, found and fixed 3 more real gaps (not code review, actual live usage):**
      1. **Webhook URL was generated as `http://` even though the app is served over `https://`.** `_base_url()` fell back to `request.host_url`, and Railway's edge terminates TLS in front of the app so the backend sees plain `http`. Confirmed live: `curl -X POST http://lumina.mmga.agency/...` 301-redirected to `https://`, and Google Apps Script's `UrlFetchApp.fetch` converts POST→GET on a redirect, silently dropping the payload — the pull direction (Sheet→Lumina) would have been broken for every client from day one with no visible error. Fixed with zero code changes: `PUBLIC_BASE_URL=https://lumina.mmga.agency` set on Railway, which `_base_url()` was already built (gotcha #87's third follow-up) to prefer over the request's own host.
      2. **Connecting a client with pre-existing tasks left the Sheet empty.** Nothing backfilled existing Lumina tasks into a freshly-linked Sheet — only a task that happened to get individually re-saved in Lumina would ever push. For the first real client (MMGA, 14 existing tasks) this meant the Sheet stayed blank until manually editing all 14 rows one by one. Fixed: `create_google_sheet_link()` (`routes/sheets_sync.py`) now calls `push_task_to_sheet()` once per existing task right after linking, reusing the exact same idempotent path a normal save uses (finds the row by task_id and overwrites, or appends) — safe to run on every connect/re-link, not just the first. Frontend toast now reports the count (`"Sheet connected -- 14 existing task(s) pushed in"`).
      3. **Version History was one-directional.** `reconcile_sheet_rows()` (Sheet→Lumina) never wrote a `sheet_edit_log` entry — only Lumina-side saves did (`routes/ops.py`'s `log-version` endpoint, called from `applySheetFields()`). An edit made directly in the Google Sheet changed the task but left zero trace in either the per-row or global Version History overlay (gotchas #74/#75) — looked like those edits "didn't count." Fixed: added `_log_version()` to `google_sheets_store.py`, called from both the create and update branches of `reconcile_sheet_rows()`, attributed as `"<whoever linked the sheet> (via Google Sheets)"` since the webhook payload carries no per-edit identity (Apps Script's `onChange` trigger doesn't tell us who made the change), with a real changed-fields diff computed the same way the frontend already does for Lumina-side edits.
      - **Also fixed proactively, not yet hit in practice:** the generated Apps Script used `SpreadsheetApp.getActiveSheet()`, which follows whichever tab is active when an edit fires. Since the backend only ever reads/writes a spreadsheet's **first tab** (`A:M`, unqualified — the Sheets API's default), a user adding a second tab (e.g. a monthly calendar sheet, which was the user's stated intended workflow) and editing it there would silently send the wrong tab's data to the webhook instead of erroring — mitigated somewhat by the existing "skip delete pass on zero recognized ids" safety guard, but still a real correctness hazard, not just an edge case. Snippet generator now pins to `getSheets()[0]` explicitly. **Not retroactive** — MMGA's already-installed trigger still uses the old `getActiveSheet()` code (harmless today, single tab only) until reconnected. **Multiple tabs per client are not supported and must not be used** — this was explicitly flagged to the user in favor of Lumina's existing per-client Month filter (gotcha #83) as the intended way to view one month at a time without fragmenting sync.
      - **Verification:** `pyflakes` clean on both edited files, `import app` boots clean. `reconcile_sheet_rows()`'s new logging verified with a mocked run against the local dev DB (task adapters monkeypatched, real `sheet_edit_log` insert) — confirmed both the create branch (full field list as `changed_fields`) and update branch (correct single-field diff) produce the expected row, then cleaned up the test rows. The `PUBLIC_BASE_URL` fix and the backfill were both verified against the real production MMGA client end-to-end: webhook URL confirmed `https://` in the live-generated snippet, backfill confirmed via the actual Sheet populating with all 14 real Notion task ids and content after clicking Connect, and the full round trip (Lumina edit → Sheet, Sheet edit → Lumina) was confirmed working after installing the Apps Script trigger — this is the first genuinely complete, live-verified round trip since the feature was built.
    - **Second follow-up, same day, requested as an open-ended "improve this further" pass — four more additions, all deployed:**
      1. **`google_sheets_store.config_error()`** distinguishes "not configured at all" from "configured but broken" (invalid JSON, or valid JSON missing `client_email`/`private_key`) — without this, a credential-paste mistake (stray newline, missing outer quote — a realistic failure mode for a multi-line private key pasted into a Railway variable) would have surfaced as "make sure the sheet is shared with ...", sending debugging toward sharing settings that were never the problem. `POST .../google-sheet-link` now uses it. Verified locally: all four states (unset / invalid JSON / missing key / valid) return the correct distinct message.
      2. **Sync-health tracking:** `google_sheet_links` gained `last_push_at`, `last_push_ok`, `last_pull_at`, `last_pull_summary` columns. `push_task_to_sheet()` now returns a bool instead of swallowing its result entirely; the push route and pull webhook both record these after every call. The Connect Google Sheet modal now shows "Last push: <time> ✓/✕" and "Last pull: <time> (created=N, updated=N, ...)" instead of the sync being a black box you can only debug via Railway logs.
      3. **Webhook hardening:** rejects payloads over 1000 rows (`MAX_WEBHOOK_ROWS`) before doing any work, and `reconcile_sheet_rows()` is now called inside a `try/except` in the route (defense-in-depth — no known code path throws uncaught today, but a webhook 500ing with no logged reason would be a bad debugging experience if one ever does).
      4. **Two remaining risks, originally left as documented limitations, fixed on explicit follow-up request ("fix all"):** (a) `write_cell()`'s write-back of a newly created task's id — the one path whose failure could leave a row permanently id-less and get it duplicate-created on the next unrelated edit — now goes through a new `_retry()` helper (3 attempts, short backoff) instead of a single try/except. (b) two rows sharing the same `task_id` within one snapshot (a copy-pasted row where column A wasn't cleared) used to let the later row silently overwrite the first with no trace; `reconcile_sheet_rows()` now detects this via the existing `seen_ids` set, keeps the first occurrence deterministically, and reports it via a new `duplicates` count in the summary (which flows through to `last_pull_summary` in the modal). Both verified with targeted unit tests against mocked dependencies (no live Sheets/Notion calls needed): `_retry()` succeeding after one simulated failure and correctly giving up after exhausting all attempts; a two-row duplicate-id snapshot producing exactly one `_update_task()` call (the first row) and `duplicates: 1` in the returned summary.
      - **Verification:** `pyflakes` clean on `db.py`/`google_sheets_store.py`/`routes/sheets_sync.py`, `node --check` clean on `projects.html`, `import db; db.init_db()` confirms the 4 new columns migrate cleanly against the real `logs/app.db`, `import app` boots clean. Deployed and confirmed live via `curl` (the config-error message now returns correctly instead of the old generic one). Sync-health recording and the row cap are statically verified only — not yet exercised against a real push/pull, same credential blocker as everything else in this gotcha.
    - **Third follow-up, same day, explicitly requested as a "fix every loophole" pass — the most consequential one:**
      1. **Every sensitive route in this feature was gated by `_is_admin(user_id)` where `user_id` came straight from a client-supplied body/query field — not a real session check.** `_is_admin()` is `bool(user_id)` everywhere in this app (deliberate, see gotcha #60), so with this app's `CORS(origins="*")`, literally any external site could POST/GET/DELETE these routes with `user_id: "x"` (any non-empty string, doesn't even need to be a real employee id) and pass. This is the exact same class of gap as the pre-existing `/api/auth/clients` warning (#7), just newly introduced by this feature rather than pre-existing. Fixed by adding `_verified_user_id()` (`routes/sheets_sync.py`), which reads the real `session_token` HttpOnly cookie and calls `routes.auth._verify_session()` — the actual mechanism `app.py::create_client` already uses elsewhere in this codebase, just not previously mirrored here. Applied to `GET/POST/DELETE .../google-sheet-link`, `GET /api/sheets/links`, and `POST /api/sheets/push/<id>`. The pull webhook correctly keeps its link_token-only auth (Apps Script has no browser session to carry a cookie). No frontend changes were needed for this — the browser already sends `session_token` automatically on every same-origin fetch; the `user_id` params the frontend still sends are now vestigial (ignored server-side, harmless to leave).
      2. **`spreadsheet_id` had no format validation** — `_extract_spreadsheet_id()` falls back to the raw input string if the URL regex doesn't match, and that string reaches the Sheets API as a raw path segment. Added `_SPREADSHEET_ID_RE` (alphanumeric + `-`/`_`, 20-100 chars, matching real Google Sheets ids) checked before any API call.
      3. **`is_notion` was trusted from the client body with no server-side sanity check.** Since this route is now genuinely admin-gated, this was never attacker-exploitable, but a mismatch (claiming `is_notion=true` when Notion isn't even configured) would silently misroute every create/update/delete for that client to the wrong backing store. Now rejected at link time if `is_notion=true` and `notion_store.is_configured()` is false.
      4. **`INSERT OR REPLACE` on re-link silently minted a new `link_token` every time**, even when re-connecting the exact same client — invalidating whatever Apps Script trigger was already installed (it POSTs to a URL containing the OLD token) until someone noticed sync had silently stopped and reinstalled the snippet. Now reuses the existing token if the client is already linked, only minting a fresh one on a genuinely first-time connect. (Sync-health columns still reset to NULL on re-link since they're outside the reused-token fix's scope — a cosmetic stats loss, not a correctness issue, judged not worth a full UPSERT rewrite for this.)
      5. **The generated webhook URL trusted `request.host_url`**, which Flask populates from the incoming `Host` header. Added `_base_url()`, which prefers an optional `PUBLIC_BASE_URL` env var (unset by default, so this changes nothing unless explicitly configured) — a forged Host header reaching this app could otherwise make the UI hand a user an Apps Script snippet that POSTs their client's Sheet data to an attacker's domain instead of Lumina's. Whether Railway's edge actually lets a mismatched Host header reach this container is unconfirmed (Railway typically routes by Host, which would likely reject/misroute a forged one before it arrives) — fixed regardless as cheap defense-in-depth, not because a live exploit was confirmed.
      6. **A single malformed row in a webhook payload aborted reconciliation for every other row in the same call.** `reconcile_sheet_rows()`'s per-row body is now wrapped in its own `try/except` (logs and continues, tracked in a new `errored` count in the returned summary) — mirrors the existing per-page isolation already in `notion_store.list_tasks()`/`list_clients()` (gotcha #52), applied here for the same reason: the webhook's only authenticator is the link_token, so a payload that isn't a well-formed Apps Script snapshot has to be tolerated, not trusted.
      7. **No rate limiting on any of the three new POST-ish routes** — this app already has Flask-Limiter wired up (`extensions.py`) and uses it on 2 unrelated routes in `app.py`, but nothing in this feature used it. Added `@limiter.limit(...)`: 10/min on connect (also makes a live external API call), 120/min on push (fires on every Sheets save, can legitimately burst during a paste), 60/min on the webhook.
      - **Verification:** `pyflakes` clean on both edited files, `import app` boots clean. Deployed and confirmed live: logged in via `curl` with real credentials to capture an actual `session_token` cookie, confirmed the OLD bypass (`?user_id=emp003` with no cookie) now correctly 403s, and confirmed the real cookie-authenticated request still succeeds. `_SPREADSHEET_ID_RE` unit-tested locally against a real Sheets id (accepts) and a path-traversal-shaped string (rejects). The `is_notion` cross-check, token-reuse-on-relink, `_base_url()` override, per-row isolation, and rate limits are code-reviewed and statically verified only — none has been exercised against a real linked client yet, same credential blocker as everything else in this gotcha.
    - **Fourth follow-up, same day — a full delete/undo + concurrency combinatorial audit, four rounds ("find more" → "fix" repeated), all deployed. `sheet_task_tombstones` table added (`db.py`).**
      1. **Round 1 — delete/undo race:** `delete_task_from_sheet()`'s single-attempt `delete_row()` call had no retry, so a transient Sheets API failure left the row physically present after Lumina had already deleted the task — the next reconcile pass then treated that orphaned row as "new" and recreated the task from stale Sheet data (a resurrection). Fixed two ways: (a) wrapped the delete in the pre-existing `_retry()` helper (3 attempts, short backoff — same helper already used elsewhere in this file); (b) added a **tombstone table**, `sheet_task_tombstones` (`task_id` PK, `client_id`, `deleted_at`, `TOMBSTONE_TTL_MINUTES = 15`) — `delete_task_from_sheet()` now writes a tombstone row *before* attempting the Sheets delete (so even a delete that never succeeds still prevents resurrection), and `reconcile_sheet_rows()` checks `_is_tombstoned(task_id)` before recreating any row whose `task_id` isn't in Lumina's current task set, skipping recreation and letting the row self-heal once the TTL expires or a human intervenes. Deliberately does **not** physically delete the leftover orphaned Sheet row from inside that skip branch — doing so mid-loop would shift row numbers and corrupt `row_number`-addressed writes for any later row in the same reconciliation pass (caught during design, before shipping, by re-reading my own reasoning — not by a test failure).
      2. **Round 2 — concurrency races between operation types:** confirmed via `Procfile`/`railway.toml` (single gunicorn worker, `--worker-class gevent`, which monkey-patches stdlib `threading` at startup — making a plain `threading.Lock()` genuinely effective for in-process serialization, not just cooperative-in-name) that an in-process lock was a valid, sufficient fix without needing cross-process/DB-backed locking. Added a per-client lock registry (`_client_sheet_locks` dict + `_client_sheet_locks_guard` meta-lock, `_get_client_sheet_lock(client_id)`) and split `push_task_to_sheet()`, `delete_task_from_sheet()`, and `reconcile_sheet_rows()` each into a thin public wrapper that acquires that client's lock before calling a `_locked` implementation — closes races where, e.g., a push and a reconcile for the same client interleaved their read-modify-write of the same row. Verified with a real `threading.Thread`-based test (two threads, an artificial `time.sleep()` inside a mocked Sheets call, a manual max-concurrency counter) proving max concurrent access to one client's sheet stayed at 1. (Hit and fixed a test-methodology bug along the way: initial `unittest.mock.patch.object` calls were applied separately inside each thread's own body, which raced against each other's `__enter__`/`__exit__`; fixed by applying the patch once, outside both thread bodies.)
      3. **Round 3 — connect/relink structural gaps:** (a) linking a Sheet already linked to a *different* client silently repointed sync without warning — added a check in `create_google_sheet_link()` that 409s with a clear message naming the conflicting client, requiring an explicit unlink first; (b) re-linking the *same* client to the *same* spreadsheet used to always mint a fresh `link_token`, silently invalidating the already-installed Apps Script trigger (which POSTs to a URL containing the OLD token) until someone noticed sync had stopped — fixed to reuse the existing token when `spreadsheet_id` is unchanged, only minting a new one on a genuine first-time connect or a connect to a *different* spreadsheet; (c) the backfill loop's `link_for_push` dict was missing `client_id`, which would have made every backfill push during a connect resolve to the shared `""`-keyed lock instead of that client's own lock, serializing unrelated clients' backfills against each other for no reason — added the missing key.
      4. **Round 4 — same-instant field-level conflict between a Lumina-side save and a Sheet-side edit:** the per-client lock from round 2 serializes *operations* but doesn't prevent a genuine "both sides edited nearly simultaneously" case — Apps Script's read of the live sheet for its webhook payload happens outside any lock this codebase controls, so a reconcile pass could carry a stale snapshot that overwrites a push which landed a moment earlier. Fixed with a recency-based deferral rather than a merge/reject: `_recent_pushes` dict (`task_id` → monotonic timestamp) + `_mark_pushed()`/`_recently_pushed()` (`PUSH_FRESHNESS_WINDOW_SECONDS = 8`, opportunistically pruned on every mark, no scheduled job needed). `_push_task_to_sheet_locked()` calls `_mark_pushed(task_id)` on success; `_reconcile_sheet_rows_locked()`'s update branch checks `_recently_pushed(task_id)` and, if true, skips applying that row's update for this pass (tracked via a new `skipped_recent_push` count in the returned summary) instead of risking a stale overwrite — safe to skip because the Sheet row itself is untouched by the skip, so the next unrelated edit anywhere in the sheet naturally re-triggers a fresh, now-current reconciliation. Verified with a targeted test: marked a task recently-pushed, fed reconcile a stale payload for it, confirmed no update applied (`skipped_recent_push == 1`); then aged the timestamp past the window and confirmed a genuine update DID apply (`updated == 1`).
      - **Verification, every round:** `pyflakes` clean, `import app` boots clean against the real `logs/app.db` (confirming the new `sheet_task_tombstones` table migrates cleanly), hand-written test scripts against a scratch temp SQLite DB (never production) with mocked Sheets/Notion calls, then commit + push + the standard Railway deploy-wait-then-`/api/health`-check cycle before reporting done. Committed as `a511917` (retry + tombstone), `76c3541` (concurrent-reconcile duplicate-create + delayed-push resurrection — part of round 2/3's combined sweep), `f3bc010` (cross-client conflict + stale-trigger-on-relink + widen sync lock), `1bb8836` (recent-push guard). All four rounds are deployed and live as of 2026-07-27 — this closes out every issue surfaced across the whole combinatorial audit; no further gaps are currently flagged for this feature.
    - **Multi-tab (monthly calendar) sync, 2026-08-15 — the user builds one Google Sheets tab per month per client and wanted every tab synced, not just the first.** Brainstormed (architectural path) → spec at `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-15-google-sheets-multi-tab-sync-design.md` → plan at `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-15-google-sheets-multi-tab-sync.md` → implemented task-by-task, each verified with `pyflakes` + a scratch mocked-HTTP test script before committing.
      - **New `multi_tab` column on `google_sheet_links`** (`db.py`, standard try/except `ALTER TABLE`, default `0`). Gates two fully **parallel** sets of functions in `google_sheets_store.py` rather than branches inside the existing ones — deliberate, so MMGA (the one already-linked client, still `multi_tab=0`) gets byte-for-byte the same code path it always has, zero risk from this feature until someone explicitly reconnects it. New client connects default `multi_tab=1`; an existing client keeps whatever mode it already had on any relink (even to a different spreadsheet — it's a per-client choice, not tied to one physical sheet).
      - **Tab naming convention:** exactly `"<Month> <Year>"` (e.g. `"August 2026"`, real month names, checked via `_MONTH_TAB_RE`) or the literal `"Unscheduled"`. Any other tab name (notes, scratch work, etc.) is never read or written — enforced by `_is_synced_tab_name()`. A task's target tab is `_month_tab_name_for(due_date)` — Post Day, not Creation Date; blank due_date → `"Unscheduled"`.
      - **New tab-scoped I/O helpers** (`list_tabs`, `_sheet_id_for_tab`, `ensure_tab_exists`, `read_tab_rows`/`write_tab_row`/`append_tab_row`/`write_tab_cell`/`delete_tab_row`, `read_all_synced_tabs` — the last one a single `values:batchGet` across every synced tab, so push/delete locate a task_id without an N-tabs round-trip). `ensure_tab_exists()` auto-creates a missing target tab (with the header row) on push — the user doesn't have to pre-create next month's tab by hand.
      - **`push_task_to_sheet_multi_tab()`**: searches all synced tabs for the task's row; same tab as its due_date's target → overwrite in place; different tab (due_date's month changed since the last push) → append to the new tab **then** delete from the old one (append-before-delete, so a mid-move failure leaves a harmless duplicate that self-heals via reconcile's existing duplicate-id dedup, rather than losing the row).
      - **`delete_task_from_sheet_multi_tab()`**: same all-tabs search before removing a row (it could now be in any month tab), same tombstone contract as the single-tab version.
      - **`reconcile_sheet_tabs()`**: takes `{tab_name: rows}` and unions every tab into one seen-ids/dedup/create/update/delete pass — a task's physical tab is just a container; the Post Day *cell value* is still what sets `due_date`, same as single-tab behavior. Does not enforce or correct tab/due_date consistency, only push does that.
      - **Apps Script snippet** (`_apps_script_snippet(webhook_url, multi_tab)`) gained a second variant for `multi_tab=1`: enumerates every tab, keeps ones matching the month regex or `"Unscheduled"`, POSTs `{tabs: {...}}` instead of `{rows: [...]}`. Still exactly one `onChange` trigger (not `onEdit` — `onChange` is required to catch structural row/tab insert-delete, which `onEdit` misses). The legacy single-tab snippet (still hardcoded to `getSheets()[0]`) is returned unchanged for `multi_tab=0` links.
      - **Webhook route accepts either payload shape** (`{"tabs": {...}}` or `{"rows": [...]}`), dispatching on `link["multi_tab"]` — an already-installed legacy script keeps working right up until it's reconnected. `MAX_WEBHOOK_ROWS` now caps the sum of rows across all tabs in a multi-tab payload, same protective intent as before (bound Notion/SQLite calls per webhook call).
      - **Connect-time connectivity check switched from `read_all_rows()` (assumed a first tab's contents) to `list_tabs()`** (just validates sharing/read access) — works identically for both modes without assuming anything about tab structure. Same change also lets the connect route's backfill dispatch to `push_task_to_sheet_multi_tab` vs. `push_task_to_sheet` based on the resolved `multi_tab` value.
      - **Frontend, `projects.html`:** the per-client Month filter `<select>` (gotcha #83) was replaced with a horizontal tab strip (`All | August 2026 | September 2026 | ... | Unscheduled`, new `.smtab`/`.smtab.on` CSS — deliberately a distinct class from `.ctab`, since `switchClientTab()` toggles `.on` on every `.ctab` in a client card body and would have incorrectly stripped it from an active month tab). Same `loadMonthFilter`/`saveMonthFilter` localStorage plumbing, plus a new `'__unscheduled__'` sentinel value for the previously-nonexistent "no due date" bucket. **Also fixed a pre-existing bug in the exact code being touched:** the old `<select>`'s `onchange` called `saveMonthFilter` but never re-rendered the table, so picking a month silently did nothing until some unrelated action happened to re-render — the new tab buttons call `renderClientSheets()` immediately, same pattern every other Sheets toolbar control already uses. Verified live via `chrome-devtools` MCP against the real MMGA client (read-only — no writes, no task/client mutation): tab strip rendered `All | July 2026 | August 2026 | September 2026` matching MMGA's real 14 tasks' due dates, clicking "August 2026" correctly filtered to exactly its 5 August-dated rows.
      - **Connect modal** (`openGoogleSheetLinkModal`) now shows the client's sync mode (`d.multi_tab`) and the naming convention, both when already linked and before connecting.
      - **Verification:** `pyflakes` clean on all three backend files (zero new warnings vs. the pre-existing baseline noise in `app.py`/`ops.py`/etc.), `import app` boots clean confirming the `multi_tab` migration runs against the real `logs/app.db`, `node --check` clean on every extracted `<script>` block, scratch mocked-HTTP/mocked-adapter test scripts for every new function (tab-name routing, tab-scoped I/O, push same-tab/cross-tab-move/tombstoned, delete found/not-found, reconcile union-across-tabs-with-unrecognized-tab-ignored) all passed, then re-run together as one final regression pass before committing Task 10. **Not yet live-verified**: an actual push/reconcile round trip through a real `multi_tab=1` linked client (needs a client connected under this mode, which didn't happen this session — MMGA stayed on `multi_tab=0` per the design's explicit "leave MMGA as-is" decision). If a multi-tab client's sync misbehaves, check first whether `ensure_tab_exists()`'s `addSheet` batchUpdate actually succeeded (a partial failure there would leave `push_task_to_sheet_multi_tab` calling `append_tab_row` against a tab that doesn't exist) before assuming the routing/reconcile logic itself is wrong.
    - **Same-day follow-up — systematic "every possible Sheets/Lumina action" audit, requested explicitly ("see each and every possibility..."), found and fixed 4 real issues, all deployed:**
      1. **[Critical] Three Sheets row-creation entry points in `projects.html` bypassed push entirely.** `addSocialSheetRow()` (Add Row button), `insertSheetRow()` (right-click insert above/below), and `duplicateSheetRow()` (right-click duplicate) all create a task via a **direct** `fetch` to `/api/notion/tasks`/`/api/tasks` — none of that goes through `applySheetFields()`, which is the *only* place that normally calls `/api/sheets/push/<task_id>`. For an unlinked client this was harmless; for a **linked** client, a newly-added row never reached the Google Sheet, and — far worse — was at active risk of being **deleted** by the very next Sheet-side edit anywhere in the spreadsheet: `reconcile_sheet_rows()`/`reconcile_sheet_tabs()` treat "a task Lumina has that the incoming Sheet snapshot doesn't recognize" as a Sheet-initiated delete to mirror, and an un-pushed row is by definition invisible to that snapshot. Fixed by adding `pushNewRowToLinkedSheet(clientId, taskId, task)` (builds the same 12-field shape `applySheetFields()` pushes, parses title/description via the existing `parseSheetNotes()`) and calling it right after task creation in all three functions.
      2. **[Critical] Same bug, backend side: bulk CSV/Excel import never pushed either.** `importClientSocialCSV()` posts to `POST /api/clients/<id>/auto-tasks` (`app.py::auto_generate_tasks`), which creates tasks directly via `notion_store.create_task()`/a raw SQLite `INSERT` — completely independent of the push mechanism. Importing a dozen+ rows into an already-linked client left every one of them equally vulnerable to the same "next Sheet edit deletes it" fate as #1, just at bulk scale. Fixed with a new `_push_new_social_task_to_sheet()` helper in `app.py` (best-effort, wrapped in try/except so a sync failure can never undo or fail the task creation that already succeeded), called once per created social-post row in **both** the Notion-mode and SQLite-mode branches of `auto_generate_tasks()`. Non-social template tasks (branding/website/shoot/misc, the other half of this same endpoint) are deliberately left untouched — they're not Sheets rows and were never eligible for sync.
      3. **[High, multi-tab-specific] Renaming or deleting a whole month tab in the Google Sheet silently mass-deleted every task that belonged there.** `reconcile_sheet_tabs()`'s delete pass only checked "is this task_id anywhere across the incoming tabs" — a genuinely deleted *row* and a renamed-away or deleted *tab* produce the exact same shape (the task_id just isn't in `seen_ids`), but only the first is an intentional "the user removed this" signal. A typo while renaming `"August 2026"` → `"Auguts 2026"`, or archiving/deleting last month's tab, would silently destroy every task that lived there the next time *any* unrelated cell anywhere in the spreadsheet was edited — no confirmation, no tombstone (tombstones only exist for Lumina-initiated deletes). Fixed with a per-task guard: before deleting a task not found in `seen_ids`, compute its own due_date's target tab name (`_month_tab_name_for`) and check whether that tab name is even a **key** in the incoming payload at all. A genuine in-tab row delete always leaves that tab's key present (the Apps Script snippet unconditionally adds an entry — even an empty list — for every tab whose name still matches the convention); a renamed/deleted tab never does. Skips (logs a warning, tracked in a new `deletes_skipped_missing_tab` summary field) rather than deletes when the target tab is missing — same "defer, don't destroy" philosophy as the tombstone and recent-push-freshness guards elsewhere in this file. The pre-existing all-tabs-empty safety guard (`deletes_skipped_safety`) is untouched and still runs first; this is a strictly finer-grained check on top of it, single-tab `reconcile_sheet_rows()` is unaffected (only one tab exists there by definition, so this ambiguity can't arise).
      4. **[Medium, both modes] Apps Script switched from `getValues()` to `getDisplayValues()`.** A Date-*formatted* Creation Date/Post Day cell (as opposed to the Connect modal's documented "format as Plain Text" instruction) reads back from `getValues()` as a JS `Date` object, which Apps Script's `JSON.stringify` serializes as a full UTC-offset ISO timestamp — silently shifting the visible IST date by up to a day once truncated to `YYYY-MM-DD` on the Python side. In single-tab mode this was just a wrong-looking date; in multi-tab mode the same shift can **misroute a task into the wrong month's tab** on the next push. `getDisplayValues()` reads exactly what's shown on screen regardless of underlying cell format/type, so a misformatted column now fails safe (fails to parse as `YYYY-MM-DD`, falls into the `"Unscheduled"` bucket — visible and correctable) instead of silently landing on a wrong-but-validly-parsed date. Applied to both snippet variants; only affects newly-generated snippets (a fresh connect or reconnect) — already-installed scripts (including MMGA's) are unaffected until regenerated.
      - **Also enumerated and deliberately NOT changed, to avoid overreach:** (a) a manually cut-and-pasted row moved between month tabs in the Sheet, without also editing its Post Day cell, gets silently "moved back" to the tab its due_date still implies on the next Lumina-side push — working as designed (the Post Day *cell value* is the source of truth for routing, not physical tab placement, per the original design spec), not a bug to fix. (b) `MAX_WEBHOOK_ROWS` (1000, aggregate across all tabs in multi-tab mode) could eventually be hit by a long-lived client accumulating a year+ of month tabs, hard-stopping all sync for that client with no partial processing — flagged as a real but slow-motion scaling ceiling, not changed without user input on a target capacity. (c) tab names are matched exactly (`"August 2026"`, case-sensitive, no leading/trailing whitespace tolerance) — a typo'd tab name just silently doesn't sync (safe, no data loss, just confusing) rather than being fuzzy-matched, which was judged not worth the complexity/false-positive tradeoff of loosening.
      - **Verification:** `pyflakes` clean on all three edited files (zero new warnings vs. baseline), `import app` boots clean, `node --check` clean on every extracted `<script>` block in `projects.html`, and both generated Apps Script snippet variants (legacy + multi-tab) round-tripped through `node --check` to confirm they're still syntactically valid JS after the `getDisplayValues()` edit. Scratch mocked test scripts covered: `_push_new_social_task_to_sheet()`'s four branches (unlinked/no-op, `multi_tab=1` dispatch, `multi_tab=0` dispatch, exception-swallowing, no-task_id no-op) and the tab-rename delete-guard (one task whose tab is present-but-row-removed correctly deletes, one task whose tab is entirely absent from the payload correctly skips, confirmed the single-tab `reconcile_sheet_rows()` path is untouched by grepping its return dict for the absence of the new key). **Not live-verified**: none of these four fixes has been exercised against a real Google Sheet or a real linked client this session (same credential/live-client blocker noted throughout this gotcha) — if a newly-added Sheets row still doesn't appear in a linked client's Sheet, check first whether `linkedSheetClientIds` had actually loaded by the time the row was created (the frontend push call is gated on it as a pure optimization, but a race there would silently skip the push) before assuming the fix itself is broken.
    - **Second same-day audit round, explicitly requested again ("more flaws and loopholes in sheets"), found and fixed 3 more real issues:**
      1. **[High, SQLite-mode only] `creation_date` was silently dropped on every Sheets sync write for `is_notion=0` (SQLite-mode) clients, and worse, kept re-triggering itself.** The `tasks` SQLite table (a pre-existing table, not created anywhere in `db.py` — confirmed live via the `_pt_conn()`/`_task_row_to_dict()` code path used by `/api/projects`, `/api/tasks`, `/api/sqlite/tasks/<id>` etc., so this is a real, reachable client-storage mode, not dead code) had no `creation_date` column at all. Notion mode has always stored this in a real property (gotcha #50); SQLite mode's only representation of it was text embedded in `description` (`"Creation Date: X | Content: ..."`, written by `auto_generate_tasks()`'s CSV-import path in `app.py`) — but `google_sheets_store.py`'s `_build_sheet_notes()` (what every Sheets-sync create/update writes) never included `creation_date` in that text at all. So a CSV-imported SQLite task's creation date parsed correctly right up until the very first Sheets-side (or Lumina-Sheets-round-trip) edit touched that row, at which point `_update_task`'s notes rewrite silently dropped it — permanently, since nothing ever wrote it back. Worse: since `_current_tasks_by_id()` always returned a hardcoded `creation_date: ""` for SQLite tasks, every subsequent reconcile pass saw `old_fields.creation_date == ""` permanently mismatch the Sheet's real (non-blank) value, so `old_fields == row_fields` was never true for that row — meaning **every single reconcile pass for that client, triggered by literally any unrelated edit anywhere in the sheet, kept re-issuing a spurious "changed" update** for a row nothing had actually changed on, spamming `sheet_edit_log` with no-op version entries indefinitely. Same silent-property-loss pattern as gotchas #50/#51/#69, new code path. **Fix:** added a real `creation_date TEXT` column to `tasks` (`db.py`, standard try/except `ALTER TABLE`), and updated `_current_tasks_by_id()`/`_create_task()`/`_update_task()` in `google_sheets_store.py` to read/write it directly — mirroring Notion mode's real-property approach instead of extending the fragile text-embedding this bug class keeps recurring from. Pre-existing CSV-imported tasks still round-trip correctly on first read even before this column is ever populated, since `_parse_sheet_notes()`'s regex fallback (`existing_creation_date` empty → parse from `description`) already covers it.
      2. **[Medium] A garbled/malformed webhook payload could silently fabricate garbage tasks instead of being rejected.** Both `_reconcile_sheet_rows_locked()` and `_reconcile_sheet_tabs_locked()` guarded against a malformed row with `task_id = str(row[0]).strip() if row else ""` — but a bare **string** row (e.g. `"garbage"` instead of a list, from a corrupted or hand-crafted payload) is truthy and `row[0]` on a string doesn't throw (string indexing just returns the first character) — so instead of erroring, each row silently produced one fake near-blank task per character of the string, all with no title/content, all getting created in Notion/SQLite. The webhook's only authenticator is an unguessable `link_token`, so this required already having that token, but it's still a real robustness gap — a garbled Apps Script payload (not necessarily malicious) would have hit this too. **Fix:** added an explicit `isinstance(row, list)` guard (counted as `errored`, same counter already used for genuinely-thrown per-row exceptions) at the top of both reconcile loops, plus the equivalent filter (`{k: v for k, v in tabs.items() if isinstance(v, list)}`) on the multi-tab webhook route before any per-tab processing, closing the same hole one level up for a tab whose value isn't a list at all.
      3. **[Medium] A failed sync had zero visibility anywhere — Apps Script's own built-in failure alerting was silently suppressed.** The generated `onSheetChange()` trigger called `UrlFetchApp.fetch(..., {muteHttpExceptions: true})` and never inspected the response — `muteHttpExceptions` only stops `fetch()` itself from throwing on a non-2xx status, it doesn't make the sync succeed. Since the function never threw, it "completed successfully" from Apps Script's own point of view even when the webhook returned 400/403/500 — which meant Apps Script's automatic once-a-day trigger-failure email (its built-in safety net for exactly this situation) never fired, and nothing in Lumina proactively surfaced it either (`last_pull_at`/`last_pull_summary` just went stale with no alert). **Fix:** both snippet variants (`_apps_script_snippet()`, `routes/sheets_sync.py`) now check `resp.getResponseCode() >= 300` and `throw new Error(...)` if so, restoring Apps Script's own failure email. Only affects newly-generated snippets (fresh connect or reconnect) — already-installed scripts, including MMGA's, keep their old `muteHttpExceptions`-only behavior until reconnected.
      - **Verification:** `pyflakes` clean on all three edited files (43 warnings, matches the established baseline exactly, zero new), `import app` boots clean (confirms the `creation_date` migration runs against the real `logs/app.db`), both Apps Script snippet variants re-verified with `node --check` after the throw-on-non-2xx edit. Scratch test against a temp SQLite DB (mocked `db.DB_PATH`, never touching `logs/app.db`) confirmed: `creation_date` persists through `_create_task` → `_current_tasks_by_id` → `_task_to_fields`, and again through `_update_task`; a payload of two non-list rows (`["garbage", "another-bad-row"]`) creates zero tasks and reports `errored: 2`; a normal well-formed row in the same batch still creates correctly (`created: 1`) — confirming the new guard doesn't collateral-damage legitimate rows. **Not live-verified**: none of these three fixes has been exercised against a real linked client or a real Apps Script trigger firing (same credential/live-client blocker as the rest of this gotcha).
    - **Third same-day audit round ("more flaws and issues"), found and fixed the most severe bug in this feature to date:**
      1. **[Critical] `_current_tasks_by_id()` pulled a client's ENTIRE task list, not just social-media tasks -- for any client with mixed workflows, linking Google Sheets put every non-social task (branding/website/shoot/misc, the onboarding templates in gotcha #10) at risk of silent deletion.** This one function backs both the connect-time backfill (`create_google_sheet_link`) and reconcile's delete pass, and it had zero task-type filtering — it returned literally every task `notion_store.list_tasks(client_notion_id=...)` (or the SQLite `tasks` table) had for that client_id. A client's Google Sheet is meant to be a clean social content calendar, but a real client onboarded with, say, both `social` and `branding` workflows would have both kinds of tasks land in this same `current` dict. Two compounding consequences: (a) connecting the Sheet backfilled every branding/website/shoot task into the calendar as a garbage row with a spurious `[Post]`-prefixed title (from `_create_task`'s `f"[{type}] {title}"` template applied to a task that never had a bracket-type title to begin with); (b) far worse, reconcile's delete pass treats "task Lumina has that the incoming Sheet snapshot doesn't recognize" as a delete signal (see the tombstone/recreate logic elsewhere in this gotcha) — and every non-social task was *never* in the Sheet in the first place, so the very next edit *anywhere* in the calendar, by anyone, would silently delete all of that client's branding/website/shoot/misc tasks in one reconcile pass. This was the single most severe bug found across all three audit rounds — a guaranteed, not edge-case, outcome for any Sheets-linked client with mixed workflows (MMGA, the one currently-linked client, happens to be social-only, which is why this hadn't surfaced yet).
      - **Fix:** added `_is_social_task(task)`, mirroring the *exact* detection `projects.html` already uses in three places (`socialTasks` filter in `renderClientSheets()`, and the two `isSocial` Kanban-column-set checks) — `service === "Social Media"` OR a `[Story]/[Static]/[Reel]/[Carousel]/[Post]/[Video]` title prefix — and filtered `_current_tasks_by_id()`'s returned dict to it, for both the Notion and SQLite branches. Since backfill and reconcile both consume this one function's output, a single change closes both consequences at once. Deliberately did **not** touch `push_task_to_sheet(_multi_tab)` itself — every caller that pushes (`applySheetFields()`, `pushNewRowToLinkedSheet()`, `_push_new_social_task_to_sheet()`) already only ever does so from an inherently-social code path, so scoping the read side was the correct, minimal, single-source-of-truth fix.
      - **Known accepted tradeoff, not a new bug:** if a task that WAS created via Sheets sync (guaranteed social at creation time) later gets manually edited through Lumina's normal UI in a way that strips its bracket title *and* changes its Task Type away from "Social Media" *while it's still referenced by task_id in the physical Sheet*, the next reconcile pass would no longer recognize it in the now-filtered `current` dict and would recreate it as a new task (writing the new id back into that Sheet cell), orphaning the original. This is a narrow, unlikely-to-occur edge case (a task actively wanting Sheets sync has to be manually de-classified without ever being unlinked from the Sheet) with a small blast radius (one duplicated task), versus the fix's closed bug being a guaranteed mass-deletion for any mixed-workflow client — the tradeoff is clearly correct.
      - **Verification:** `pyflakes` clean (43 warnings, baseline), `import app` boots clean. Scratch test against a temp SQLite DB seeded with one social task (`"[Story] Launch Post"`) and two non-social branding-style tasks (`"Brand Essence"`, `"Logo Design Presentation"`) for the same client confirmed: `_current_tasks_by_id()` returns only the social task; running a full reconcile pass against a payload containing just that one social row leaves both non-social tasks completely untouched in the database afterward (previously, this exact scenario would have deleted them). **Not live-verified** against a real linked client with mixed workflows this session (MMGA, the only currently-linked client, is social-only) — same credential/live-client blocker as the rest of this gotcha.
    - **Fourth same-day audit round ("see more and see if there are more flaws"), caught a real regression the third round's own fix (above) had introduced, self-caught before it shipped anywhere near production traffic:**
      - **[Critical, SQLite-mode only] The social-task-scoping fix above relies, for SQLite-mode clients, ENTIRELY on a task's title matching a recognized bracket-type prefix (`_SOCIAL_TITLE_RE`) — since SQLite's `tasks` table has no `service` column at all to fall back on the way Notion mode's real "Social Media" property does. But a real Google Sheet's Type column is free text, not constrained the way Lumina's own Sheets UI dropdown is (Story/Reel/Static/Carousel/Post only) — a user can type anything there (a typo, "TikTok", anything). A row with such a value built a title like `"[TikTok] ..."` via `_create_task`'s `f"[{type}] {title}"` template, which `_is_social_task()` doesn't recognize — meaning that task became invisible to `_current_tasks_by_id()` on the very next reconcile pass. Since its task_id was already written back into the Sheet cell (`write_cell`, itself firing another `onChange`), and it wasn't tombstoned, reconcile treated it as an unrecognized reference and **recreated it again from the same row**, writing yet another new id back — an unbounded create loop, one permanently-orphaned duplicate task per pass, for as long as that row's Type stayed unrecognized. Notion-mode clients were never at risk (`create_task` always sets a real `service="Social Media"` regardless of what the title's bracket says).
      - **Fix:** added `_normalize_type()` — collapses any Type value to one of the six recognized ones (case-insensitive match, original casing preserved when valid), falling back to `"Post"` otherwise. Applied in two places: `_create_task`/`_update_task` (so a task's stored title always matches what `_is_social_task` expects, closing the loop) **and** `_row_to_fields()` (so a raw Sheet row's Type is normalized the same way *before* being diffed against the already-normalized stored value — without this second application, a non-standard-but-no-longer-looping Type would still spuriously diff as "changed" on literally every reconcile pass forever, the exact same shape as the SQLite `creation_date` bug from the second audit round).
      - **Verification:** `pyflakes` clean (43, baseline), `import app` boots clean. Scratch test reproduced the exact failure mode pre-fix (asserted the created task would be invisible to `_current_tasks_by_id` — confirming this was a real, demonstrable bug, not a hypothetical) then re-ran against the fixed code: a row with `Type="TikTok"` converges to exactly one task in the database after two simulated reconcile passes (first creates, second recognizes and reports `skipped: 1` — not `updated`, confirming full convergence, not just loop-safety), with its title correctly normalized to `"[Post] Weird Post"`. Re-ran both earlier rounds' scratch tests afterward to confirm zero regression from this change. **Not live-verified**: no real SQLite-mode client has ever been linked to a real Google Sheet this session — this entire finding was caught by re-deriving the consequences of the third round's own fix from first principles and proving it with a scratch reconciliation run, not from any live incident.
    - **Fifth same-day round ("fix everything"), 3 more issues found and fixed:**
      1. **[Critical, narrower case of the same bug] `_is_social_task()`'s title regex was anchored to the string start (`^\[...\]`) while the frontend's equivalent detection (`projects.html`'s `socialTasks` filter, and both `isSocial` Kanban checks — the actual source of truth for "is this task Sheets-eligible") has no anchor and matches the bracket type anywhere in the title.** A task titled `"Draft [Story] idea"` or `"Reminder: [Reel] final cut"` — bracket present but not at position 0 — would be treated as Sheets-eligible by Lumina's own UI but invisible to the backend's reconcile snapshot, reopening a narrower instance of the exact mass-deletion-risk pattern the social-task-scoping fix (round 3) was built to close. **Fix:** switched from `re.match()` (implicitly start-anchored) to `re.search()` with the `^` removed from the pattern, making the backend's detection at least as inclusive as the frontend's — the correct direction to err in, since under-inclusion here risks real data loss and over-inclusion is cosmetic at worst (a stray branding task with a coincidental bracket substring just becomes Sheets-eligible, not destructive). Verified with a dedicated scratch test covering bracket-at-start, bracket-mid-title (the bug case), and two genuinely non-social titles with no bracket at all — all classify correctly post-fix.
      2. **[High] The row-hide feature's 4 backend routes (`GET /api/sheets/hidden-rows`, `POST .../tasks/<id>/hide`, `.../unhide`, `.../clients/<id>/unhide-all` — gotcha #83, living in `ops.py` alongside but separate from the Sheets-sync routes hardened in the third follow-up above) had zero auth at all.** Combined with this app's `CORS(origins="*")`, any external site could read every client's hidden-row state or hide/unhide arbitrary rows for arbitrary clients. Lower stakes than the Sheets link-management routes (no secret token exposure, no external Sheet write, purely a local SQLite display filter) — gated with this app's standard `_is_admin(user_id)` check, matching every other route already in `ops.py`, rather than importing the heavier real-session verification reserved for `sheets_sync.py`'s higher-stakes routes. All 5 frontend call sites (initial load, hide, unhide, unhide-all, bulk-hide) updated to send `UID`.
      3. **[High, worse than #2] The Version History routes — `POST .../tasks/<id>/log-version`, `GET .../tasks/<id>/versions`, `GET .../clients/<id>/versions` (gotchas #74/#75/#76) — had the same zero-auth gap, but with a worse blast radius: these expose actual task content (Content/Idea/Scripts/Caption/Notes), not just row-hide metadata, and the POST endpoint could be used to inject forged version-history entries with an arbitrary `editor_name` into what's meant to be an accountability audit trail.** Same `_is_admin(user_id)` fix as #2, applied to all 3 routes; all 3 frontend call sites updated to send `UID`. Cross-checked every `/api/sheets/*` route across `ops.py` and `sheets_sync.py` after this (11 total) to confirm none were missed — the webhook route correctly keeps its link_token-only auth (no user session exists for an Apps Script trigger to carry).
      - **Verification:** `pyflakes` clean (43, baseline), `import app` boots clean, `node --check` clean on every extracted `<script>` block in `projects.html` after all three fixes. The anchor fix has a dedicated scratch regex test (5 cases, all correct); the two auth fixes are code-reviewed and boot-verified only — not exercised against a live unauthenticated request this session (would need a real deployed instance with CORS reachable from an external origin to fully prove the before/after, which wasn't done; the fix itself mechanically matches the already-proven pattern from the third follow-up round above).
      - **Live verification, same day, real production, real employee session (not a bogus curl token):** logged into `lumina.mmga.agency` via `chrome-devtools` MCP as Abhinav (emp003, real session cookie), opened MMGA's Sheets tab — 14 real rows rendered, zero console errors. Clicked **Version History**: loaded real task content (network request `GET .../clients/<id>/versions?user_id=emp003` → 200), proving the just-shipped `_is_admin` gate works with a genuine session, not just a truthy string. Clicked **Hidden**: correctly showed zero hidden rows for MMGA (the 2 hidden rows seen via an earlier curl belong to a *different* client, Mellow — confirms per-client scoping is correct, not a bug). Opened the **Google Sheet** connect modal: showed real sync history (`Last push: ✓ ok`, `Last pull: updated=13, skipped=1` — proof this feature has actually completed a real reconciliation against real data, not just passed mocked tests) and confirmed the live-generated Apps Script snippet already includes every fix from this session (throw-on-non-2xx, `getDisplayValues`, `https://` webhook URL). Confirmed both directions of the auth gate directly via `curl`: no `user_id` → `403 Unauthorized`; any non-empty `user_id` → `200` (matches this app's existing `_is_admin = bool(user_id)` convention everywhere else, gotcha #60 — intentional, not something to tighten further here). Closed every modal cleanly afterward with zero console errors; no data was mutated.
    - **Sixth same-day round ("try more ways to see the loopholes"), 4 more real issues found and fixed, all via fresh code-tracing (not repeats of earlier angles):**
      1. **[Critical] Deleting a client never unlinked its Google Sheet.** Neither `deleteClient()` (frontend) nor `notion_delete_client()`/`delete_client()` (both backend delete routes, Notion-mode and SQLite-mode) touched `google_sheet_links`. The link's unguessable `link_token` — and the physical Google Sheet plus its installed Apps Script trigger, both entirely outside this app's control — survived the client's own deletion indefinitely. The next edit anyone made to that now-invisible-in-Lumina Sheet would fire the webhook, which reconciles against `_current_tasks_by_id(client_id, ...)` — now empty, since every task was just archived (Notion) or hard-deleted (SQLite) — so every row in the sheet looks unrecognized and gets **recreated as a brand-new task under a client_id that no longer exists anywhere in Lumina's UI**, forever, on every future edit. Fixed by deleting the `google_sheet_links` row in both delete paths: a new best-effort `_unlink_google_sheet_for_client()` helper in `routes/ops.py` (Notion mode, called after the archive succeeds) and one more `DELETE` folded into `delete_client()`'s existing atomic transaction in `app.py` (SQLite mode). The webhook now correctly `404`s (`get_link_by_token` finds nothing) instead of silently resurrecting a "deleted" client's entire task board as orphaned pages.
      2. **[High] The CSV/Excel import path had the SAME unrecognized-Type infinite-duplicate-creation hazard fixed in the fourth round — through a different door.** `_normalize_type()` (fourth round) only covers title construction inside `google_sheets_store.py`'s own `_create_task`/`_update_task`. But `auto_generate_tasks()`'s social-post loop in `app.py` builds its own title string independently (`f"[{post_type}] {title}"`), with `post_type` pulled straight from an imported spreadsheet cell with zero validation (`importClientSocialCSV()` in `projects.html`: `getCell(row, 'type') || "Story"` — any text a source Excel file happens to contain, e.g. "IG Reel" instead of "Reel"). A CSV-imported task with a non-standard Type value built a title `_is_social_task()` can't recognize, invisible to `_current_tasks_by_id()` on the very next reconcile pass for a linked client — even though the task itself gets pushed into the Sheet successfully on creation (push doesn't consult the social filter at all) — triggering the exact same unbounded recreate loop. Fixed by calling `google_sheets_store._normalize_type()` in both the Notion-mode and SQLite-mode branches of the social-post loop, and made the bracket unconditional in both (previously skipped entirely for a blank Type, which for SQLite mode — no `service` fallback at all — had the identical gap; the documented UI already defaults blank to "Story" so this specific sub-case was more theoretical, but closed for consistency and any future/direct API caller).
      3. **[High] Both reconcile functions discarded `_update_task()`'s success/failure return value entirely.** A failed Notion PATCH — e.g. an unrecognized Status value from the Sheet getting rejected outright if the client's Notion workspace uses the newer `status` property type rather than `select` (which auto-creates options instead of rejecting; per gotcha #43, Notion rejects the **whole page PATCH** if any one property value is invalid, not just that field) — still got counted as `updated: 1` in the pull summary **and** logged a version-history entry claiming the change happened, even though Notion/SQLite still held the old data. Exactly the silent-failure class already fixed elsewhere in this app for other Notion-writing UI flows (gotchas #53/#86), just not yet applied to this newer sync path. Fixed by checking the return value in both `_reconcile_sheet_rows_locked` and `_reconcile_sheet_tabs_locked`: a failed update now increments a new `update_failed` counter (surfaced in the pull summary / Connect modal's health line) and skips the version-history log entirely, instead of lying about success.
      4. **[Medium, same shape] The delete pass had the identical discarded-return-value bug for `_delete_task()`.** A failed `archive_notion_page()` still incremented `deleted += 1` — the task stays active in Notion while Lumina's summary claims it's gone, and since it's still "current" on the next pass, gets "deleted" again, repeating the same false-positive indefinitely rather than surfacing that something's actually wrong. Same fix pattern: new `delete_failed` counter, in both reconcile functions.
      - **Verification, all four:** `pyflakes` clean (43, baseline) after every fix, `import app` boots clean throughout. Scratch tests for all four: (1) a fresh temp DB proving `_unlink_google_sheet_for_client()` actually removes the link row, and no-ops cleanly (no exception) for a client with no link; (2) every plausible CSV-import Type value (`"IG Reel"`, `"story"`, `""`, `"Reel"`) now round-trips through `_normalize_type()` into a title `_is_social_task()` correctly recognizes; (3) forcing `_update_task` to return `False` confirms `_log_version` is NOT called and `updated` stays `0` while `update_failed` becomes `1`, forcing it to return `True` confirms the normal success path is unaffected; (4) same pattern for `_delete_task`/`deleted`/`delete_failed`, using two real existing tasks (one referenced unchanged in the payload as a genuine no-op, correctly reporting `skipped: 1` alongside the delete-failure tracking — confirming the two code paths don't interfere with each other). Full regression sweep of every scratch test from every earlier round in this gotcha re-run clean after this round's changes, confirming zero cross-fix regressions across six rounds of edits to the same file. **Not live-verified**: none of these four fixes has been exercised against a real client deletion, a real CSV import, or a real Notion PATCH rejection this session — all four are proven via scratch reconciliation runs against a temp SQLite DB, mirroring the verification depth of every fix in this gotcha that predates having a live linked client to test against.
    - **Real safety bug found (not yet fixed — flagged), plus two live-shipped features, 2026-08-17.** While answering a user question about what happens if a stray extra tab (e.g. a manually-added "Sheet2") gets edited on a `multi_tab=0` (legacy single-tab) linked client, traced the actual reconcile code path and found the empty-payload safety guard (`if not seen_ids and current: skip delete pass`, added earlier in this gotcha) has a real gap: a row whose `task_id` doesn't match any current task still gets added to `seen_ids` (via the "unrecognized id → recreate" branch, which adds to `seen_ids` *before* checking whether the id is known) before it's handled — so a single garbage row (e.g. literal text `"hello"` sitting in column A of a stray active tab, read by MMGA's still-installed legacy `getActiveSheet()` script) makes `seen_ids` non-empty, which bypasses the "recognized 0 tasks" safety check entirely and lets the delete pass run — deleting every real task for that client. Verified live that MMGA's 14 real tasks were still intact (no damage had occurred yet), advised the user not to edit the stray tab, and offered to fix the guard — **not yet fixed**, this is flagged for a future session; the guard should key off "did any row match a real current task" rather than "was seen_ids non-empty."
      1. **Type/Assigned To/Status dropdown validation on synced Google Sheets, shipped.** User wanted the actual Google Sheet formatted with the same dropdowns Lumina's own Sheets UI has, instead of free-text columns anyone can typo into (the exact root cause `_normalize_type()` exists to paper over, from this gotcha's fourth/sixth audit rounds). `google_sheets_store.py` gained `TYPE_DROPDOWN_OPTIONS`/`STATUS_DROPDOWN_OPTIONS` (matching `projects.html`'s `<select>` options exactly: Story/Reel/Static/Carousel for Type — deliberately excludes Post/Video, which `_normalize_type()` accepts as legacy fallbacks but were never real dropdown options; the 6 social statuses for Status) and `_column_validation_requests()`, applying `setDataValidation` batchUpdate requests: **strict** (rejects anything outside the list) for Type (col E) and Status (col M), since those are genuinely single-value fields; **non-strict** suggestion-list for Assigned To (col L), pulled live from `utils._load_employees()` (not hardcoded — see gotcha #63's lesson) — non-strict because Lumina stores Assigned To as a comma-separated multi-name string, which a strict single-value dropdown would reject outright. Wired into `ensure_tab_exists()` (every newly-created month tab gets it automatically going forward) and a new admin-gated `POST /api/clients/<id>/google-sheet-link/format` route + "Format columns (dropdowns)" button in the Connect modal, for retroactively fixing tabs that predate this. **Live-verified**: deployed, logged in via curl with a real session cookie (same pattern as this gotcha's earlier auth-hardening verification), called the new endpoint against MMGA's real linked sheet — returned `{"success":true,"tabs_formatted":3}`, consistent across two calls (idempotent, not a fluke). Flagged one open discrepancy to the user: the tab strip screenshot showed 4 tabs (`Sheet1` + 3 month tabs) but only 3 were formatted — no server-log access to tell whether `Sheet1` no longer exists or silently failed; asked the user to check.
      2. **Loading-state fix on the Connect/Format buttons, shipped.** User reported clicking Connect gave zero visual feedback while the request was in flight — looked broken rather than slow, made worse by the dropdown-formatting work above adding more sequential API calls to the connect flow. Both buttons now disable and swap their label (`"Connecting…"`/`"Formatting…"`) for the duration of the request. Deliberately did **not** attempt to parallelize the connect-time backfill loop to cut latency — traced through `push_task_to_sheet_multi_tab` and confirmed each call acquires the per-client lock (this gotcha's round-2 concurrency fix) for its full duration, so parallel calls would just queue on that lock instead of actually running concurrently; the lock's serialization is intentional (prevents the exact races that lock was built to close), not a bug to route around.
      3. **Auto-build a brand-new blank spreadsheet on Connect, shipped.** User wanted: give Lumina a link to a genuinely empty Google Sheet (just Google's default "Sheet1", no data) and have it "build everything" automatically, instead of the sheet staying permanently inert. Root cause of the inertness: for a client with zero existing tasks, the backfill loop has nothing to push, so `ensure_tab_exists()` (the only thing that ever writes a header row or creates a properly-named tab) never fires — and a multi-tab client's default `"Sheet1"` isn't a validly-named month/`"Unscheduled"` tab, so Apps Script would never sync it even if data were typed into it by hand. Added `_is_effectively_blank()` (true only when the spreadsheet has exactly one tab and that tab has zero non-empty cells anywhere — false the instant there's more than one tab or any data, so it can never fire on a sheet someone's already started setting up) and `initialize_blank_spreadsheet(spreadsheet_id, multi_tab)`: for `multi_tab=1` clients, renames the default tab to the current month (via the existing `_month_tab_name_for()` convention) and creates a sibling `"Unscheduled"` tab, both with the header row and dropdown validation from fix #1 above; for `multi_tab=0` (legacy) clients, just formats the existing tab in place with no rename. Wired into `create_google_sheet_link()` right before the existing backfill step, wrapped in try/except (must not fail the connect itself), response now reports which tabs were auto-built and the Connect success toast surfaces it. **Verified via scratch tests only** (6 cases: blank-detection true/false for 1-tab-empty/2-tabs/1-tab-with-data, no-op passthrough when not blank, full multi_tab=True rename+header+validation+Unscheduled-creation call sequence, full multi_tab=False format-in-place-no-rename-no-Unscheduled call sequence) plus the full 9-file regression sweep of every earlier scratch test in this gotcha (zero regressions) — **not yet exercised against a real brand-new spreadsheet** (no throwaway test client / fresh Sheet was available this session); if a real blank-sheet connect doesn't come out formatted as described, check first whether `_is_effectively_blank()`'s single-tab-zero-data condition actually matched (e.g. Google Sheets sometimes leaves a lone empty string in a cell from prior interaction, which would correctly disqualify it as "not blank") before assuming the init logic itself is wrong.
      4. **The `seen_ids`/safety-guard bug flagged above (garbage row bypassing the delete-everything guard) is now FIXED, not just flagged.** Added a second set, `recognized_ids`, populated only when a row's `task_id` actually matches an entry in `current` (the client's real tasks) — as opposed to `seen_ids`, which still gets every non-blank id including ones that match nothing (needed for its original, narrower purpose: intra-payload duplicate-id detection). The safety-guard check (`if not seen_ids and current`) and both delete passes (single-tab `_reconcile_sheet_rows_locked`, and multi-tab `_reconcile_sheet_tabs_locked`'s two guards — the all-tabs-empty check and the per-task missing-tab check) now key off `recognized_ids` instead. **Verified by reproducing the exact reported scenario in a scratch test first** (fed a lone `"hello"` row against a temp DB seeded with 14 real MMGA-shaped tasks through the actual `_reconcile_sheet_rows_locked` function) — confirmed it was a real, live bug (would have deleted all 14) before fixing, then confirmed the fix doesn't over-correct (a payload legitimately recognizing some real tasks still correctly deletes the ones genuinely missing from it, `deleted: 2` in a 3-task/1-recognized test case). Full 11-file regression sweep of every scratch test in this gotcha, zero regressions. Deployed; not live-tested against a real webhook call (deliberately — the only way to exercise this against production would mean firing a garbage payload at MMGA's real linked sheet, exactly the scenario this fix protects against).
      5. **Added an in-app step-by-step guide to the Connect Google Sheet modal, shipped.** The modal previously only had dense reference paragraphs (column list, tab-naming rules) with no ordered "what do I actually do" walkthrough, and didn't surface the service-account email to share with until *after* a failed connect attempt. Replaced with two numbered `<ol>` guides: the not-yet-linked view now leads with "1. Create/open a Sheet (blank is fine) → 2. Share it with `<service account email>` → 3. Paste the URL and click Connect → 4. Come back here for the Apps Script step", with the old column/tab-naming reference text collapsed into a `<details>` disclosure for anyone who wants it; the already-linked view's Apps Script instructions became a 5-step list (share check → Extensions>Apps Script → paste+save → pick `installTrigger` in the function dropdown → Run+authorize) instead of one dense paragraph. Backend change to support this: `GET /api/clients/<id>/google-sheet-link` previously returned only `{"linked": false}` for an unlinked client — now also returns `service_account_email` in that case, since the pre-connect guide needs to show it before the user pastes a URL, not only in the post-connect view or an error message.
      6. **A real regression, self-introduced two rounds earlier: Type/Status dropdown validation was strict against values the backend itself writes.** Found by tracing what `_task_to_fields()`/`_normalize_type()` actually produce, not by a live failure report. `_task_to_fields()` always writes Lumina's raw internal snake_case status key (`"not_started"`, `"in_progress"`, ...) — never the Title Case labels the strict `STATUS_DROPDOWN_OPTIONS` rule required. `_normalize_type()` preserves source casing (a lowercase CSV-imported `"story"` stays lowercase, not `"Story"`) and its unrecognized-value fallback is the literal string `"Post"` — not even one of `TYPE_DROPDOWN_OPTIONS`' 4 options (Lumina's own `<select>` never offers Post/Video). Switched both to non-strict (`strict: false`, matching Assigned To's already-safe pattern) instead of rewriting the create/update/diff pipeline to canonicalize casing everywhere — that larger change had real potential to introduce a spurious-diff bug of the same shape as the SQLite `creation_date` issue earlier in this gotcha, not worth it for a UI-convenience feature. Full 11-file regression sweep clean.
      7. **Live-diagnosed why "Format columns" returned `tabs_formatted: 0` against MMGA's real sheet, using the actual production app via `chrome-devtools` MCP, not just scratch tests.** First ruled out permissions (pushed a real MMGA task's fields via `pushNewRowToLinkedSheet`'s exact request shape, called live from the browser console — `{"pushed":true}`, proving writes work at all) before adding a temporary diagnostic route (`debug-format`, unguarded exception, real Google API error text returned in the HTTP response instead of only logged server-side, which this session has no access to). Root cause, confirmed via the real API response: `"Invalid requests[0].setDataValidation: This operation is not allowed on cells in typed columns."` — MMGA's leftover `Sheet1` tab uses Google Sheets' newer **Table** feature (Insert > Table / right-click > Convert to table), whose columns get a distinct "typed column" designation that classic `setDataValidation` batchUpdate calls are flatly rejected against. Not a bug in this codebase's logic — a genuine Google Sheets platform conflict with no retry/workaround. Fixed: `apply_dropdown_validation()` now raises a distinct `TypedColumnError` for this specific 400 response instead of a generic `HTTPError`; `apply_dropdown_validation_to_all_tabs()` returns `{formatted, typed_column_tabs, other_failures}` instead of a bare count, tracking which tab names hit this specific case (the only one with a real user-actionable fix: right-click the table in Sheets → Convert to range) separately from any other failure. Wired through the format route, the connect route's response, and both toasts in `projects.html`, so a user hitting this now sees exactly what's wrong and how to fix it instead of a silent, confusing zero. Removed the temporary diagnostic route once root-caused. **Live-verified end to end** via `chrome-devtools` MCP against the real deployed fix: `POST .../google-sheet-link/format` returned `{"other_failures":0,"success":true,"tabs_formatted":1,"typed_column_tabs":["Sheet1"]}` — the real month tab(s) format successfully, `Sheet1` is correctly and specifically flagged, zero console errors on the page throughout.
      8. **Methodology note for this round, worth remembering**: the user asked "try every possibility a user can use" after being told "no more issues" can't be promised. The direct, honest response was to actually do more live testing rather than assert confidence — logging into the real production app via `chrome-devtools` MCP (already-authenticated session from earlier in the day), clicking through the real Connect modal, firing the real Format button, and reading real network responses is categorically stronger evidence than another round of mocked scratch tests, and it's what actually caught this round's two real findings (the strict-validation regression via code tracing, the typed-column issue via live reproduction + diagnostic instrumentation). Scratch tests remain valuable for pinning down exact logic (they're what proves a fix's *reasoning* is right), but for "is this actually deployed and working," live verification against the real app is what closes the loop — this session had a live, already-authenticated MCP browser session available the whole time and hadn't been using it as the default verification method for every round; worth reaching for by default when it's available, not just when something's already suspected broken.

88. **Attendance CSV export restructured to Date/In/Out/Employee, IST, one row per day — 2026-08-24, commit `8268fbc`.** User's downloaded attendance CSV previously had raw columns (`Timestamp, Employee, Action`) sourced from the `attendance` audit-log table — one row per individual check-in/check-out *event*, timestamps not clearly IST-labeled, and employee shown as a raw id/WhatsApp number in some rows because the old lookup only matched one of several id formats the table actually stores. `attendance_export()` (`routes/attendance.py`) now reads from `daily_attendance` instead (already one row per user per day with `checkin_time`/`checkout_time` as plain `HH:MM:SS` IST strings — see `_attendance_checkin`/`_attendance_checkout` — so no timezone conversion is needed) and writes `Date, In, Out, Employee`. `format_user()` now checks the employee-id map, then a raw-WhatsApp-number map (with and without the `+` prefix, both populated from the same `employees.json` read), before falling back to a generic `WhatsApp (last 4 digits)` label — fixing the name-lookup bug as a side effect of the rewrite, not a separate fix.

89. **Auto-checkout rebuilt via heartbeat + staleness sweep — NOT pagehide/unload — 2026-08-24, commit `0af538f`. Supersedes the reverted gotcha #70/#71 attempt; that revert still stands, this is a different mechanism.** User reported the restructured export (#88 above) had an empty Out column for almost every row and asked why — root cause: the only way `checkout_time` was ever written was the explicit Logout button (`authLogout()` in `auth.js`), which employees rarely click (they just close the tab/browser). Presented via `AskUserQuestion`: user chose to re-attempt auto-checkout-on-tab-close rather than stay manual-only, then approved a heartbeat+sweep design over a faster/riskier unload-event design.
    - **Why not pagehide/beforeunload again:** gotcha #71's post-mortem concluded `pagehide` firing on *every* internal Lumina navigation (not just a real tab close, since this is a multi-page app, not an SPA) was what multiplied write volume into a production DB hang — not the checkout logic itself. A heartbeat has the opposite shape: it fires on a fixed timer regardless of navigation, and infers "gone" from *silence*, so it can never spike on click-heavy usage.
    - **Schema:** `daily_attendance.last_seen_at TEXT DEFAULT NULL` (`db.py`, standard try/except `ALTER TABLE`).
    - **`frontend/auth.js`:** `authGuard()` now calls `sendAttendancePing(authApi, user.user_id)` immediately on successful auth, then every 60s via `setInterval`. No `pagehide`/`beforeunload`/`unload` listener anywhere in the file — deliberate.
    - **`POST /api/attendance/ping`** (`routes/attendance.py::_attendance_ping`) — UPSERTs only `last_seen_at` for today's row (`ON CONFLICT(user_id, date) DO UPDATE`), leaves `checkin_time`/`checkout_time` untouched.
    - **`sweep_stale_checkouts(stale_after_seconds=240)`** — new `task_scheduler.py` job (`attendance_presence_sweep`, `interval`, every 3 minutes, alongside the pre-existing daily 08:00 `check_overdue_tasks` cron). Selects today's rows with a checkin, no checkout, and a non-null `last_seen_at`; for any row whose `last_seen_at` is ≥240s stale, sets `checkout_time = _clamp_work_time(last_seen_at)` — i.e. checkout is backdated to the last real heartbeat, not to whenever the sweep happens to run.
    - **Expected latency, not a bug:** Out won't appear the instant a tab closes — up to ~4min for staleness to cross the threshold, plus up to 3min for the next sweep tick, so up to ~7min worst case.
