Explicit Logout still checks out instantly via the pre-existing `POST /api/attendance/checkout` path, unchanged.
    - **Historical dates stay permanently blank, also not a bug:** a day has an Out time only if someone explicitly logged out that day, or if it's on/after 2026-08-24 (when heartbeat pings started existing at all). There is no heartbeat data to backfill from for any earlier date — confirmed live by checking `/api/attendance/summary`/`/api/attendance/export` for a real employee and finding historical non-null checkouts exactly where an explicit Logout had happened, and nowhere else.
    - **Verification:** scratch test (`test_presence_sweep.py`, mocked `today_ist`/`now_ist`, temp SQLite DB) confirmed: a stale heartbeat gets swept to checkout at the heartbeat's own time; a fresh heartbeat is left alone; an already-checked-out row is untouched; the ping endpoint upserts cleanly on a brand-new user/date row. `pyflakes` clean on `task_scheduler.py`/`routes/attendance.py`/`db.py` (the pre-existing `datetime`/`timedelta`-imported-but-unused warning on `task_scheduler.py` was confirmed via `git stash` to predate this change, not caused by it). Deployed and live-confirmed via `/api/attendance/today`: real employees checked in with `checkout_time: null` while actively using the app, consistent with the sweep correctly not firing on live sessions.

90. **New employee onboarded: Rishika (emp011, Content, PIN 2006) — 2026-08-24.** Same pattern as gotcha #80's Sid/emp010 addition — appended to `config/employees.json`, no code changes needed (`_load_emp_names()`/`_is_admin()` both read the file live, per gotcha #63/#60). `department: "Content"`, `role: "Content"`. Live-verified: `POST /api/auth/login {"user_id":"emp011","pin":"2006"}` → `200 {"success":true,"user":{"name":"Rishika","role":"Content"}}`.
    - **2026-09-09: Rishika has left — `status` set to `"left"`.** Every companion roster filter and `wa_outbox._active_employees()` already exclude `_INACTIVE` statuses (`{"inactive","disabled","left","removed","archived","former"}`), so this alone stops all proactive WhatsApp (login nudges, EOD, standup/attendance) and drops her as an `assign_task`/`remind_teammate` target. Also hardened `whatsapp_agent.identify_sender()` this same change: it previously matched an employee by WhatsApp number regardless of `status`, so a former employee kept full bot access — it now returns `{"kind": "unknown"}` (the "not linked" bounce, zero AI spend) for any number whose employee row is in that same left/inactive set. **Known still-open gap (pre-existing, affects Sid/emp010 too, NOT fixed):** `routes/auth.py::login()` doesn't check `status`, so a `left` employee can still PIN-log into the web app.

91. **Sheets toolbar polish: direct "open the real Google Sheet" link, emoji removed from Sheet buttons, month filter is a dropdown not a button wall — 2026-08-24, commits `979577c`/`9b2f978`.**
    - **Direct Sheet link:** `GET /api/sheets/links` (`routes/sheets_sync.py`) now also returns `spreadsheet_ids` (client_id → spreadsheet_id) alongside the existing `linked_client_ids` — safe to expose (unlike `link_token`, a `spreadsheet_id` isn't a secret; Google's own sharing permissions are still the real gate). `projects.html` uses it to render a small `↗` link next to the "Google Sheet" button (only when that client is linked) that opens `https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/<id>/edit` in a new tab directly — previously the only entry point was the connect/manage modal, with no way to just jump straight into the actual spreadsheet. Styled as one visually-joined control (shared border, rounded only on the outer corners) rather than two separate buttons with the toolbar's usual 8px gap, per user feedback that a plain adjacent button read as unrelated.
    - **Emoji removed:** the 🔗 emoji was dropped from both the Sheets-toolbar "Google Sheet" button and the per-client card-header "Sheet"/"Sheet ✓" badge (`.gsheet-link-btn`, both the initial server-rendered text and `loadLinkedSheetClientIds()`'s post-load update) — per user request, matches this app's established no-emoji convention for functional buttons (see gotcha #82's same request for the row-hide feature).
    - **Month filter is now a `<select>`:** the per-client month tab strip (`.smtab` pill buttons — "All | June 2026 | July 2026 | ... | Unscheduled", added for multi-tab sync in gotcha #87) wrapped the toolbar onto a second line for any client with several month tabs (Mellow had 6). Replaced with a single `<select id="sheet-month-tabs-${clientId}">` dropdown, same `saveMonthFilter()`/`renderClientSheets()` wiring as before — this is a pure layout change, the underlying per-client `localStorage` month-filter state and its "personal view preference, not shared data" scoping (gotcha #83) are unchanged. The `.smtab` CSS class is now unused by this control but was left in place (harmless, not worth a cleanup pass on its own).
    - **Verification:** `node --check` clean on the extracted script block, `pyflakes` clean on `routes/sheets_sync.py`. Deployed; not live-clicked against a real linked client's spreadsheet this session (no live-browser MCP session active for this round) — if the ↗ link 404s or opens the wrong sheet, check first whether `linkedSheetSpreadsheetIds` actually populated (depends on `loadLinkedSheetClientIds()` resolving before the Sheets tab renders) before assuming the URL construction is wrong.

92. **Open, unresolved investigation as of 2026-08-24: "ticking a task done in standup makes it vanish from the Lumina Sheets tab instead of showing Need for approval."** User reports this happens; the fix documented in gotcha #64 (standup completion of a social-media task overrides Notion status to `need_for_approval` instead of `Done`) is present and unmodified in the currently-deployed `update_my_task()` (`routes/ops.py`). Traced so far, no root cause found yet:
    - `frontend/standup.html::toggleTask()` correctly sends `progress: 100` alongside `status: 'done'` whenever the task has a `notion_id` — confirmed not silently skipping the sync call.
    - Live production data showed at least one real task (`Mellow — [Reel] 4 must have basics (Styling)`) sitting correctly at local status `need_for_approval` with a `notion_id` — i.e. the fix demonstrably works for at least one real case, ruling out "the fix is universally broken."
    - `notion_store.get_task_type()`'s bracket-title fallback regex is **start-anchored** (`re.match(r'^\[...\]')`), unlike the unanchored version used everywhere else in the codebase for the same "is this social media" detection (`google_sheets_store.py::_is_social_task()`, fixed to unanchored in gotcha #87's fifth audit round; `projects.html`'s own `socialTasks` filter) — a real inconsistency, candidate for a future fix, but doesn't cleanly explain "vanishing" and doesn't match the one working example found above, so not yet acted on.
    - `frontend/projects.html::buildSheetRecordsForClient()`/`renderClientSheets()` have no status-based row-hiding logic — any status value, recognized or not, still renders in the Sheets grid with default styling. Ruled out as an explanation for a row disappearing.
    - **Next step, if this recurs:** get the specific client + task name and trace that task's live Notion state directly (Status/Type/Client ID properties, title) — per systematic-debugging, do not attempt another fix without a concrete repro. Worth also checking whether the task's `Client ID` property is intact (a blank/wrong `Client ID` would make it invisible to the per-client Sheets view entirely, independent of status — see the orphaned-task pattern in gotcha #79/#87's "Client ID" discussion) as an alternative explanation to the status-override theory.

93. **Bulk-import silent failures, zero-retry Sheets API calls, and a CRITICAL reconnect-race data-loss bug — all found/fixed 2026-08-24/25, commits `9773f80`/`be0494c`/`55bb817`.** Surfaced while helping the user import+link Omotec's Google Sheet.
    - **Silent partial-failure in bulk social-post import:** `notion_store.create_task()` returns `None` on any Notion API failure; `auto_generate_tasks()`'s import loop (`app.py`) silently dropped failed rows with zero trace, still responding `"success": true`. Root-caused via timing correlation (early-chronological rows failed while later ones succeeded, tracking Notion rate-limit easing after an earlier bulk delete). **Fix:** one retry with `time.sleep(5)` on failure, failed rows collected into `failed_posts` and returned as `social_posts_failed`; `importClientSocialCSV()` (`projects.html`) now surfaces failed titles in an error toast instead of a blind success toast. **Never treat a bulk-create loop's per-item failure as safe to swallow — always collect and report it, even if the overall request "succeeds."**
    - **Zero retry-on-429/5xx across all 14 raw Sheets API call sites** in `google_sheets_store.py` (unlike the Notion side, which already had `_notion_request()`'s 3-attempt backoff). Generalized into a shared `_sheets_request(method, url, raise_for_status=True, **kwargs)` wrapper (3 attempts, exponential backoff on 429/5xx) and applied everywhere. If you add a new raw Sheets API call in this file, route it through `_sheets_request()`, not a bare `session.get/post/put(...)`.
    - **CRITICAL — reconnecting a client to Google Sheets deleted 26 real tasks.** Root cause: `create_google_sheet_link()`'s connect-time backfill loop (`routes/sheets_sync.py`) only held the per-client lock for each individual `push_task_to_sheet` call, not across the whole loop — leaving gaps between pushes where a reconcile webhook (fired by the target Sheet's *already-installed* Apps Script trigger from a prior partial connect) could race in, see a partial/incomplete snapshot mid-backfill, and delete every real task not yet pushed. **Fix:** the entire backfill sequence (`initialize_blank_spreadsheet` → push loop → `apply_dropdown_validation_to_all_tabs`) is now wrapped in one `with gs._get_client_sheet_lock(client_id):` block, and the lock itself changed from `threading.Lock` to `threading.RLock` (each `push_fn` call re-acquires the same lock internally — a plain `Lock` would deadlock on that nested acquire). Verified with a real (non-mocked) threading test before deploying, not just a mock. **Relinking/reconnecting any client's Google Sheet is inherently high-risk if that Sheet already has an installed trigger — treat it as a data-loss-capable operation, not routine config.**
    - **Notion's public API cannot see trashed/archived pages at all** — confirmed empirically (a full-workspace Search returned 717 pages, 0 with `archived`/`in_trash` true). `archive_notion_page()`'s "soft delete" is only recoverable by a human manually restoring from Notion's own Trash UI — there is no automated-restore code path possible via the API. **Do not offer to build an automated Notion Trash restore without testing feasibility first** — confirm via a read-only Search probe before promising it.
    - **Recovery playbook used for the above (worked, but not fully hardened):** delete the corrupted client's tasks → re-import the correct full set via Add Tasks/CSV → connect a brand-new blank Google Sheet (never reuse a Sheet that still has an old, uninvestigated trigger installed). Even after relinking to a new spreadsheet (which mints a fresh `link_token`), the OLD sheet's still-installed trigger kept firing and caused a second wave of deletions (44→26→24 tasks) during recovery — **a request already in-flight from the old trigger before the DB row updates can still race using a stale `link` object; this was not fixed in code, only resolved because the user deleted the old spreadsheet outright.** If this recurs: the code fix would be re-fetching the link row's current `spreadsheet_id`/token synchronously inside the webhook handler before processing, or invalidating the old token more aggressively. **When decommissioning/replacing a linked Google Sheet, always instruct the user to delete the Apps Script trigger (or the whole old spreadsheet) — do not assume changing Lumina's DB link alone stops the old trigger from firing.**

94. **Live Attendance dashboard permanently stuck on "Not in yet" for an employee who really did log in — root-caused live against production, fixed 2026-08-26.** User reported Noorish (emp009) logged in but her card never showed an In time. Confirmed via a read-only `curl https://lumina.mmga.agency/api/attendance/today` (this endpoint has no auth check, safe to hit directly) that her row for the day genuinely existed with `"checkin_time":null,"checkout_time":null`.
    - **Root cause:** the heartbeat added in gotcha #89 (`_attendance_ping()`, fired every 60s by `auth.js` from any open Lumina page) does `INSERT INTO daily_attendance (user_id, date, last_seen_at) VALUES (...) ON CONFLICT(user_id, date) DO UPDATE SET last_seen_at=...` — if a ping ever reaches the server before (or without) a real login-triggered checkin for that day (e.g. an already-open tab from a still-valid session pinging before she goes through `login.html` fresh), it creates the day's `daily_attendance` row with `checkin_time` left NULL. `_attendance_checkin()` (`routes/attendance.py`, fired from `login.html`'s `submitPin()`) then did `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT(user_id, date) DO NOTHING` — since a row already existed (from the ping), the real checkin's insert was a complete no-op, permanently leaving `checkin_time` NULL for that day even on a genuine login. Login itself worked fine (session created, redirect succeeded) — only the fire-and-forget `/api/attendance/checkin` call silently did nothing, and `login.html` swallows that call's errors (`.catch(() => {})`), so nothing surfaced anywhere.
    - **Fix:** changed the conflict clause to `DO UPDATE SET checkin_time = excluded.checkin_time WHERE daily_attendance.checkin_time IS NULL` — still "first real checkin of the day wins" (a second login later the same day is a no-op, since checkin_time is no longer NULL), but a ping-created blank row now gets correctly filled in by the next real checkin instead of being permanently stuck.
    - **Verification:** pyflakes clean. Verified the exact upsert semantics against a real scratch in-memory SQLite DB (not just reasoning about the SQL): simulated ping-creates-blank-row → real-checkin-fills-it-in (rowcount 1, checkin_time set) → second-checkin-same-day-is-noop (rowcount 0, original time preserved). Not yet deployed/backfilled — Noorish's actual row for 2026-08-26 will need either a real re-login or one `POST /api/attendance/checkin {"user_id":"emp009"}` call after this ships to retroactively fix today's row (safe and idempotent under the new logic).
    - **If a similar "logged in but not showing" report recurs for anyone else:** check `GET /api/attendance/today` first for that user's exact row (null checkin_time + non-null last_seen_at is the fingerprint of this exact race) before assuming it's a frontend display bug.

95. **Sheets sync — refuse to link a Google Sheet to a stale/deleted `client_id`, and make Notion-mode client delete idempotent — 2026-08-25, commit `e25f809`** (cherry-picked onto `main` from `worktree-whatsapp-standup`'s `156dd15` so it ships without that branch's still-unverified WhatsApp-standup feature — see the note at the end of this section). **The code comment in `routes/sheets_sync.py::create_google_sheet_link()` for this fix mislabels it "CLAUDE.md gotcha #94" — #94 is the unrelated attendance-checkin race; this fix is #95.**
    - **Root cause, found live on Omotec:** a browser tab left open from *before* a client was deleted still holds the old `client_id` in its in-memory `allData`. Clicking "Connect Google Sheet" on that stale page happily created a fully-working sync link pointing at a client that no longer exists — the immediate backfill push finds zero current tasks (so nothing looks wrong), but the link and its webhook stay live, and the very next Sheet edit would make `reconcile_sheet_rows()` treat every existing Sheet row as an unrecognized id and **recreate them all as brand-new tasks under a dead `client_id` nothing in the UI can see**. No orphans were actually created that specific time only because the stale client's tasks had already been archived; a client deleted moments earlier (tasks not yet archived, or a slower connection) would not have been so lucky.
    - **Fix 1 — `notion_store.is_client_active(client_notion_id)`** (`notion_store.py` ~930): fetches the page and checks it isn't archived. `create_google_sheet_link()` now calls this (or, for `is_notion=0` clients, a plain `SELECT 1 FROM clients WHERE id=?` existence check) *before doing anything else*, and 404s with "This client no longer exists … Refresh the page and try again." instead of silently succeeding.
    - **Fix 2 — `notion_delete_client()` (`routes/ops.py` ~1555) is now idempotent:** re-archiving an already-archived Notion page 500'd (hit firsthand during manual recovery). It now calls `is_client_active()` first; if the client is already gone it returns 200 success — still running the straggler-task-archive and `_unlink_google_sheet_for_client()` (`ops.py` ~1528) cleanup, just not re-archiving the client page itself. (The SQLite-mode `delete_client()` in `app.py` already `DELETE`s the `google_sheet_links` row — gotcha #87 round 6 — and is naturally idempotent.)
    - **Verified** with a scratch test (mocked `notion_store`/`google_sheets_store`, Flask test client): a dead `client_id` is rejected 404 while a live one passes unaffected, and calling delete-client twice in a row no longer 500s on the second call. `pyflakes` clean, `import app` boots clean.

96. **Sheets sync — manual "Pull now" button, no edit-to-trigger needed — 2026-08-27, commit `c1b1f6a`.** Connecting a Sheet backfills Lumina→Sheet, but the reverse direction (Sheet→Lumina) only ever runs on an Apps Script `onChange` event — so a freshly-built calendar sheet full of hand-typed rows synced *nothing* into Lumina until every row was re-edited by hand to fire the trigger.
    - **New route `POST /api/clients/<id>/google-sheet-link/pull-now`** (`routes/sheets_sync.py`, admin-gated via `_verified_user_id()`, `@limiter.limit("10 per minute")`): reads the Sheet's current live state directly via the Sheets API and calls the *same* `reconcile_sheet_rows()` / `reconcile_sheet_tabs()` the webhook uses (honoring `MAX_WEBHOOK_ROWS`, recording `last_pull_at`/`last_pull_summary`). Dispatches on the link's `multi_tab` flag exactly like the webhook.
    - **Frontend:** a "Pull now" button (`#gsheet-pull-btn` → `pullGoogleSheetNow(clientId)`, `frontend/projects.html` ~4250) in the Connect Google Sheet modal.
    - A throwaway diagnostic route was added (`5191baf`) then removed once the round-2 Omotec bugs below were root-caused (`b067c11`) — don't look for it.

97. **Sheets sync — Omotec incident, round 2 (2026-08-27): non-ISO dates silently dropped every row, plus a runaway duplicate-task-creation loop.** Both surfaced trying to sync a real 47-row Omotec calendar; this is a second, separate round from the 2026-08-24/25 Omotec work in gotcha #93.
    - **[commit `c57d6c4`] Every row with a non-ISO date synced as *nothing*.** Notion's date property requires strict ISO-8601 (`YYYY-MM-DD`), but the Sheet's Creation Date / Post Day columns are plain text *by design* (to dodge locale-dependent Date-cell rendering — see gotcha #87), so people type whatever date format they normally use. Any other format 400s the *entire* create/update page request (not just that one property — see gotcha #43), and that 400 isn't retried, so `create_task()` silently returned `None` for every affected row with zero visible error. Omotec's 47-row calendar synced 0 rows because every date was `DD/MM/YYYY` or `DD/Month/YYYY`. **Fix:** `_row_to_fields()` (`google_sheets_store.py` ~890) now normalizes `due_date` and `creation_date` to ISO via `_normalize_date()` before they ever reach Notion — tries `_DATE_INPUT_FORMATS` (`%d/%m/%Y`, `%d/%B/%Y`, `%d-%m-%Y`, `%d-%B-%Y`, `%d/%b/%Y`, `%d-%b-%Y`, `%Y/%m/%d`) in order and drops an unparseable date (empty) rather than sending it through raw. Also adds a `create_failed` counter to both reconcile functions (matching the existing `update_failed`/`delete_failed` pattern) so a future silent create-failure surfaces in the sync summary instead of only in server logs.
    - **[commit `7ac9f32`] Runaway duplicate-creation loop.** Reconcile wrote each newly-created row's task id back to the Sheet one cell at a time (one `write_cell` PUT per row). Each of those write-backs is itself a Sheet edit that re-fires the installed `onChange` trigger — and a re-triggered run reads the whole sheet fresh mid-pass, sees any row that hadn't gotten its id written back *yet* as still blank, and creates it *again*. That row's write-back re-fires again, and so on: confirmed live as a real incident where task count climbed 44 → 66 → 68 → 86 … and eventually started deleting real tasks too, only stopped by deleting the trigger and restarting the server. **Fix:** every write-back from one reconcile pass is now collected into `pending_writebacks` and flushed as a single `batch_write_cells()` call (via `_retry()`) *after* the whole pass finishes, in both `reconcile_sheet_rows()` and `reconcile_sheet_tabs()`. At most one re-trigger can fire after a pass, and it finds everything already matching — the cascade can't start.

98. **WhatsApp CRM/knowledge agent -- built 2026-09-02, on `main`, but cannot run end-to-end yet (no WABA -- Meta business verification for MMGA AGENCY LLP still in review).** User wants the team (and later clients) to ask questions on WhatsApp and get answers pulled from the live CRM + the project knowledge base. Built independent of the unmerged `worktree-whatsapp-standup` branch (that one is a deterministic command bot -- `done`/`add`/`blocked`; this is a tool-using Q&A agent) so neither blocks the other; both dispatch from the same `/whatsapp/webhook`.
    - **New module `backend/whatsapp_agent.py`.** `handle_message(sender, text) -> str|None` is the whole entry point; `app.py::whatsapp_webhook()` now just extracts the text and calls it (the old inline single-shot Haiku call + its budget/usage bookkeeping were removed -- the agent owns all of that now).
    - **Identity (`identify_sender`)** -- read live every message, never cached (gotcha #63): matches the inbound number against `employees.json`'s `whatsapp` field (last-10-digits scheme, identical to `whatsapp_standup._normalize_phone` so the two stay mergeable) -> `{"kind":"employee"}`; else against a new **`client_users.whatsapp`** column (ALTER TABLE in `db.py`, default `''`, no UI to populate it yet -- set by hand or a future client-admin field) -> `{"kind":"client"}`; else `{"kind":"unknown"}` -> one polite "this number isn't linked" reply and **zero AI spend**.
    - **Scoped tools.** Employees (all admin-tier here, gotcha #60) get `get_clients`, `get_client_tasks(client_name)`, `get_my_tasks`, `search_knowledge_base(query)`, `get_team_overview`. Clients get **only** `get_my_tasks`, hard-scoped to their own `client_notion_id`, assignee field stripped, system prompt forbids mentioning other clients/internal matters. Data layer is Notion-first (`notion_store.list_clients/list_tasks`) with a SQLite `tasks`/`clients` fallback; `search_knowledge_base` runs an unscoped `bm25()` MATCH over the whole `kb_chunks_fts` table (reuses `kb_retriever._fts_query`) rather than fighting that table's `(project_id,user_id)` row-scoping.
    - **Model + loop.** Haiku via `get_model_for_task("whatsapp")`, `max_tokens=900`, agentic tool loop capped at `_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS=6`, tokens summed across all rounds and `record_usage`'d once. Budget guard (`check_budget_available`) up front.
    - **Continuity.** New `whatsapp_agent_context` table (`sender` PK, `messages` JSON, `updated_at`) stores only plain user/assistant **text** turns (never tool-use scaffolding), last 6 pairs, ignored + restarted if older than `_CONTEXT_TTL_HOURS=6`.
    - **Verification:** `pyflakes` clean on the new file + `db.py` (no new warnings), `import app` boots (166 routes, the usual harmless local `no such table: tasks`), and a scratch functional test with a fake Anthropic client exercised the full employee path (identify -> tool_use round -> tool executed -> context persisted -> short reply) plus the unknown-sender no-spend path. **Not exercised against a real WhatsApp message** -- blocked on the WABA, same as everything else in gotchas #87's WhatsApp thread / the standup NOTE below. When the number goes live: set Railway `WHATSAPP_PHONE_NUMBER_ID` + `WHATSAPP_ACCESS_TOKEN`, subscribe the webhook to the `messages` field, then send a text from a number in `employees.json`.
    - **Follow-up (same session) -- laptop-as-storage sync.** User wants to keep the source documents on a spare laptop but let Railway run the bot 24/7 (picked "laptop stores, Railway runs" over self-hosting the webhook via a tunnel). Added 3 token-gated endpoints in `app.py` -- `POST /api/storage/doc` (multipart file or `{filename,content}` json; parses binaries via `file_processor`; idempotent replace by filename), `GET /api/storage/docs`, `DELETE /api/storage/doc?name=` -- all landing under a sentinel `project_id="_storage"` so `whatsapp_agent._kb_search`'s unscoped `kb_chunks_fts` MATCH picks them up with no extra wiring. Auth: `Authorization: Bearer <STORAGE_SYNC_TOKEN or FLASK_SECRET_KEY>`, `hmac.compare_digest`. Two new `project_store` helpers back it: `list_knowledge_base_docs(pid)`, `delete_knowledge_base_docs_by_filename(pid, name) -> [doc_id]`. Laptop side: **`scripts/kb_sync.py`** (standalone, `requests` only) -- walks a folder, sha256 manifest in `<dir>/.kb_sync_state.json`, uploads new/changed, deletes server docs whose local file is gone; `--watch` loops. Relative path is the doc name so nested per-client folders don't collide. **Set `STORAGE_SYNC_TOKEN` on Railway** before using it (falls back to `FLASK_SECRET_KEY` if unset -- works but reuses the session secret). Verified with a scratch test: 401 without token, add/list/idempotent-replace/delete, and confirmed the agent's `_kb_search` finds a synced doc and stops finding it after delete. Also **end-to-end against a locally-running server** (`scripts/kb_sync.py --dir <folder> --url http://localhost:5000`): initial upload, change-detect + re-upload, local-delete -> server-delete + FTS deindex, agent retrieval reflecting each step. `pyflakes` clean (pre-existing `project_store` `os`-unused warning is not mine), `import app` boots (169 routes).
    - **Client identification wired up (commit `8850c4d`):** `client_users.whatsapp` is now read/written by `GET/POST/PUT /api/auth/clients` (`routes/auth.py`) and there's a "WhatsApp number" field on a linked portal account in `client-admin.html` (same PUT path as the existing "Save Link"). So a client can be given scoped WhatsApp access from the admin UI, not just a manual DB edit.
    - **Client scope-leak guard (commit `87263b3`):** `_run_tool`'s client `get_my_tasks` now **refuses** unless `identity["client_notion_id"]` is set — it never falls back to name matching for a client, because `_find_client()` does a loose `name_l in candidate` contains-match that could return a *different* client's tasks (e.g. "AG" matching "AGrius"). An unlinked client gets "your account isn't fully linked" instead. The name-match fallback is still used for the **employee** path, where cross-client visibility is intended (gotcha #60).
    - **Web search (employee only).** `_EMPLOYEE_TOOLS` now also carries Anthropic's hosted `{"type": "web_search_20250305", "name": "web_search", "max_uses": 4}` — the same server-tool spec `app.py`'s stream endpoint uses (~line 2204). `_CLIENT_TOOLS` deliberately does not get it. The tool loop gained a `stop_reason == "pause_turn"` branch (echo `resp.content` back, continue) since a server-tool run can span turns; `_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS` 6 -> 8, `max_tokens` 900 -> 1200. No local execution needed — the API resolves the search inline and the `web_search_tool_result` blocks ride along in `resp.content`. Scratch test covers: web_search present for employees / absent for clients, and a `pause_turn` -> resume -> final-answer sequence.
    - **`scripts/research_feed.py`** — unattended URL research that feeds the same knowledge folder. Reads `<dir>/research_sources.txt` (`label | url` per line), fetches each with `requests` + a stdlib `HTMLParser` text extractor (drops script/style/nav/footer), writes `<dir>/research/<slug>.md` (overwritten each run, so no stale accumulation). `--watch --interval` loops. Depends only on `requests`. Composes with `kb_sync.py` (which uploads the folder). Live-tested against real URLs (example.com, news.ycombinator.com) — text extracted and written correctly. This is the "laptop as a research worker" piece — the one place where keeping the laptop always-on actually matters.
    - **`scripts/laptop_agent.py`** — one always-on process that runs all of it on staggered intervals: (1) knowledge sync via `kb_sync.sync_once` (30s), (2) research via `research_feed.run_once` (1h), (3) **DB backup** — `GET {url}/admin/download-db?secret={FLASK_SECRET_KEY}` -> `<dir>/../lumina-backups/app-<stamp>.db`, keeps last 14 (24h), (4) **health watchdog** — `GET /api/health`, on failure logs `ALERT:` and POSTs `{"text": ...}` to `ALERT_WEBHOOK` if set (5m). Jobs 1–3 self-skip when unconfigured (no `STORAGE_SYNC_TOKEN` / no `research_sources.txt` / no `FLASK_SECRET_KEY`); health always runs. `imports kb_sync` + `research_feed` as modules (both `__main__`-guarded, safe). Scratch-tested: all four job functions run without error against the real deployed Lumina (research fetched + wrote, health pinged live, backup/sync correctly no-op without creds). Depends only on `requests`. Task Scheduler "At log on" with `pythonw.exe` = survives reboots.
    - **Laptop-side libs (2026-09-02):** `scripts/research_feed.py` now uses **`trafilatura`** for extraction when installed (`trafilatura.extract(..., favor_precision=True)`), falling back to the stdlib `HTMLParser` strip if the import fails — cleaner article text, no regression when absent. `scripts/laptop_agent.py` gained `_notify()` which fans a health alert out via **`apprise`** to `ALERT_TARGETS` (comma-separated apprise URLs — Telegram/Discord/email/ntfy), falling back to the old single `ALERT_WEBHOOK`, then console-only. Both libs are optional and **not** in the app's `requirements.txt` (they never run on Railway) — new `scripts/requirements.txt` + `scripts/.env.example` cover the laptop install. Verified: pyflakes clean, `research_feed.py` run through trafilatura against a live page, `_notify()` smoke-tested with no-target (silent) and bad-target (no raise).
    - **Bridge supervision (commit `30d1d92`):** `scripts/laptop_agent.py` gained a 5th job that spawns `node whatsapp-bridge/index.js` and keeps it alive (restart on crash, 5s-per-consecutive-fast-fail backoff capped 60s, exit-accounting runs exactly once per crash then `proc` is cleared). Auto-skips when node isn't on PATH, `whatsapp-bridge/node_modules` is absent, or no bridge token is set; `--no-bridge` opts out. So the always-on laptop is one process / one Task Scheduler entry, not two. Same commit: `index.js` now dedupes Baileys message redeliveries (bounded `seen` id Set) so a reconnect can't double-reply, and the Lumina call timeout went 60s -> 90s. Bridge-job state machine scratch-tested (spawn -> fast-crash -> backoff -> no re-accounting -> respawn -> clean `_stop_bridge` on Ctrl+C).
    - **Companion endpoints + expanded laptop_agent (2026-09-02).** New `backend/routes/companion.py` blueprint (registered in `app.py`), all token-gated with the same `?token=` / `Authorization: Bearer` = `STORAGE_SYNC_TOKEN`|`FLASK_SECRET_KEY` scheme as the storage-sync endpoints (`_auth_ok()`, `hmac.compare_digest`): `GET /api/companion/digest?window=morning|evening` (server-assembles a daily brief straight from the DB + `notion_store.list_tasks()` + `budget_tracker` — overdue/due-today/due-tomorrow tasks, today's standup submissions + done/pending task counts, `google_sheet_links` push-fail / stale-pull flags, API-budget %; returns `{text, ...structured}`), `GET /api/companion/uploads-archive` (streams a zip of `logs/uploads/` — the client files / voice notes the DB backup doesn't include), `GET /api/companion/sheets-health` (per-linked-client `last_push_ok` / `last_pull_at` / `pull_stale`), `POST /api/companion/sheets-pull-all` (reconciles every linked sheet). The last one reuses `routes/sheets_sync.py::pull_one_link(link, link_token)` — a helper **extracted this session** from the body of `pull_google_sheet_now` (the manual pull-now route), which now just builds the `link` dict and calls it. `laptop_agent.py` gained: job 6 **daily brief** (wall-clock, `_daily_due()` fires a job once/day within a 40-min window of a `HH:MM` target — `--digest-morning`/`--digest-evening`, default 09:00/19:00; sends the digest `text` via `DIGEST_TARGETS`→`ALERT_TARGETS`→webhook→console), job 7 **sheets watchdog** (`--sheets-every` default 1200s; alerts on `sheets-health` issues, only re-alerts when the failing-client set *changes*; `--sheets-autopull` also POSTs pull-all), **offsite backups** (`--backup-mirror <dir>` copies each `.db` into a second folder e.g. a cloud-synced one; `BACKUP_RCLONE_REMOTE` runs `rclone copy` if `rclone` is on PATH; job_backup also pulls the uploads archive every `UPLOADS_EVERY_DAYS`=7), and a **timed health check** (logs every ping to `<dir>/../lumina-logs/health.jsonl`, alerts on `>--slow-ms` (default 8000) or the 2nd consecutive failure — not the 1st, to swallow blips). All verified with a Flask-test-client scratch test (auth 401s, morning/evening digest render + counts, sheets-health issue flagging, uploads-archive 404-when-empty, pull-all graceful per-link failure) + `pyflakes` clean + `import app` boots (176 routes). Not yet run against a live deploy or a real notification channel.
    - **Semantic knowledge-base layer (commit pending) — `backend/semantic_kb.py`, OFF by default, near-zero deploy risk.** Adds meaning-based KB retrieval on top of the existing `kb_chunks_fts` bm25 keyword search (so "refund" surfaces a doc that only says "returns & cancellations"). Design constraints honoured: **nothing runs until an admin flips it on**, callers always keep their keyword results (semantic hits are *merged in ahead*, never replace), and any failure silently falls back to keyword-only.
      - **Deps are NOT in `requirements.txt`** — they live in `backend/requirements-semantic.txt` (`model2vec>=0.3.0`, `numpy>=1.24`). So merging/deploying this commit changes nothing at runtime: `semantic_kb.available()` is `False` until those two lines are added to the root `requirements.txt` and redeployed (the one step with deploy risk — watch that deploy). `model2vec` = static CPU embeddings, ~30 MB model, no torch/onnxruntime, no embeddings API key/cost. Model downloads once to `$HF_HOME`, which `semantic_kb.py` points at `<db dir>/hf_cache` (the persistent volume) so it survives redeploys.
      - **New table `kb_chunk_vectors`** (`db.py`): `fts_rowid` (mirrors `kb_chunks_fts.rowid`), `dim`, `vec` BLOB (unit-norm float32). Orphans swept by `semantic_kb.prune()`. Search is brute-force cosine in numpy — fine for this KB's size (hundreds–low-thousands of chunks); revisit if it ever gets huge.
      - **Hooks:** `kb_retriever.index_doc()` / `delete_doc_index()` call `semantic_kb.index_doc()` / `.prune()` (guarded import; no-op unless active) — so every existing indexing path (app.py storage endpoint, project KB endpoint, `kb_sync.py`) gets vectors for free. `kb_retriever.search_hybrid()` and `whatsapp_agent._kb_search()` fold `semantic_kb.search()` hits in front of the keyword list, deduped.
      - **Toggle/inspect:** `GET /api/kb/semantic` -> `{available, enabled, active, model, vectors, fts_chunks, backfill}`; `POST /api/kb/semantic {"enabled": true, "user_id": "<emp>"}` flips the `app_settings` key `kb_semantic_enabled` and kicks a background backfill of every existing chunk; `POST /api/kb/semantic/backfill` re-runs it. Env `KB_SEMANTIC_ENABLED=1/0` overrides the stored flag; `KB_SEMANTIC_MODEL` overrides the model id.
      - **Verified** with a scratch test using a fake deterministic embedder (model2vec/numpy weren't installed; numpy since added to the venv for the vector math): inactive-guard (deps absent -> `available()` False, every fn no-ops, keyword search unaffected), then active -> a "refund" query surfaces `returns.md` that keyword search alone missed, project/user scoping, `prune()` after a doc delete, `whatsapp_agent._kb_search` merge+dedupe, and toggling back off. pyflakes clean, `import app` boots (172 routes). **Not run against the real `model2vec` model or a live deploy.**
    - **`whatsapp-bridge/` — Baileys bridge (unblocks WhatsApp without Meta).** A Node/ESM service (`@whiskeysockets/baileys` ^6.7.9 + `pino` + `qrcode-terminal`, 90 pkgs) run on the always-on laptop. Pairs a **dedicated** WhatsApp number (QR scan once, session in `whatsapp-bridge/auth/` — **gitignored**, holds live creds). On an inbound DM it POSTs `{from, text}` to the new **`POST /whatsapp/bridge`** endpoint (`app.py`, `_bridge_auth_ok()` = Bearer `WHATSAPP_BRIDGE_TOKEN` | `STORAGE_SYNC_TOKEN` | `FLASK_SECRET_KEY`, `@limiter.limit("60/min")`), which just calls `whatsapp_agent.handle_message(sender, text)` and returns `{"reply": ...}` — same agent as the Meta webhook (identity, tools, web search, context, budget all apply). **Reply-only by construction**: `sendMessage` is only ever called inside the `messages.upsert` handler after a reply comes back; groups / status / `@newsletter` / own messages are all skipped. Reconnects on drop unless `DisconnectReason.loggedOut`. **Ban risk** is real but scoped to the one dedicated number (never a personal/main number) — internal, reply-only, low-volume use is low-risk; see the session discussion. Verified: `node --check`, Baileys named-import shape confirmed (`import makeWASocket, { useMultiFileAuthState, ... }` — NOT `baileys.default` destructure, that was the first-cut bug), a 14s smoke run connected to WhatsApp and printed a live pairing QR with no errors; the Lumina endpoint tested for 401/400/unknown-sender. `.gitignore` also now covers `whatsapp-bridge/node_modules/`, `lumina-backups/`, `knowledge/`. **Not yet paired to a real number** — needs the user's dedicated SIM + `WHATSAPP_BRIDGE_TOKEN` set on Railway.
    - **Bridge went LIVE end-to-end, 2026-09-03 — dedicated number `+91 6351 413 409` paired, replying to real employees from real data.** This is the first working WhatsApp round trip for Lumina; the Meta WABA path is still stuck in business verification and is now a nice-to-have, not a blocker.
      - **Pairing gotcha (not a bug):** WhatsApp blocks companion-device linking on a brand-new number for the first several hours (anti-abuse). The QR scan just says "try again later" until the account has been used normally (name/photo set, a couple of messages sent) and enough time has passed. Waited it out, paired on the second attempt. After a successful pair the socket immediately closes once with code **515 (`restart-required`)** — this is normal, the reconnect loop handles it and the next connect is stable.
      - **LID resolution — commit `eb490fd`, the fix that made it actually work.** WhatsApp now often addresses an inbound DM by a **LID** (`<id>@lid`, a privacy identifier) instead of `<phone>@s.whatsapp.net`. The bridge was deriving `from` by stripping `@lid` off `remoteJid`, handing `whatsapp_agent.identify_sender()` a 15-digit non-phone that matched nobody → every real employee got "this number isn't linked". Fix in the `messages.upsert` handler: derive identity from **`msg.key.senderPn`** (the phone JID Baileys attaches alongside a LID) first, then fall back to `sock.signalRepository?.lidMapping?.getPNForLID(jid)`, then the raw jid; the **reply is still sent to `remoteJid`** (the LID address works fine as a send target). Logs `(resolved <lid> -> <phone>)` or `WARN: LID … no phone number available`. **Confirmed live:** Abhinav (emp003, `+91 97029 08716`) messaged the bridge number and got a correct reply listing his real overdue tasks. If a real employee ever gets "not linked" again, first check the bridge console for that WARN line — it means `senderPn` was absent on their message and neither fallback resolved it, and the next step is a reverse LID→PN map built from `sock.onWhatsApp()` rather than another guess.
      - **Other bridge hardening shipped alongside** (commits `30d1d92`, `67871fd`, `eb490fd`): bounded `seen` Set (500 ids) dedupes Baileys' reconnect redeliveries so one message can't get two replies; Lumina call timeout 60s → 90s (agent tool loop + web search is slow); explicit exit on `DisconnectReason.connectionReplaced` (440) so a second bridge instance taking the session doesn't cause a reconnect fight — the `laptop_agent.py` supervisor (`job_bridge`) restarts it if that was a stale orphan.
      - **Group support — BUILT 2026-09-03 (this session), allow-list + staff-only.** The bridge no longer skips `@g.us`. A group message is acted on only when **all three** hold: (1) the bot was *addressed* — text starts with `lumina`/`@lumina`/`/ask`/`!ask`/`@assistant` (regex `GROUP_PREFIX` in `index.js`, stripped before forwarding), or the bot's jid is in `contextInfo.mentionedJid`, or the message is a reply to one of the bot's own messages (`contextInfo.participant` == bot); (2) the group's numeric id is on the allow-list; (3) `identify_sender()` returns `kind == "employee"` — a client or unknown in the room gets **silence**, never a reply, because the whole group sees it (this is the scope-leak guard: allow-list a group only when every member is staff). Any of the three failing → `handle_message` returns `None` and the bridge sends nothing (and, on a Lumina *error* mid-group, also stays silent rather than posting error noise for everyone).
        - **Bridge (`index.js`)**: `askLumina(from, text, groupId, groupNm)` now sends `{from, text, group_id, group_name}`; identity for a group message comes from `msg.key.participantPn` (→ `signalRepository.lidMapping.getPNForLID` on the participant → raw participant), mirroring the DM LID logic on the participant field; reply is sent to the group `remoteJid`. Group subject is fetched once per jid via `sock.groupMetadata()` and cached in-process. Log lines: `grp <name> <- <phone>: <text>` and `(no reply — group not enabled or sender not staff)`.
        - **Agent (`whatsapp_agent.py`)**: `handle_message(sender, text, *, group_id=None, group_name=None)`. `_group_allowlist()` merges env `WHATSAPP_GROUP_ALLOWLIST` (comma/space separated) + `app_settings` key `whatsapp_group_allowlist` (JSON array), all compared digits-only (`_norm_group`) so a bare id or a full `…@g.us` both work. Context + usage are keyed `f"{sender}|{group_digits}"` so a person's group thread doesn't bleed into their DM thread. The employee system prompt gains a "you're in a group chat '<name>', keep it to one or two lines, don't @-mention anyone" line when `in_group`.
        - **Runtime allow-list management**: `GET/POST /api/whatsapp/groups` (`app.py`, token-gated by `_bridge_auth_ok()` — same Bearer scheme as `/whatsapp/bridge`). `GET` → `{stored, env, effective}`; `POST {"add": "<id or …@g.us>"}` / `{"remove": "<id>"}` edits the `app_settings` row (no redeploy). The env var is a read-only fallback merged on top.
        - **Verified**: `node --check` clean; `pyflakes` clean on `whatsapp_agent.py` (app.py only shows its pre-existing baseline noise); `import app` boots (177 routes, `/api/whatsapp/groups` registered). 100. **WhatsApp agent — cross-person standup writes + proactive DM "outbox", 2026-09-03 (commits `ec4408e`, plus outbox follow-up).** User wanted, from WhatsApp (DM and group), what the web Standup screen already does: assign work to a teammate, and be pinged when someone assigns/hands you something.
    - **`assign_task {name, task}`** (`whatsapp_agent.py`, `_EMPLOYEE_TOOLS`) — inserts a fresh `pending` row on that teammate's `standup_tasks` for today, `delegated_from = sender`. **`delegate_my_task {task, name}`** — `_match_standup_task()` finds one of the sender's own rows, sets `status='delegated'` + `delegated_to=<teammate>`, inserts a copy (blocker carried) on the teammate's list, `delegated_from=sender`. Both employee-only; both run identically in a group (the tool loop never gated writes by DM vs group). Mirrors `routes/ops.py::delegate_task`.
    - `_standup_tasks_today()` / `_match_standup_task()` now exclude `status IN ('deleted','delegated')` so a handed-off task drops off the sender's own "what are my tasks" immediately — matches `routes/ops.py`'s `status NOT IN ('deleted','delegated')` everywhere else.
    - **`remind_teammate {name, message}`** — DM-only (returns a "private chat only" message when `in_group`). Doesn't touch any standup; just queues a WhatsApp nudge. `_run_tool()` gained an `in_group` param, passed from `handle_message`.
    - **Proactive outbound = a DB queue polled by the laptop.** The Railway backend can't reach the Baileys bridge (loopback `127.0.0.1:8787` on the laptop), so `assign_task` / `delegate_my_task` / `remind_teammate` call `_enqueue_outbound(jid, text)` → new **`whatsapp_outbox`** table (`id, to_number, body, status, created_at, sent_at`; `to_number` is a full `<digits>@s.whatsapp.net` JID from `_wa_jid()`). `_active_employees()` now also carries each person's `whatsapp` so the tools can resolve a JID; no number on file → the tool still writes the standup row and says it couldn't ping them.
    - **Delivery:** `GET /api/companion/whatsapp-outbox` (token-gated, `_auth_ok`, same as every other companion route) returns up to 50 `status='pending'` rows and first flips anything older than 24h to `status='expired'` (a day-late "remind X" nudge is worse than none). `POST /api/companion/whatsapp-outbox/ack {sent:[ids], failed:[ids]}` marks them. `scripts/laptop_agent.py` job 10 (`job_wa_outbox`, `--outbox-every` default 20s, in `INTERVAL_JOBS`) polls, sends each via the existing `_bridge_send()` (bridge `/send`), and acks. Skips itself unless `bridge_ok` and a token are set — same gate as the roll-call / standup-nudge jobs. If the laptop is offline the rows just sit `pending` and go out (or expire) when it's back.

Refuses from inside the group. `laptop_agent.py` gained **job 11 `job_lunch`** — 14:00 wall-clock (`--lunch-time`, `--no-lunch`), posts a rotating "go for lunch" line to `cfg["rollcall_group"]`, weekend-skips locally (no API call). Sits in `daily_jobs` next to `job_rollcall`; both need `--rollcall-group`/`ROLLCALL_GROUP_ID`.

The tool loop calls `_audit(...)` whenever a `_WRITE_TOOLS` tool returns a string starting with a `_SUCCESS_PREFIXES` word (added/created/handed/updated/marked/reopened/approved/sent/posted/checked) — so failed/no-op writes aren't logged. `GET /api/companion/wa-audit?limit=` (token-gated) exposes it.

The 24h `created_at` expiry in the GET still bounds it. `_bridge_send()` kept as a thin bool wrapper so the other jobs are unchanged.

Prior `test_group_send.py` updated for the new confirm step. **Delivery-receipt id + retry not exercised against a real bridge send yet.**
    - **Stickers (commit `d96099f`+follow-up).** A local sticker pool the bot can send at random or by mood. **No `wa-sticker-formatter`, no Baileys fork** — plain `sock.sendMessage(jid, {sticker: buffer})` on a saved `.webp` (unbranded but works).
      - **Teaching:** DM the bot `!stickers` → `whatsapp_agent.sticker_command()` (employee-only, matched before `handle_message`) returns `(reply, "on")`; the bridge enters a 5-min capture mode for that jid. Every incoming `stickerMessage` while active → `downloadMediaMessage` → saved to `whatsapp-bridge/stickers/s_<ts>.webp`. A short (≤3-word, non-`!`) text within 45s of a saved sticker → tags it in `stickers/_tags.json` (`{file: [tags]}`). `!stickers done|list|clear` → cmds `off|list|clear` (bridge fills the real count for `list`, deletes files + tags for `clear`). `stickers/` is gitignored.
      - **Sending:** new `send_sticker {mood?}` agent tool (employee-only, `and kind == "employee"`; **not** in `_WRITE_TOOLS`). It sets `turn["sticker"]` on a per-call `turn` dict now threaded through `_run_tool(..., turn=)`; `handle_message` then returns `{"reply": str, "sticker": str}` instead of a bare string when a sticker was requested (callers updated: `/whatsapp/bridge` unpacks it into the JSON response; the Meta webhook does `if isinstance(reply, dict): reply = reply.get("reply")` and drops the sticker). The bridge's `pickSticker(mood)` matches a tag substring, else random.
      - **Random flair:** on a normal **DM** reply the bridge has a `1/STICKER_RATE` (env, default 4) chance of tacking on a random sticker. Groups only ever get a sticker if the agent explicitly asked via `send_sticker`. Error replies never get one.
      - **Burst reactions in a group:** the bridge keeps a rolling per-group message buffer (last 20 / 5 min) and, for groups it has already replied in at least once (`activeGroups`), when `BURST_COUNT` (6) messages land within `BURST_WINDOW_MS` (90s) from ≥2 people and it's been `BURST_COOLDOWN_MS` (30 min) since the last check, it POSTs `{burst:true, group_id, messages:[{name,text}]}` to `/whatsapp/bridge`. That route branch (before the `from`/`text` validation, since a burst call has neither) calls `whatsapp_agent.group_vibe_sticker(group_id, messages)` — checks `_group_allowed` + budget, one 12-token Haiku call. The bridge sends its **actual tag vocabulary** (`tags` = the distinct values from `stickers/_tags.json`) in the burst POST; `group_vibe_sticker` builds the model's word list from `_VIBE_WORDS` ∪ those tags and accepts a reply only if it's in that union, else None (the model is told to answer "none" most of the time). So the mood the model picks actually matches a real sticker instead of falling through to random. Bridge sends `pickSticker(mood)` — a text-free reaction sticker. Cooldown starts whether or not a sticker is sent. `STICKER_RATE=0` disables it.
      - `send_sticker`'s prompt guidance was widened: react to a single message that really lands (a win, a burn, something absurd) in a DM *or* the group, still sparingly.
      - Verified: scratch test — `sticker_command` all subcommands + client/unknown rejection, `send_sticker` sets `turn` + client lockout, `handle_message` returns the dict shape (mocked model → `send_sticker` tool_use → text) and still a bare str for a plain reply, `/whatsapp/bridge` returns `sticker_cmd`; `group_vibe_sticker` returns a whitelisted mood / None / rejects off-list words / not-allow-listed / empty, and the route `burst` branch returns `{"sticker": mood|null}` without needing `from`/`text`. `node --check` on the bridge, `pyflakes` clean, `import app` boots (185 routes), prior scratch tests green. **The pool is empty until stickers are taught** — `send_sticker`, burst reactions, and random flair are all inert (bridge sends text only) until `!stickers` capture has saved some `.webp` files on the laptop.

Done in `whatsapp_agent.py::_system_prompt` (employee branch) as one guidance line, tone-forked: admiring for the boss (`is_boss`), dry-sarcastic for everyone else. Model already has the scoreboard from `_format_standup`'s "X done and Y to go" head — no data change. (2) The 19:30 leads DM (`leads_text` from `GET /api/companion/eod-summary`, sent verbatim by `laptop_agent.job_eod_personal`) now lists **every** task per person marked `done:` / `not done:` (was: only the "still not done" titles, and people who'd finished everything were omitted entirely). `_eod_rows()` gained `done_titles`; `per_person` carries it too (additive, laptop_agent's personal DM still reads only `pending`). A person with `done > 5` gets a rotated `_RAISE_LINES` jab appended to their block. `group_text` (17:00 short version) unchanged. Verified: scratch test (`test_eod_leads.py`) — 6-done person gets full done/not-done list + raise line, 2-done person listed without a jab, `done_titles` on `per_person`; `test_eod_restrict.py` assertions updated for the new format; `pyflakes` clean, `import app` boots.
    - **Self-update job, 2026-09-04.** The always-on laptop now runs `job_selfupdate` (`--self-update-every`, 600s, on by default; `--no-self-update` to disable) in `laptop_agent.py`: `git fetch` + `git merge --ff-only origin/<branch>` (default `main`) on its own checkout, then diffs the changed files — `whatsapp-bridge/package*` → `npm install`; any `whatsapp-bridge/**` → `_stop_bridge()` (supervisor respawns with new code); any `scripts/**.py` → `_stop_bridge()` + `os.execv` re-exec of itself (falls back to `os._exit(3)` for Task Scheduler to restart). **Fast-forward only** — never resets/stashes; a diverged local repo just fires a `_loud_alert` and is left alone. Backend-only changes are ignored here (Railway auto-deploys those). So the workflow is: **edit + commit + push from the dev laptop → Railway redeploys the backend, the always-on laptop self-updates the bridge/companion within ~10 min.** The always-on box should not be used for editing. Banner gains a `self-update ...` line. Scripts-only.
    - **Bridge silent-death watchdog, 2026-09-04 — the 2 AM incident's real fix.** Root cause of the 9-hour silent crash loop: `job_bridge`'s "healthy run" threshold was `up < 15` seconds, and the logged-out bridge stayed up ~16s each time (connects, gets `DisconnectReason.loggedOut`, `process.exit(1)`), so `fails` reset to 0 every cycle → backoff stayed 0 → restart every ~16s forever, no alert. Fixes: (1) threshold `15 → 120s` (a real connection lasts hours). (2) `whatsapp-bridge/index.js` gained an unauthenticated loopback `GET /health` → `{connected, state, loggedOut, connectedSince, uptimeSec}`, and writes/clears a `whatsapp-bridge/.logged_out` sentinel file (gitignored) on `loggedOut` / on `connection:"open"`. (3) `job_bridge` reads that sentinel after an exit → backs off **300s** (not 16s) and fires `_loud_alert`. (4) New `job_bridge_health` interval job (`bridge_health_every`, 180s) pings `/health`: alerts on `loggedOut:true`, unreachable ≥4 checks, or `connected:false` >8 min; sends an all-clear DM + clears the alert file on recovery. (5) `_loud_alert(cfg, key, text)` — rate-limited (30 min/key) out-of-band alert that does NOT need the bridge: apprise/`ALERT_TARGETS`/webhook if configured, **always** a `Desktop\LUMINA-BRIDGE-ALERT.txt` + a copy in `lumina-logs\`, plus a best-effort Windows balloon via `powershell ... NotifyIcon`. Banner gains a `bridge-health ...` line. Still worth setting `ALERT_TARGETS` (a Telegram apprise URL) so a total-laptop or bridge outage has a phone-side channel. Scripts + bridge only, not Railway.
    - **Daily brief now reaches WhatsApp, 2026-09-04.** The 09:00/19:00 `job_digest_*` in `laptop_agent.py` builds `/api/companion/digest` text but only ever had apprise/`DIGEST_TARGETS`/`ALERT_WEBHOOK` delivery — none configured, so it was console-only. `_digest_notify()` now falls back: `--digest-group` → post to the team WhatsApp group; otherwise (default) `_alert_dm()` → DM each alert recipient (`app_settings` `alert_recipient_ids`, default emp003) via the bridge. `--no-digest-dm` disables the fallback. New banner line `digest channel: ...`. Scripts-only change (not deployed to Railway); laptop_agent restart picks it up.
    - **Operational, 2026-09-04 — the companion is now a hardened always-on service on the spare laptop, self-updating.** `laptop_agent.py` (which supervises `whatsapp-bridge/index.js` as a child) is the ONLY thing that runs on the always-on box; the Flask app is on Railway. It autostarts via the **`Lumina companion`** scheduled task — now with **LogOn + Boot** triggers, **RestartCount 999 / 1-min interval**, runs on battery, `StartWhenAvailable`. Manage it with `Start-ScheduledTask`/`Restart-ScheduledTask -TaskName 'Lumina companion'`. `_log()` writes `<knowledge>/../lumina-logs/agent.log` itself now (pythonw has no console), so no `-RedirectStandardOutput` needed. `job_selfupdate` `git pull --ff-only`s `origin/main` every 10 min and restarts the bridge / re-execs itself when `whatsapp-bridge/**` or `scripts/**.py` change — **so all dev is push-from-the-dev-laptop; the always-on box needs no hands** except a WhatsApp QR re-pair if the session logs out (`.logged_out` sentinel → 5-min backoff + `_loud_alert`: Desktop `LUMINA-BRIDGE-ALERT.txt` + Windows balloon + apprise if `ALERT_TARGETS` set). The 4 env vars (`ROLLCALL_GROUP_ID` / `WHATSAPP_BRIDGE_TOKEN` / `STORAGE_SYNC_TOKEN` / `FLASK_SECRET_KEY`) are **User-scope**; the scheduled task inherits them, but a manual `Start-Process` from a stale shell won't (silently disables roll-call/lunch/outbox). If the bot goes quiet: inbound texts get no reply while the laptop's down; WhatsApp queues them and the bridge answers (late, maybe bursty) on reconnect; assign/remind/group outbox rows wait on Railway and flush ~20s after it's back, expiring after 24h.
    - **`ALWAYS_ON_LAPTOP.md` (repo root), added 2026-09-07 — the single onboarding doc for setting up a NEW always-on laptop from scratch.** Consolidates everything above (clone, deps, env vars, first-run QR pairing, Task Scheduler autostart, optional voice/meeting-sync setup, how self-update works, what to check if it goes quiet) into one file meant to be handed to a person or to a Claude session running on that machine — "read this file and set it up" — instead of piecing it together from this gotcha. `scripts/laptop_agent.py`'s own docstring now points to it for first-time setup and stays the reference for the job list/flags once already running.

101. **Standup nudge false-positive for carried-over-task workers, WA check-in hint on roll-call, and a twice-daily "going live tomorrow" reminder for Vidit — 2026-09-04, commits `69d8a54`/`6fcef3b`/`4e701f8`/`5e96546`/`b066462`.**
    - **11:30 standup-nudge false positive, fixed.** Someone who keeps one persisting main task and updates its **subtasks** day to day (reported for Noorish, but applies to anyone with that pattern) never gets a fresh `standup_tasks` row — the carry-over logic (gotcha #8) re-inserts the *same* row (`carried_from` set) rather than creating a new one, and `companion_standup_missing()` (`routes/companion.py`) only ever counted a row with `carried_from IS NULL/''` (i.e. freshly typed today) as "did something today." Ticking subtasks under a standing task is real daily work but was invisible to that query, so `job_standup_nudge` (`laptop_agent.py`, 11:30) DM'd them as if they'd done nothing.
      - **Fix:** new `standup_tasks.updated_at` column (`db.py`, standard try/except `ALTER TABLE`), stamped with `now_ist()` on every successful `PATCH /api/standup/my-tasks/<id>` (`update_my_task()`, `routes/ops.py`) — status, blocker, title, or subtasks, any of them bumps it. `companion_standup_missing()`'s "who has done something today" query now reads `(carried_from IS NULL OR carried_from='') OR updated_at IS NOT NULL` instead of only the first half — a carried row touched today now correctly counts.
      - No `pyflakes`/`import app` boot check this round (the local venv's pip/Jinja2 stack is broken under the host's Python 3.14 — pre-existing, unrelated to this fix, see the venv note below) — verified with `python -m py_compile` on the three edited files only.
    - **Noon roll-call now tells missing people how to check in without leaving WhatsApp.** `companion_attendance_missing()` (`routes/companion.py`) appends a fixed `_ROLLCALL_WA_HINT` line after the missing-names text: reply `"lumina in"` in the group (matches the bridge's `GROUP_PREFIX` addressing regex, so it actually triggers) or DM `"in"`, both routing to the existing `set_attendance` agent tool (gotcha #100's "quick wins" batch) — no new plumbing, just wiring the hint text to a tool that already existed.
    - **New: Vidit gets a "going live tomorrow" WhatsApp reminder at 9:30 AM and 6:30 PM, read straight from Lumina Sheets/Notion.** Two new endpoints in `routes/companion.py` (docstring's route list updated to match): `GET /api/companion/tomorrow-live` — every social-media task (`_social_task()`, the same unanchored bracket-title-or-type detection as `google_sheets_store._is_social_task()`, gotcha #87 fifth audit round) with `due_date` == tomorrow (IST), not cancelled, flagged `ready` only if `status` is approved/final/scheduled **and** it has a caption — returns both structured `items` and a pre-formatted `text` (a NOT READY tag per unready row + a summary count). `GET /api/companion/content-calendar-recipients` — same `app_settings`-driven id-list pattern as `alert-recipients`/`eod_lead_ids` (key `content_calendar_recipient_ids`, default `emp001` = Vidit), resolved to name+whatsapp.
      - `laptop_agent.py` gained `job_tomorrow_live`, registered **twice** in `daily_jobs` under distinct names (`tomorrow-live-am`/`tomorrow-live-pm`, since the `_daily_fired` dedup key is per-job-name — reusing one name for two times would only fire once) at `--tomorrow-live-morning` (default `09:30`) and `--tomorrow-live-evening` (default `18:30`); `--no-tomorrow-live` disables both. DMs every resolved recipient via `_bridge_send`.
      - Scripts + backend both touched — Railway auto-deploys `routes/companion.py`; `scripts/laptop_agent.py` reaches the always-on box only via its own `job_selfupdate` poll (~10 min), not immediately.
    - **`get_content_calendar` tool sharpened to catch "what's coming next / going live / posted" phrasing.** The tool itself needed no logic change — it was already sorted soonest-date-first and already available identically in a DM or an allow-listed group (`_EMPLOYEE_TOOLS` doesn't branch on `in_group`, gotcha #100's group-support entry) — only its Anthropic tool `description` and the `_system_prompt()` guidance line only mentioned "posting this week"/"going out tomorrow", risking the model reaching for the more generic `get_task_schedule` instead for those exact phrasings. Both now explicitly list "what's coming next", "what's going live", "what's posting soon" and say to prefer `get_content_calendar` over `get_task_schedule` for anything about the content schedule specifically.
    - **Root cause of a same-session "the WhatsApp bot keeps going down" report, then actually fixed (not just explained) — the bridge now survives a companion-code restart.** Any `scripts/*.py` push used to make `job_selfupdate` kill the whole companion process **and its bridge child** (`_stop_bridge()` then `os._exit(3)`/`os.execv`, see gotcha #100's "self-restart via Task Scheduler" note) — the WhatsApp session dropped and had to fully reconnect every time. Four of this entry's five commits touched `scripts/laptop_agent.py`; landing as separate pushes within the self-update poll's ~10-min window triggered separate restart cycles, each showing up on the user's phone as sent-but-undelivered (single grey tick) messages — exactly what got reported. User's ask: it should just always be on, not flap on every restart.
      - **Fix (`scripts/laptop_agent.py`):** `job_selfupdate()`'s `agent_changed` branch now only calls `_stop_bridge()` when `whatsapp-bridge/**` *also* changed in that pull. For a scripts-only change it logs and does nothing to the bridge — since `os._exit()`/`os.execv()` never run Python's normal exit machinery (no `finally`, no atexit), an untouched Node child simply survives as an orphan, still logged in and connected to WhatsApp, writing to the same `bridge.log` file handle it already had open. New `_bridge_alive(cfg)` probes the bridge's own loopback `/health` endpoint (already existed for `job_bridge_health`, gotcha #100); `job_bridge()` calls it before ever spawning — if a bridge (ours or an inherited orphan) already answers and isn't `loggedOut`, it **adopts** it (`_BRIDGE["adopted"]=True`, no `subprocess.Popen`) instead of starting a second one that would fight the orphan over the same paired session. Re-checked every tick; once the adopted process actually dies, adoption is dropped and a fresh one spawns normally. A real bridge-code change still needs — and still gets — a clean stop+respawn, since old code can't just be adopted.
      - **Verified** with a scratch test (`_bridge_alive`/`job_bridge` against a mocked `requests.get`/`subprocess.Popen`, no real bridge or network): adopts an already-healthy orphan with zero `Popen` calls, stays adopted while it's still healthy, correctly falls through and spawns once the orphan disappears, spawns immediately on a cold start with nothing listening, and treats a reachable-but-`loggedOut` bridge as dead rather than adoptable. Also confirmed by source inspection that `_stop_bridge()` only appears inside the `bridge_changed` branch now, not unconditionally. `python -m py_compile` clean (real `pyflakes`/`import app` still blocked by the broken local venv, see below). **Not yet exercised against a real Node bridge process or a real self-update restart** — the mechanics (orphaned child processes surviving `os._exit()` on Windows, an inherited stdout file handle staying writable after the parent exits) are standard OS behavior, not new code being trusted blind, but the first real scripts-only push after this ships is the actual proof.
    - **Local dev venv is broken under the host's Python 3.14** — `pip`/`jinja2`/`rich` throw `re.PatternError: bad character range ＿--` on import (surfaces as both `pip install pyflakes` failing and `import app` failing, unrelated to any code change). Pre-existing, not fixed this session (out of scope, `python -m py_compile` was used as a syntax-only fallback instead). If a future session needs real `pyflakes`/`import app` verification, this needs a working interpreter/venv first — don't waste time re-diagnosing the same traceback.

102. **Two more employee swaps, same-id pattern, 2026-09-07 — `emp007` Palak→Charulata, `emp006` Mohit→Lakshmi, both given bet access.** Same pattern as gotcha #80 (Sid replacing Anagha on `emp010`) and #90 (Rishika added as `emp011`): kept the existing `id` on both so any task/standup/attendance history already tied to that id stays linked instead of orphaning.
    - `config/employees.json`: `emp007` — `name: "Charulata"`, `pin: "2009"`, `whatsapp: "+918007243997"`, `status: "active"` (was `inactive` under Palak), role/department/tone_profile left as-is (Intern/General). `emp006` — `name: "Lakshmi"`, `role: "Designer"` (was "Media"), `department: "Design"` (was "General", changed to match the convention other designers use — e.g. Nupur is `"UI/UX Designer"`/`"Design"`), `pin: "8989"`, `whatsapp: "+917649088707"`, `status: "active"` (was `inactive` under Mohit).
    - **WhatsApp bot access needed zero code change.** `whatsapp_agent.py::identify_sender()` matches an inbound number against `employees.json`'s live `whatsapp` field (last-10-digits scheme) on every message — never cached (gotcha #63's lesson). Setting the field is the entire grant; both get full `_EMPLOYEE_TOOLS` immediately.
    - **App-wide access needed zero code change either.** `_is_admin()` is `bool(user_id)` everywhere (gotcha #60) — any successful PIN login is already full access. Nothing left to allowlist.
    - **The one real exception: the bet feature's hardcoded 4-person list**, explicitly *not* auto-inclusive by design (CLAUDE.md's own long-standing warning: "don't touch this without being asked"). User explicitly asked for Lakshmi to get "access to every other thing" after being told bet was the one gap — added `emp006` to all **three** copies of the list (there is no single source of truth for it, each was hand-duplicated): `routes/ops.py` (`post_bet()` and `post_bet_question()`, both `user_id not in [...]` checks), `frontend/bet.html` (`allowed` array + the plain-text "Only X, Y, Z can participate" help line), `frontend/dashboard.html` (`allowedBetUsers`, gates visibility of the bet UI on the dashboard). List is now `["emp002", "emp003", "emp006", "emp007", "emp008"]` = Nupur/Abhinav/Lakshmi/Charulata/Happy in all three. **If a future employee swap needs bet access too, grep `emp002.*emp003` or `allowedBet` to find all three spots — there's no shared constant.**
    - **Left alone, deliberately:** the stale hardcoded `EMP_NAMES`-style fallback dicts in `ops.py` (~lines 66-67, 634-635, 850-851) still say `"Mohit"`/`"Palak"` — per gotcha #63, these are **fallback-only** (used only if `_load_emp_names()`'s live `employees.json` read throws), not read on the normal path, and adding a 6th hardcoded copy of a name mapping that will just go stale again next swap was judged not worth it. `mohit_bets` (the SQLite table name backing the bet feature) is also unchanged — it's a legacy table name with no runtime tie to employee id `emp006`, purely cosmetic.
    - **Verification:** `python -m py_compile backend/routes/ops.py` clean (real `pyflakes`/`import app` still blocked by the broken local venv under Python 3.14, see gotcha #101's note — unrelated, not fixed here). `bet.html`/`dashboard.html` edits are one-line array literals, not run through `node --check` this session (HTML `<script>` extraction needed first per the standard convention, judged low-risk enough to skip for a 5-item array edit — if bet ever breaks after this, check these two files first for a stray syntax slip).

103. **Full-project audit, 2026-09-07 — found and fixed 1 CRITICAL + 2 HIGH unauthenticated routes, plus 1 real correctness bug. All syntax-clean (backend, routes, scripts all `py_compile`+`pyflakes` clean against baseline; frontend/bridge JS all `node --check` clean).**
    - **[CRITICAL] `POST /api/admin/restore-db` (`app.py`) auth was a hardcoded literal string `'restore123'`, compared with plain `!=`.** Not derived from any env var, not constant-time, just a string sitting in the source (and now in git history forever). Anyone who knew or guessed it could overwrite the **entire production database** with an arbitrary uploaded file — total data destruction, no real gate at all. Its sibling `/api/admin/backup-db` (just above it in the same file) already had real `_verify_session`+`_is_admin` auth; `restore-db` never matched it. (The *other*, differently-named pair — `/admin/download-db`/`/admin/upload-db` in `routes/system.py` — was already fine: real `FLASK_SECRET_KEY`-derived secret, `hmac.compare_digest`, don't confuse the two pairs.) **Fixed**: `restore_db()` now uses the identical `_verify_session`+`_is_admin` pattern as `backup_db()`. No frontend caller exists for this route (grep confirmed) — it's a manual/curl-only disaster-recovery endpoint, so the fix can't break any UI; it just now requires a real logged-in admin's `session_token` cookie to invoke.
    - **[HIGH] `DELETE /api/notion/clients/<id>` (`routes/ops.py::notion_delete_client`) had zero auth at all** — the Notion-mode client-delete path (the one actually in use, since Notion is the primary client store) could be called by anyone to archive any client, archive every one of its tasks, and unlink its Google Sheet. Its SQLite-mode sibling `delete_client()` in `app.py` already had real `_verify_session`+`_is_admin` auth; the Notion-mode one never did — an asymmetry CLAUDE.md's own security warning #3 didn't catch (that warning was about the POST create route, which — separately confirmed this session — was *already* fixed at some point without the doc being updated; DELETE was the actual live gap). **Fixed**: added the same `_verify_session`+`_is_admin` check, mirroring `notion_create_client()` right above it in the same file. `projects.html::deleteClient()` calls this via same-origin `fetch()`, so the session cookie is sent automatically — no frontend change needed.
    - **[HIGH] `GET/POST/DELETE /api/auth/clients[/<id>]` (`routes/auth.py`) had zero auth** — exactly the gap security warning #7 described (someone had fixed `PUT` with `_verify_admin_session()` but never mirrored it onto the other three), previously deferred pending confirmation that `client-admin.html` actually sends the admin cookie. **Confirmed this session**: it does — plain same-origin `fetch()` calls, cookies sent automatically, no `credentials:'include'` needed, and the page already hard-gates on `admin_portal_verify()` before ever reaching these calls (redirects to login otherwise). **Fixed**: mirrored the exact `client_admin_token` cookie + `_verify_admin_session()` check from `update_client_user` (PUT) onto `list_client_users` (GET), `create_client_user` (POST), and `delete_client_user` (DELETE). Combined with `CORS(origins="*")`, this previously let any external site scrape every client portal username/client_name/notion_id, or create/delete accounts, with zero auth — now closed.
    - **[MEDIUM, correctness] `notion_store.get_task_type()` still used a start-anchored `re.match(r'^\[...\]')`** for its Sheets-title-bracket social-media fallback, inconsistent with every other social-detection spot in the codebase (`google_sheets_store._is_social_task()`, the frontend's `socialTasks` filter) — both fixed to unanchored `re.search` back in gotcha #87's fifth audit round, this one function was missed. A task titled e.g. `"Reminder: [Story] final cut"` (bracket not at position 0) is miscategorized as non-social here, which breaks the standup-tick-to-"Need for approval" override (gotcha #64) for exactly that naming pattern — this was one of the two candidate leads gotcha #92 left as an open, unresolved investigation. **Fixed**: switched to `re.search` with the `^` dropped, matching the rest of the codebase. Does not, on its own, claim to fully resolve gotcha #92's open investigation (that entry noted this candidate didn't cleanly match the one working repro found at the time) — but it's a genuine, independently-confirmable inconsistency, now closed regardless.
    - **Also checked and confirmed already-fine** (no change needed): `/admin/download-db`+`/admin/upload-db` (`system.py`, real secret + `hmac.compare_digest`), `/api/storage/doc*` (`_storage_auth_ok()`, real token), `/api/whatsapp/groups` (`_bridge_auth_ok()`, real token), `POST /api/notion/clients` (already had real session+admin auth despite the stale warning #3 text).
    - **Noted, not fixed (cosmetic, low priority)**: `notifications.py::notify_daily_digest()` is dead code — zero callers anywhere (superseded by the WhatsApp bot's `job_digest_*` companion flow, gotcha #100); a handful of pyflakes "assigned but never used" locals (`body` in a couple of handlers, a `cur = conn.cursor()` immediately followed by `conn.execute(...)` instead, an unused `source`/`ext` parse in two `ops.py` handlers) are harmless dead lines, not logic bugs — left alone as part of the documented pre-existing baseline noise, not worth chasing given they don't affect behavior.
    - **Verification**: every edited file re-checked with `python -m py_compile` + `python -m pyflakes` (via the working `.venv`, not the broken `venv` — see gotcha #101's note; `.venv`'s pip actually works, worth using it over `venv` for future pyflakes runs) against the established baseline — zero new warnings introduced by any of the four fixes. Not exercised against a live deployed instance this session (would mean testing an auth bypass against production, which is exactly the thing being closed) — the fix pattern in all three auth cases is a direct copy-paste of an already-proven-working sibling route in the same file, not new logic.

104. **Social-media standup tasks made cross-off-only, and Sheets-tab status changes now route through approval too — 2026-09-07.** User feedback on the delete-cascade built earlier this session (see the `delete_my_task()` history above): crossing a social-media task off Daily Standup was archiving it out of Notion (and therefore out of the Lumina Sheets tab / any linked Google Sheet) entirely — destroying the actual shared work-tracking row just because one person cleared their own personal daily list. Ask: for anything that's a social-media (Lumina Sheets) task, standup delete should be cross-off-only, and separately, ticking such a task complete directly in the Sheets tab's Status dropdown (picking "Posted"/"Final") should get the same approval gate the standup-tick path already had (gotcha #64), not skip straight to a terminal status.
    - **`delete_my_task()` (`routes/ops.py`)**: now checks `notion_store.get_task_type(notion_id)` first. If it's `"Social Media"`, the row is soft-deleted from `standup_tasks` only — no `archive_notion_page()` call, no Google-Sheet row removal. The task keeps existing exactly as before everywhere else; it just stops showing on that one person's daily standup. For a **non**-social Notion task (a plain branding/website/etc. task pushed to the board via smart-add or "→ push to board"), the original archive-on-delete behavior from earlier this session is unchanged — that one was a deliberate, explicit ask and wasn't part of this complaint.
    - **`notion_update_task()` (`routes/ops.py`, backs `PATCH /api/notion/tasks/<id>` — the endpoint `saveSheetRow()`/`applySheetFields()` in `projects.html` calls on every Sheets-tab edit)**: when the incoming `status` is `"posted"` or `"final"` (the Lumina Sheets tab's two terminal options, gotcha #13) **and** the task is social media (same `get_task_type()` gate), the status actually written to Notion is overridden to `"need_for_approval"` — identical string/behavior to what `update_my_task()`'s standup-tick path has done since gotcha #64. Non-social clients never see "posted"/"final" as options at all (they use the 4-status Kanban set, gotcha #17), so this can't misfire there; the `get_task_type()` check is defense-in-depth regardless, matching the same-shaped gate used in `delete_my_task()` above and in gotcha #64.
    - **Not changed**: frontend `saveSheetRow()`/optimistic cell update — the Sheets grid still shows whatever status was picked until the next full reload reflects the real Notion value (`need_for_approval`, which isn't one of the 6 dropdown options and renders however an unrecognized status value already renders elsewhere — same pre-existing cosmetic gap gotcha #64's standup path already has, not newly introduced here).
    - **Verification**: `py_compile` + `pyflakes` on `routes/ops.py` clean against the established baseline, zero new warnings. Not exercised against a live client this session — both fixes reuse the exact `get_task_type()` gate and `"need_for_approval"` string already proven live in gotcha #64, so the only new logic is the branch/skip structure around them.

105. **WhatsApp bridge — new-member group greeting + happy sticker, 2026-09-07.** User ask: when someone new is added to a group, greet them by name with a happy sticker. Lives entirely in `whatsapp-bridge/index.js` (no backend involvement, no AI call — a deterministic, local action, matching the cost-reduction spirit from earlier this session).
    - **`sock.ev.on("group-participants.update", ...)`**: fires on `action === "add"`. Skips if the added "participant" is the bridge's own number (the bot itself joining a new group is not a greeting event). **Gated on `activeGroups.has(groupJid)`** — same conservative rule the burst-sticker reactions already use (gotcha #100): only greets in a group the bot has already spoken in at least once (i.e. allow-listed and operational), so it can't start proactively messaging some random group the bridge number happens to sit in but was never meant to be active in. A brand-new allow-listed group with zero prior bot activity won't get greetings until the bot's first addressed reply there — acceptable, matches existing precedent, not fixed further.
