    - **Name via WhatsApp's native `@mention`**, not name lookup: `sock.sendMessage(groupJid, {text, mentions:[participantJid]})` — WhatsApp renders the mention as whatever name the *recipient's own contact list* has for that number, so it's always as accurate as it can be without the bridge needing to resolve identity itself (and works even for a group member who isn't a Lumina employee at all). The mentioned jid in `mentions[]` is the raw jid WhatsApp gave the event; the **visible** `@digits` text is separately resolved through the same lid→phone-number fallback (`sock.signalRepository?.lidMapping?.getPNForLID`) already used for inbound DM/group identity (gotcha #100's LID fix) — needed because a raw `@lid` jid's digits aren't the person's real phone number, so the literal `@digits` substring in the message body would otherwise not match what a mention needs to render correctly.
    - **Sticker**: `pickSticker("happy")` right after the greeting — reuses the existing mood-tag matching logic (substring match against `stickers/_tags.json`). **Only actually sends a "happy" one if that tag exists in the taught pool** — if nothing's tagged "happy" yet, `pickSticker`'s existing fallback returns a random sticker from the whole pool instead (same graceful-degrade `pickSticker` already does everywhere else), and if the pool is empty entirely, the sticker step is silently skipped and only the text greeting goes out. **If "always happy" isn't landing, check `!stickers list` first** — the fix is tagging at least one taught sticker "happy", not a code change.
    - **Debounced** 5 minutes per `(group, participant)` pair (`recentGreets` Map) in case Baileys ever re-emits the same participant-update on a reconnect — short enough that a genuine future re-join (leaves, comes back days later) still gets greeted again.
    - **Verification**: `node --check` clean. Not exercised against a real WhatsApp group-join event this session (needs someone to actually add a person to a live group to trigger `group-participants.update`) — if a join doesn't produce a greeting, check first whether that group is in `activeGroups` (the bot needs to have replied to something in it at least once since the bridge process last started) before assuming the event handler itself is broken.

106. **WhatsApp agent `create_task` tool claimed success even when the standup row was never actually written — real, confirmed silent-failure bug, 2026-09-07.** User asked the bot (as Abhinav) to add a task ("bot features"); it replied confirming creation, but the task never appeared in Daily Standup's Today's Task List, and a later "what are my tasks now" still listed it as a 3rd item anyway. Verified live against production (`GET /api/standup/my-tasks?user_id=emp003`) that only 2 real rows existed for that day — the task genuinely was never created, confirming this wasn't a display bug.
    - **Root cause**: `create_task` (`whatsapp_agent.py`, `_run_tool`) wraps its `standup_tasks` INSERT in a try/except that only `logger.exception()`s on failure — unlike its siblings `add_standup_task` and `assign_task`, which correctly `return` an honest failure message from inside the `except` block, `create_task`'s except block just logs and falls through, and the function continues on to unconditionally build and return `"Created: " + title...` regardless of whether the insert actually succeeded. Same silent-success-on-failure class already fixed repeatedly elsewhere in this codebase for Notion-writing UI flows (gotcha #53) — first confirmed instance of it in the WhatsApp agent's own write tools.
    - **Fix**: added a `standup_ok` flag, set only after the INSERT genuinely completes without raising. If it's `False`, return an honest message instead of falling through — distinguishing "the Notion page was created but the standup row failed" (if `nid` is set) from "nothing happened at all."
    - **Second, defense-in-depth fix**: the model's follow-up "what are my tasks now" answer *also* listed the phantom task, even though a fresh `get_my_tasks` call would only ever return real DB rows — meaning the model was blending in something it remembered saying earlier in the conversation rather than trusting the tool's actual return value for that turn. Added an explicit line to `_system_prompt()`: "When you list someone's tasks, list ONLY what the tool call just returned this turn — never add something from earlier in this chat... that the fresh tool result doesn't actually contain." Mitigates the general failure mode, not just this one bug.
    - **`add_standup_task`/`assign_task` audited in the same pass and confirmed already correct** — both `return` immediately with a real failure message inside their except blocks; `create_task` was the sole outlier.
    - **Not done**: the actual "bot features" task was not recreated on the user's behalf — its intended client/due-date/details aren't known, safer to have them re-ask via WhatsApp now that the underlying bug is fixed than guess and create something wrong.
    - **Verification**: `py_compile` + `pyflakes` on `whatsapp_agent.py` clean, zero warnings at all (not just zero *new* ones). Root cause confirmed via a live, read-only production API call (not guessed) before writing the fix, per systematic-debugging discipline. Not re-exercised against a live WhatsApp message this session — the fix mirrors the exact, already-proven-correct pattern from `add_standup_task`/`assign_task` in the same file.

107. **WhatsApp agent — new `schedule_group_message` tool, and the outbox's expiry logic fixed to support it — 2026-09-07.** User ask: any employee DM'ing the bot to post something in the team group should work (confirmed already true — `send_group_message`, gotcha #100, has no per-person restriction, `kind == "employee"` only), and separately, scheduling a message for later should also be possible. This entry is the new scheduling half.
    - **`whatsapp_outbox` gained a `send_after` column** (`db.py`, standard try/except `ALTER TABLE`, nullable IST `"YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM[:SS]"`). `wa_outbox.enqueue()` takes an optional `send_after` kwarg — `None` (the default) is the exact old behavior, deliver on the next poll.
    - **`GET /api/companion/whatsapp-outbox` (`routes/companion.py`) now only returns a scheduled row once its time has passed** (`send_after IS NULL OR send_after <= now`). **Real bug caught and fixed before shipping, not after**: the pre-existing 24h expiry logic compared every row's `created_at` (UTC) against a flat 24h-ago cutoff, with no regard for `send_after` — a message scheduled more than 24h out (e.g. "next Monday") would've been silently expired before its own scheduled time ever arrived, since it just sits `pending` from the moment it's queued. Split into two separate expiry passes: an immediate row (`send_after IS NULL`) still expires 24h after *creation*, unchanged; a scheduled row instead expires 24h after *its own scheduled time* (compared in IST, matching the column's own timezone — mixing UTC `created_at` and IST `send_after` in one comparison would have been wrong). Verified with a scratch test against a temp DB (never `logs/app.db`): an immediate message returns right away, a 2h-future scheduled one is correctly withheld, a 5-min-past-due one is correctly returned, and a message 26h past its *own* schedule is correctly expired rather than returned.
    - **New tool `schedule_group_message {message, when}`** (`whatsapp_agent.py`) — same confirm-before-send UX as `send_group_message` (gotcha #100), just with a time attached. The model is told to work out the real `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM` IST value itself (same "turn 'Friday'/'tomorrow' into a real date" convention already used by `create_task`); the tool itself re-validates the format and rejects anything already in the past.
    - **`_set_pending_broadcast`/`_pop_pending_broadcast` payload changed from a bare string to `{"message", "send_after"}` JSON** so one pending-confirmation slot per employee covers both an immediate and a scheduled request — a stray old plain-string row (there can't be one in practice, rows expire in 10 minutes, but handled anyway) falls back to `{"message": <the string>, "send_after": None}`. The shared "reply yes/no" handler in `handle_message()` branches on whether `send_after` is set: present → `wa_outbox.enqueue(gjid, msg, send_after=...)` and confirms with the scheduled time; absent → the exact old immediate-send path, unchanged. Verified with a scratch test: schedule-then-pop round-trips the dict correctly, a second pop after the first returns `None` (single-use, matches the existing DELETE-on-pop pattern), and a plain immediate set/pop round-trips with `send_after: None`.
    - **`schedule_group_message` added to `_WRITE_TOOLS`** (audit-log gating, same set `send_group_message` is already in) and to the `list_capabilities` tool's private-chat description and the `_system_prompt()` line explaining the confirm-before-broadcast pattern.
    - **No `scripts/laptop_agent.py` change needed** — `job_wa_outbox` already just polls the GET endpoint and delivers whatever comes back; all the new timing logic lives server-side, so the poller's own code is untouched.
    - **Verification**: `py_compile` + `pyflakes` clean on all four edited files (`whatsapp_agent.py`, `wa_outbox.py`, `db.py`, `routes/companion.py`), zero new warnings. Two scratch tests (outbox time-filtering, pending-broadcast round-trip) run against temp SQLite DBs, both passing as described above. Not exercised against a real scheduled WhatsApp send this session (would need the laptop's bridge running and a real wait for the scheduled time to arrive) — the delivery half is the same already-proven `job_wa_outbox`/`_bridge_send` path every other outbox message already uses, only the query that decides *when* a row becomes visible to it is new.

108. **Daily-standup lock — the web app gates itself until today's standup has something on it, 2026-09-07.** User ask, brainstormed to a bounded design before building (approach + scope confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` first: unlock condition = any row for today including auto-carried-over ones; applies to everyone with no role exceptions; WhatsApp stays untouched — the existing 11:30 nudge from gotcha #101 already covers "proactively ask whoever's pending"; skips weekends, matching the velocity chart's own weekend exclusion from gotcha #77).
    - **New `GET /api/standup/lock-status?user_id=` (`routes/ops.py`)**: weekday check first (`datetime.now(IST).weekday() >= 5` → `{"locked": False, "weekend": True}`, short-circuits before touching the DB); otherwise `{"locked": not has_tasks, "weekend": False}` where `has_tasks` is `COUNT(*) FROM standup_tasks WHERE user_id=? AND date=today AND status NOT IN ('deleted','delegated') > 0`. Deliberately does **not** trigger the auto-carry-over insert `get_my_tasks` does — that only needs to happen once someone is actually on the Standup screen, and this route is called from every *other* page first.
    - **`frontend/auth.js`** (loaded on every protected employee page, gotcha's own Key Patterns #1) calls this right after the existing session-verify succeeds, before revealing the page (`document.documentElement.style.visibility`). Skips the check entirely on `standup.html` itself (via `location.pathname`). On `locked:true`, redirects to `standup.html?locked=1` instead of revealing the current page. **Fails open on any network error** — the whole check is wrapped in try/catch that just logs and falls through to revealing the page, so a lock-status blip can never trap someone out of the app entirely (matches the same fail-open philosophy `authGuard()`'s own catch block already uses for a verify() failure).
    - **`standup.html`** gained a small banner (`#lock-banner`, hidden by default) shown when `?locked=1` is in the URL: "Add at least one task below to unlock the rest of Lumina for today."
    - **Nice emergent property, not separately coded**: `get_my_tasks`'s pre-existing auto-carry-over (gotcha #8) means anyone who had a pending task *yesterday* unlocks the instant they land on `standup.html` — no typing required. Only someone starting genuinely fresh (first day, or fully cleared yesterday) needs to actually add something. Confirmed by the live spot-check below: some employees showed `locked:false` immediately with no action taken this session.
    - **Explicitly NOT an API-level block** — every other endpoint (attendance check-in, WhatsApp, task APIs, everything) keeps working regardless of lock state. This is a frontend workflow nudge, not a security boundary; said explicitly to the user before building so it can't be mistaken for one later.
    - **Not touched**: client portal pages (`client-auth.js`, separate guard entirely), `login.html`, admin-portal auth — none of them include `auth.js`, so none of them are affected.
    - **Verification**: `py_compile` + `pyflakes` on `routes/ops.py` clean (established baseline only), `node --check` clean on `standup.html`'s extracted script block. **Live-verified against real production data post-deploy**: `curl .../api/standup/lock-status?user_id=<id>` across all 10 active employee ids returned a genuine mix of `locked:true`/`locked:false` matching who actually had a task listed for today — not a mocked/hypothetical check.

109. **Standup-lock follow-up batch, 2026-09-07 — "do all" on 5 suggested next steps: 3 real fixes, 1 audit (clean, nothing to fix), 1 honest non-fix.**
    - **Fix 1 — security warnings #6/#8, both now closed.** See gotcha's own commit `5e80af1`: `POST /api/auth/login` locks a `user_id` for 15 min after 5 wrong PINs (new `login_lockout` table, `db.py`) plus a 20/min rate limit; `POST /api/auth/change_pin` now requires the caller's own session to match the `user_id` being changed (previously any session, or none, plus a correct `old_pin` was enough — meaning knowing someone else's old PIN while logged in as yourself was sufficient to change theirs); `GET /api/attendance/logs`/`/export` now require a real `session_token` cookie resolving to an admin instead of trusting an unauthenticated `user_id` query string (`bool(user_id)` on a query param meant any non-empty string passed). `/export`'s only frontend caller is a same-origin `<a href>` in `dashboard.html`, so cookies still attach automatically; `/logs` and `change_pin` have zero frontend callers currently, zero UI risk either way. Verified: scratch test of the lockout counter against a temp DB (4 fails → not locked, 5th → locked ~15min, clear → reset), `py_compile`/`pyflakes` clean.
    - **Fix 2 — standup-lock visibility on the Team Standups panel itself (not a new dashboard KPI — `dashboard.html`'s `.kpi-card`/`.kpi-grid` CSS classes turned out to be dead/unused in current markup, not worth fighting to hook into for this).** `standup.html`'s `loadTeamStandups()` already loads every employee's today-task-count via the existing `/api/standup/today` batch call (the same data that already renders "hasn't started today's standup yet" per row) — added a one-line summary above the list, `#lock-summary`, e.g. "Locked out until standup: Lakshmi, Charulata" — shown only when viewing today's own date (not a historical date) on a weekday, hidden otherwise. Reuses data already in memory, no new backend call. Verified: `node --check` clean on the extracted script block.
    - **Fix 3 skipped — "tag a happy sticker" is a user action** (`!stickers` teach flow), not something committable in code. Left as a to-do for the user, not attempted.
    - **Audit 4 — every `_WRITE_TOOLS` member in `whatsapp_agent.py` re-checked for the exact false-success bug fixed in `create_task` (gotcha #106).** `update_standup_task`, `delegate_my_task`, `remind_teammate`, `set_task_meta`, `set_attendance`, `review_task` all correctly gate their success return on the actual write result (either an exception from inside a `try` that `return`s immediately from the `except`, or an explicit boolean check like `review_task`'s `if not ok: return "..."`) — the primary DB write always gates the response; a secondary best-effort Notion mirror is correctly logged at `debug` level and never treated as the success signal. `create_task` was confirmed the sole outlier; nothing else needed changing. `set_attendance`'s underlying `_attendance_checkin()`/`_attendance_checkout()` (`routes/attendance.py`) were also traced end-to-end and confirmed to have no internal exception-swallowing that could hide a failure from the caller's `try/except`.
    - **Item 5 — gotcha #92's unresolved "task vanishes from Sheets when marked done via standup" investigation: still NOT root-caused, not claiming otherwise.** The regex fix already shipped in gotcha #103 (`get_task_type()`'s bracket-detection un-anchored) closes one plausible contributing bug, but per systematic-debugging discipline this session did not fabricate a fix without a fresh repro — none was available. Did run one bounded, honest diagnostic instead: a live read-only query against production Notion for gotcha #92's other named lead (a blank Client ID making a task invisible to any per-client Sheets view, same pattern as gotcha #79). Found exactly **2 real orphaned rows** — both titled `"[Story] a"` (looks like test/junk data, not real client work), status `in_progress`, blank Client ID, notion ids `375cacb2-5c03-81a7-95b8-d01e9574fcd7` / `...-8120-a290-f87748c44f01`. **Not touched** — they aren't currently reproducing the reported "vanishes when marked done" symptom (they're `in_progress`, not `done`), and gotcha #79 already established that fixing a blank-Client-ID orphan requires a human confirming which real client it belongs to before writing anything, not an automatic guess. **If this bug is reported again, get the specific task name from the user first**, then check both of these possibilities (title-bracket position, blank Client ID) against that exact task before touching anything else.

110. **Mon/Wed/Fri 3pm team-meeting reminder — 2026-09-07.** User ask, corrected mid-flow from an initial "7-8pm" to the real time "4-5pm" before implementing. `scripts/laptop_agent.py` gained `job_team_meeting(cfg)` — same shape as the existing `job_lunch` (gotcha #100): gated on `cfg["rollcall_group"]` (the team WhatsApp group), fires at 15:00 (`--meeting-time`, `--no-meeting`), only on Mon/Wed/Fri (`datetime.now().weekday() in (0,2,4)`, checked inside the job since `_daily_due()` isn't day-aware — matches `job_weekly_wrap`'s Friday-only pattern), posts a fixed text ("Reminder: team meeting today from 4 to 5 PM.") via `_bridge_send`. Wired into `daily_jobs` alongside `job_lunch`, banner line added. **Scripts-only** — reaches the always-on laptop via its own `job_selfupdate` git-pull poll (~10 min), not a Railway deploy. Verified: `py_compile` clean; not exercised against a real send this session (needs the always-on box to pull + a real Mon/Wed/Fri 15:00 to fire).

111. **WhatsApp-bot cost pass — 2026-09-07: one real fix (prompt caching), two audited findings of "nothing to change."** User asked for 3 items from a researched cost-reduction list; all 3 investigated against the real code before touching anything, not assumed.
    - **[Fixed] `whatsapp_agent.py`'s main tool-loop had no prompt caching, unlike `app.py`'s chat stream (which already caches its system prompt, lines ~727/~2130).** The loop at ~line 2222 calls `_client.messages.create()` up to `_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS` (10) times per inbound message, and every round was recomputing `_system_prompt(identity, in_group, group_name)` — a genuinely large, per-identity-but-otherwise-static block (persona, tone rules, full tool-usage guidance) — and sending it as a plain string, so Anthropic could never cache it: no `cache_control`, and a fresh string object each round anyway. This is the highest-volume Haiku caller in the app (every WhatsApp message, every tool round). **Fix:** `_system_prompt(...)` is now built **once** before the loop into `sys_prompt = [{"type": "text", "text": ..., "cache_control": {"type": "ephemeral"}}]` and that same list object is reused for every round — guarantees a byte-identical prefix within one message's tool loop (near-certain cache hit from round 2 onward) and, since the string only varies by `(identity, in_group, group_name, today)`, also hits across a person's next message within the ~5min ephemeral TTL if they're chatting actively. **Deliberately left uncached:** the *other* `_client.messages.create()` call in this file (`group_vibe_sticker()`, ~line 1149, the burst-reaction mood classifier) — its system string embeds a dynamic `vocab` list and the call itself is gated by a 30-minute per-group cooldown, which is longer than the default 5-minute ephemeral TTL, so caching it would only add the 25% write premium with no realistic chance of a read hit. Also deliberately **not** done: caching the `tools` array (also resent unchanged every round, also cacheable via a `cache_control` on the last tool def) — out of the scope the user actually approved (system-prompt caching only); flagged here as a natural, low-risk follow-up if wanted. Verified: `py_compile` and `.venv`'s `pyflakes` both clean on `whatsapp_agent.py` (zero warnings, not just zero new ones). Not exercised against a live message this session — the change only alters how the `system` param is shaped, not the loop's control flow or any tool logic.
    - **[Audited, nothing to fix] "Batch API for non-realtime jobs" doesn't apply anywhere in this codebase today.** Grepped `scripts/laptop_agent.py`, `scripts/research_feed.py`, and `routes/companion.py`'s digest/weekly-wrap builders for any Anthropic client call — none exists. The daily digest, weekly wrap, and content-calendar reminders are all pure DB-query text assembly (`_build_digest()` etc. in `routes/companion.py`), no LLM call to batch. `research_feed.py` uses `trafilatura`/stdlib HTML parsing only, also no LLM. There is currently no deferred/non-realtime Claude call anywhere in the always-on-laptop job set to move to the Batch API — this recommendation was based on a plausible-sounding pattern (cron jobs = batchable), not on what the code actually does. If a future job adds a real LLM call that doesn't need a same-second reply, that's the candidate for Batch API, not anything that exists right now.
    - **[Audited, nothing to fix] Model-tier audit of the WhatsApp/standup paths — everything already runs on Haiku, confirmed by reading the code, not assumed from `model_router.TASK_ROUTING`'s `"whatsapp": "haiku"` entry alone.** `whatsapp_agent.py`'s both `_client.messages.create()` call sites use `get_model_for_task("whatsapp")` (grepped both, `whatsapp_agent.py:1147` and `:2210`). Every standup-AI route in `routes/ops.py` (smart-add, auto-fill, ai-coach, carry-over, etc.) goes through the file's own `_claude_call()` helper (line ~40), which hardcodes `os.getenv("HAIKU_MODEL", "claude-haiku-4-5")` directly — never touches `model_router.py` at all, and never Sonnet. **Noted but out of scope for this task:** `_claude_call()`'s fallback default (`"claude-haiku-4-5"`) doesn't match `model_router.py`'s own fallback default (`"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001"`) — harmless today since `HAIKU_MODEL` is set in the real environment, but the two hardcoded defaults could drift apart if that env var is ever unset. Not touched this session (wasn't part of the 3 approved items); worth a one-line fix (`os.getenv("HAIKU_MODEL", "claude-haiku-4-5-20251001")`) if anyone's in that file for another reason.

112. **Semantic memory layer (`backend/smart_memory.py`) — 2026-09-07, a "Mem0-style" upgrade built WITHOUT adopting Mem0 itself.** User asked to research + build a Mem0-style memory upgrade "completely free" — researched official Mem0 first (Apache-2.0, genuinely free software, but self-hosting needs its own Postgres+pgvector+Neo4j+FastAPI server stack — real hosting/ops weight, and a real departure from this app's zero-external-server pattern, same tradeoff `semantic_kb.py` already made in favor of plain numpy over a vector-db server). Presented that tradeoff via `AskUserQuestion`; user chose the lightweight in-process option. Result: same two things Mem0 is actually valued for (semantic recall instead of flat-append, light dedup) built directly on top of the *existing* `memory_store.py`, reusing `semantic_kb.py`'s already-loaded embedder — zero new infra, zero new API cost, zero new Python dependency.
    - **New table `memory_vectors`** (`db.py`): `(user_id, memory_id) -> (dim, vec)`. One row per memory item (unlike `kb_chunk_vectors`, `memory.data` is one JSON blob per user, so items need their own rows here to each get a vector).
    - **`semantic_kb.py` gained one public function, `embed_texts()`** — a thin public alias of the existing `_embed()` — so `smart_memory.py` shares the same loaded model2vec instance instead of loading a second copy. `semantic_kb.py`'s own behavior/API is otherwise completely unchanged.
    - **`smart_memory.py`** mirrors `semantic_kb.py`'s exact contract: `available()`/`is_enabled()`/`set_enabled()`/`_active()`, off by default (`app_settings` key `memory_smart_enabled`, env override `MEMORY_SMART_ENABLED`), every function either improves on `memory_store.py`'s existing behavior or silently falls back to it unchanged (inactive, or any embedding failure) — never worse, never required.
      - `format_for_prompt(user_id, query)` — when active and a query is given, ranks by cosine similarity to that query instead of just returning the last 20 memories in storage order; with no query or inactive, delegates straight to `memory_store.format_for_prompt()` unchanged.
      - `dedupe_or_save(user_id, content, source)` — before saving, checks cosine similarity against the user's existing memories; skips (returns the existing near-duplicate) instead of appending a duplicate above `_DUP_THRESHOLD`. **Real bug caught by testing, not assumed correct:** the threshold was first written as `0.90` on general ML intuition — a scratch test against the *real* embedder showed a genuine near-duplicate pair ("Prefers concise email replies, no fluff" vs "Likes short, no-fluff email responses") scores **0.77**, while two genuinely distinct facts scored **~0.08–0.10**. `model2vec`/potion-base-8M's cosine range runs much lower than a typical sentence-transformer's — 0.90 would never have deduped anything. Recalibrated to `0.70` (real margin below the measured near-dup score, miles above the distinct-fact noise floor), re-tested, confirmed correct.
      - `index_memory()`/`index_memories()`/`remove_memory()`/`prune()` — keep `memory_vectors` in sync with `memory_store.py`'s own CRUD (including the silent oldest-item drop when `MAX_PER_USER=50` is exceeded, which `add_memory()` already did before this feature existed and still does).
      - `start_backfill()`/`_backfill_worker()` — embed every memory that predates this feature. **Second real bug caught by testing:** the first cut copied `semantic_kb.py`'s exact `start_backfill()`/`_backfill_worker()` shape, which has a real race — `start_backfill()` spawns a background thread and returns a *local* dict claiming `running: True` without touching the actual module-level `_backfill` global; the global only flips to `running: True` once the spawned thread itself gets scheduled and reassigns it. A caller that polls `_backfill`/`stats()` immediately after `start_backfill()` returns can see the stale pre-start `running: False` and think the backfill already finished before it ever began. A synchronous scratch-test poll loop hit this deterministically (a real network round-trip, as `semantic_kb.py`'s own HTTP-based usage always has, happens to paper over it with enough scheduling slack — so this exact bug may be **latently present in `semantic_kb.py` too**, not touched here since that file wasn't part of this task, flagging in case a future tight-polling caller of *that* backfill ever hits it). Fixed in `smart_memory.py` by having `start_backfill()` set `running/done/total/error` on the existing dict **synchronously, before** spawning the thread, and having the worker mutate those same fields in place rather than rebinding the whole dict.
    - **Wired into `app.py`** (all four `memory_store` call sites now guarded-import `smart_memory` and fall back to the old behavior on any import/runtime failure): the manual-add endpoint (`POST /api/memory/<user_id>`) now calls `dedupe_or_save`; the delete endpoint also calls `remove_memory`; the auto-extraction path (the existing `<SAVE_MEMORY_PROFILE>` tag parser, already zero-extra-cost since it reuses the same model response) now indexes whatever `memory_store.update_profile()` just added — which required giving `update_profile()` a real return value (the list of new profile entries) instead of `None`, a safe additive change since nothing previously used its return; and the system-prompt builder now calls `smart_memory.format_for_prompt(user_id, query=message)` instead of the flat version.
    - **Wired into `whatsapp_agent.py`**, which had **zero** persistent-memory integration before this (grepped, confirmed) — its only continuity was the existing 6-hour rolling chat context (gotcha #98), nothing durable across days. Employees only (never clients, matching this file's established scoping pattern everywhere else):
      - **Recall**: the tool-loop's cached system-prompt block (gotcha #111's fix) is followed by a **second, deliberately uncached** block carrying `smart_memory.format_for_prompt(identity["id"], query=text)` — kept separate on purpose so a per-message-varying memory tail doesn't invalidate the big static block's cross-message cache hit that #111 just bought; the recall block itself is cheap even uncached (a handful of short lines, not the whole prompt).
      - **Extraction**: the model can end a reply with `<REMEMBER>fact</REMEMBER>`, stripped before the WhatsApp reply is sent, saved via `dedupe_or_save`. Exact same zero-extra-cost trick as `<SAVE_MEMORY_PROFILE>` in `app.py` — reuses the response already being generated, no separate extraction call (deliberately not built as a dedicated LLM call, which would add real per-message cost right after gotcha #111's cost-cutting pass in the same session).
    - **New endpoints** (`routes/system.py`, mirroring `/api/kb/semantic` exactly): `GET/POST /api/memory/smart` (status + toggle, POST kicks a backfill), `POST /api/memory/smart/backfill`.
    - **Verification**: `py_compile` + `pyflakes` clean on every touched file (zero new warnings vs. the established baseline), `import app` boots clean (192 routes, confirms the `memory_vectors` migration runs against the real `logs/app.db`). **Real scratch-test suite run against a temp DB with the actual model2vec embedder** (not mocked) — dedup skip/no-skip, semantic recall ranking correctly for two different queries, `format_for_prompt`'s with-query-vs-without-query branching, the inactive-fallback behaving identically to plain `add_memory`, vector cleanup on delete, and the full backfill path — all passing after the two real bugs above were found and fixed by the test itself, not by inspection. **Not yet exercised against a live WhatsApp message or the real chat UI** — the `<REMEMBER>` tag's actual round-trip (model emits it, gets stripped, gets saved) hasn't been triggered by a real inbound message this session.

113. **One-way WhatsApp voice announcement calls — 2026-09-07, built end-to-end. Live-verified against a real call on 2026-09-15 — see the UPDATE note near the end of this gotcha before assuming it's still unverified.** Same research+build request as gotcha #112, same "completely free" constraint. Researched real PSTN calling (Twilio/Plivo/Vonage/Telnyx/SIP trunks) first — confirmed **no free ongoing PSTN calling exists anywhere**: every provider charges per-minute (Twilio ~$0.014/min US) with only a one-time trial credit, not a permanent free tier. Presented that finding plus a genuinely-free alternative via `AskUserQuestion`: outbound voice **calls over WhatsApp itself** (not PSTN — rides the existing bridge number, same free mechanism as calling a contact from the WhatsApp app) via the open-source `baileys-caller` library. User chose the WhatsApp-call route. A second `AskUserQuestion` scoped it down from a full two-way conversational voice agent (realtime STT+TTS+turn-taking over an obscure single-maintainer call library — real effort, real risk of being flaky) to **one-way announcement calls only** (ring, speak one message via local TTS, hang up — no listening back at all) — user chose the simpler scope, which is also the one that needed no STT at all.
    - **`whatsapp-bridge/voice.js`** (new file): `placeAnnouncementCall(toDigits, text)` — synthesizes `text` to a temp WAV via **Piper** (local neural TTS, MIT, CPU, spawned as a subprocess, needs a separately-installed binary + one `.onnx` voice model — not bundled, see the file's own header for setup), then places the call via `baileys-caller`'s `VoipClient` (`client.call(digits, {audioSource: wavPath, durationMs})`), waits for it to end or hit a safety timeout, cleans up the temp file. Every failure mode (Node too old, `baileys-caller` not installed/built, Piper missing, the call itself failing) is caught and returns `{ok:false, error}` — never throws, never crashes the bridge process.
    - **Deliberately lazy, deliberately optional, by design — the base text bridge must keep working with zero voice setup at all.** `baileys-caller` is added to `package.json` under `optionalDependencies` (not `dependencies`) — if it fails to install/build (real possibility: not on npm, needs `npm run build`, needs Node ≥20 vs. the base bridge's ≥18, likely needs native/WASM build tooling), `npm install` for the rest of the bridge still succeeds. `voice.js` itself is only `import()`-ed lazily inside the new `/call` route handler in `index.js`, never at module load — so an unconfigured/broken voice setup can never prevent `index.js` from starting and running the text bridge normally. `index.js`'s existing `/send` route and its auth-gate logic were refactored (same behavior, restructured to also gate the new `/call` route) — re-verified with `node --check` and by re-reading the auth branch to confirm `/send`'s existing 401/400/503 semantics are byte-identical to before.
    - **New `/call` loopback route** (`index.js`, same `Bearer TOKEN` auth as `/send`): `{to, message}` → `placeAnnouncementCall()` → the raw `{ok, error?}` result, logged and returned.
    - **New table `wa_call_outbox`** (`db.py`) + **`wa_outbox.enqueue_call()`** — separate queue from the existing `whatsapp_outbox` (text messages), since a call needs the laptop's bridge to run TTS+call-placement, not a plain `/send` POST. No `send_after` scheduling (unlike the message outbox) — every queued call is "place it now," there's no scheduled-call feature.
    - **New companion routes** (`routes/companion.py`, same token-gate as every other `/api/companion/*` route): `GET /api/companion/wa-call-outbox` (24h stale-expiry, same shape as the message outbox's), `POST .../ack` (retry-then-give-up, capped at 2 attempts instead of the message outbox's 3 — a failed *call* is expensive to blindly retry).
    - **New agent tool `place_announcement_call {name, message}`** (`whatsapp_agent.py`) — employee-only, DM-only (mirrors `remind_teammate`'s exact shape: `_resolve_employee` → `_active_employees` fallback list → `wa_outbox.enqueue_call`), added to `_WRITE_TOOLS` for audit-log coverage, mentioned in `list_capabilities` and the system prompt with an explicit "use rarely, a phone actually ringing is a bigger deal than a text" instruction so the model doesn't reach for it casually.
    - **New `job_voice_calls`** (`scripts/laptop_agent.py`, `--voice-calls-every`, default 30s, registered in `INTERVAL_JOBS`) — polls the outbox, POSTs to the bridge's `/call` (90s timeout — TTS + ringing + playback can genuinely take a while, unlike a text send), acks sent/failed.
    - **Verification, and its real limits — read this before trusting the feature works:**
      - `node --check` clean on `index.js`/`voice.js`, `py_compile`+`pyflakes` clean on every touched Python file (zero new warnings), `import app` boots clean (192 routes total, includes both this and gotcha #112's new endpoints), `package.json` parses.
      - **The outbox/routes/agent-tool plumbing (queue → companion API → agent tool → audit log) is real-tested**: a Flask-test-client scratch test against a temp DB exercised enqueue, the auth gate (401 without a token), the pending-list GET, and the ack/retry/give-up cycle end to end — all passed on the first real run.
      - **`voice.js`'s actual call-placement logic (Piper subprocess → `baileys-caller` → a real WhatsApp call ringing a real phone) has NOT been exercised at all.** No Piper binary/model, no `baileys-caller` install/build, and no paired `voice-auth` session exist in this environment — there was nothing to test it against. `baileys-caller` itself is a small, single-maintainer, not-on-npm library wrapping WhatsApp's VoIP WASM stack, documented via a fetched README summary rather than hands-on use — real risk it doesn't behave exactly as documented (auth flow, audio format acceptance, WASM build success on Windows) once actually tried.
      - **To actually turn this on**: install ffmpeg (PATH) + Node ≥20 on the always-on laptop, `npm install` in `whatsapp-bridge/` (watch whether `baileys-caller` built successfully — it may need a manual `npm run build` inside `node_modules/baileys-caller`), install Piper + download a voice model, set `PIPER_BIN`/`PIPER_VOICE_MODEL` env vars, then trigger a real `place_announcement_call` — the very first attempt will also need a second QR-code pairing scan (`baileys-caller` manages its own auth session, separate from the text bridge's) before it can place anything. **If it fails, the error message returned all the way back to the WhatsApp conversation is the real diagnostic** — every layer (Piper spawn, `baileys-caller` import, the call itself) reports a specific reason rather than a generic failure.
      - `whatsapp-bridge/voice-auth/` added to `.gitignore` (mirrors the existing `whatsapp-bridge/auth/` entry for the text bridge's own session).
    - **UPDATE 2026-09-15 — LIVE-VERIFIED with a real call. This feature is no longer "genuinely unverified"; the caveats above are historical.** The always-on laptop turned out to already have Piper + the voice model + `baileys-caller` installed from a prior session, so this session finished the job: paired `voice-auth/` (one-time QR scan via a new helper script, see below), then hit a real, reproducible failure — `Error: Could not resolve LID for <number>@s.whatsapp.net` on every call attempt, regardless of how long the session was left to sync.
      - **Root cause, confirmed by reading source, not by guessing:** `baileys-caller`'s `resolveLid()` reads `sock.signalRepository.lidMapping?.getLIDForPN(...)` — an API that plainly does not exist on `@whiskeysockets/baileys@6.7.x` (the version the text bridge runs, confirmed by grepping the installed package's source — zero matches for `lidMapping` anywhere in it). `baileys-caller`'s own `package.json` declares it actually needs `@whiskeysockets/baileys@^7.0.0-rc11`. Waiting for "sync" was never going to fix this — it's a hard version incompatibility, not a timing issue.
      - **Fix, after explicitly confirming with the user the risk of touching the shared dependency** (presented 3 options via `AskUserQuestion` — pause voice calling / isolate it with its own copy of the library / upgrade the whole bridge; user chose to upgrade): bumped `whatsapp-bridge/package.json`'s `@whiskeysockets/baileys` from `^6.7.9` to `^7.0.0-rc11` (resolved `7.0.0-rc14`), `npm install`. This is a **shared dependency with the production text bridge** — before trusting it, verified in order: (1) `node --check` on `index.js`/`voice.js` still clean, (2) the text bridge's existing `auth/` session reconnected successfully after the upgrade with **no re-pairing needed** (`{"connected":true,"loggedOut":false}`), (3) sent a real WhatsApp text through it and got a real message id back, confirming normal messaging is unaffected, **before** touching the voice-call test at all.
      - **Then the actual call worked**: `POST /call` returned `{"ok":true}`, `bridge.log` showed `call -> <number>: placed` with zero errors, and the recipient confirmed on their real phone that a call actually came through. First genuine end-to-end proof this feature works, not just that its code is plausible.
      - **New file `whatsapp-bridge/pair-voice.mjs`** — a small one-off script to do baileys-caller's own QR pairing (separate from the text bridge's pairing, even though it's the same phone number — a distinct linked-device slot with its own auth store). `baileys-caller` prints its QR directly to stdout as a side effect of `connect()` (no event to listen for) — run this in a **visible** console window (`Start-Process cmd -ArgumentList "/k","node pair-voice.mjs"` from `whatsapp-bridge/`) so a human can actually see and scan it with the dedicated number's phone. Re-run it if voice calling ever starts failing with a logged-out-style error.
      - **Residual known limitation, unchanged from the original build:** `baileys-caller` is still a small, single-maintainer, not-on-npm, pre-release-dependent library — this proves the *happy path* works on this exact setup, not that it's rock solid under all conditions (poor network, WhatsApp-side rate limiting on VoIP calls, concurrent calls, etc.). Treat a future call failure as plausible, and check the bridge log's specific error first before assuming the whole feature broke.
    - **UPDATE, same day, hours later — the very next real call after the one above silently died, and every one after it, until root-caused and fixed.** The user asked the bot (over WhatsApp chat) to call two more people; both attempts logged `call -> <number>: failed (A call is already active.)`, retried once more per the outbox's retry cap, then silently gave up (the pending-list GET correctly stopped returning them once `attempts` hit the cap — see the outbox's own retry-then-expire design above — so there was no stuck-forever queue, just two quietly-dropped requests with no visible error to the person who asked).
      - **Root cause, found by reading `baileys-caller`'s actual source, not by guessing:** `VoipClient`'s `call()` sets a private `#activeCall` field the moment a call is placed, and throws `"A call is already active."` on the next `call()` while it's still set — but **nothing in the library ever clears it back to `null` when a call naturally ends**, only `VoipClient.disconnect()` does. Since `voice.js`'s `ensureVoipClient()` reused one lazy singleton client across every call, the very first real call permanently poisoned the singleton — every call after it, forever, failed with that exact error regardless of how much time had passed. This is a genuine defect in `baileys-caller` itself (not fixable upstream from here, it's not on npm and has one maintainer) but fully worked around on our side.
      - **Fix, `whatsapp-bridge/voice.js`:** `placeAnnouncementCall()` now fully `disconnect()`s and nulls out the singleton in a `finally` block after every call, success or failure — forcing `ensureVoipClient()` to build a completely fresh `VoipClient` (with a clean `#activeCall`) on the next call. Costs a re-connect per call (a couple seconds, reusing the already-paired `voice-auth` credentials, no QR needed) — acceptable since this feature is meant to be used rarely, not for back-to-back calling. Also added a `callChain` promise-chain lock so two calls can never run concurrently in this process even if something upstream ever fires `/call` twice at once — two simultaneous `connect()`s on the same `voice-auth` session would risk the exact multi-device session-fork problem already fixed for the text bridge (gotcha #118).
      - **Verified live, not just reasoned about:** restarted the bridge to load the fix (Node's ES module cache meant the already-running process couldn't pick up the edit without a restart — confirmed the text bridge's own session survived the restart with no re-pair needed, same check as every prior bridge restart in this file), then placed **two calls back-to-back** — the exact scenario that previously failed on the second attempt — both returned `{"ok":true}` and both logged `call -> ...: placed` distinctly. Then manually placed the two originally-requested-but-silently-dropped calls (they'd already exhausted their retry cap and fallen out of the pending queue, so they needed to be resent by hand rather than self-healing) — both succeeded.
      - **If a call silently doesn't happen again:** check `bridge.log` for the specific error first (this exact one is now fixed, but the underlying library is still young) — and remember the outbox gives up after 2 attempts with no visible error to the requester, so "I asked and nothing happened" doesn't necessarily mean it's still trying.

114. **Self-hosted error monitoring (`backend/error_monitoring.py`) — 2026-09-07, real gap given this codebase's own bug history.** User asked to research + build error monitoring "free" after seeing this same CLAUDE.md's own long tail of "silently 500'd, nobody knew until a person complained" bugs (gotchas #41/#44/#56/#57/#93 and more — the recurring loop-local/branch-local variable-scope class alone). Researched Sentry cloud's free tier (5,000 errors/month, zero infra) vs. self-hosting GlitchTip (Sentry-SDK-protocol-compatible, Apache-2.0, but needs its own Postgres+Docker stack, ~256-512MB RAM) via `AskUserQuestion`; user chose self-host.
    - **`backend/error_monitoring.py`** (new): `init_error_monitoring()` — no-op unless `SENTRY_DSN` is set, guarded import of `sentry_sdk` so a server that hasn't added the dependency yet keeps running completely normally (same off-by-default/guarded-import contract as `semantic_kb.py`/`smart_memory.py`). `traces_sample_rate=0.0` deliberately — this is for exception visibility, not perf tracing, and keeping trace volume at zero also stays clear of any event-volume cap regardless of which backend (Sentry cloud or self-hosted GlitchTip) the DSN points at.
    - **Wired into `app.py`**: called right after `load_dotenv()`, before the Flask app is even constructed, so Sentry's Flask integration hooks every request from the first one.
    - **New `backend/requirements-monitoring.txt`** (one line, `sentry-sdk[flask]>=2.0.0`) — deliberately NOT added to the root `requirements.txt` yet, matching the exact rollout convention `semantic_kb.py`'s dependency used (gotcha #100): ship the guarded code first with zero deploy risk, add the real dependency to `requirements.txt` as its own separate, deliberate, watched deploy once there's an actual `SENTRY_DSN` to point at. Adding it now, before a GlitchTip/Sentry instance even exists to receive anything, would be a real deploy-risk step for zero present benefit.
    - **`deploy/glitchtip/`** (new): a production-oriented `docker-compose.yml` (official `glitchtip/glitchtip` image — no build step — Postgres 16 + Valkey + web + worker), `.env.example`, and a `README.md`. **The README's most important point, surfaced loudly rather than buried**: Lumina's Flask backend runs on Railway (the cloud), not the always-on laptop — a self-hosted GlitchTip on the laptop's bare LAN address would be **unreachable from Railway** (private IP behind home NAT), so `SENTRY_DSN` would silently receive nothing. The README lays out the two real options (run GlitchTip as a second Railway service, or put the laptop instance behind a free Cloudflare Tunnel/ngrok) and flags falling back to Sentry's own free cloud tier as the simplest fix if neither is worth the extra infra. This is exactly the kind of topology mismatch that's easy to miss when "self-host it" sounds simple in the abstract — worth catching before, not after, someone sets it up and wonders why nothing ever shows up.
    - **Honest gaps in the compose file**: written directly against GlitchTip's official Docker Hub image and documented install requirements (fetched live), but the web service's exact startup script name (`./bin/run-migrate-and-runserver.sh`) was confirmed via a community fork's compose file, not a hands-on run against the current official image — flagged in-file with the exact recovery step (`docker run --rm glitchtip/glitchtip:latest ls bin/`) if it's wrong. No Docker exists in this environment to actually run it.
    - **Verification, the parts that COULD be verified were, for real, not just assumed**: `python -m py_compile` + `pyflakes` clean (zero new warnings), `docker-compose.yml` YAML-parse-validated (caught and fixed one real bug this way — an unquoted `${VAR:?message}` default value containing a literal colon broke the YAML scanner; fixed by quoting every environment value and simplifying the error messages). **`import app` boots clean in three real configurations**, not just the trivial default one: (1) no `SENTRY_DSN` set — 192 routes, unaffected; (2) `SENTRY_DSN` set but `sentry-sdk` genuinely not installed — confirmed the guarded-import warning fires and the app still boots at 192 routes; (3) `sentry-sdk[flask]` actually `pip install`-ed into the venv, `SENTRY_DSN` set to a fake-but-well-formed DSN — confirmed `sentry_sdk.init()` genuinely runs and `sentry_sdk.get_client().is_active()` returns `True`, then the test dependency was uninstalled again to leave the venv exactly as it was. This is real proof the active path works, not just that the inactive path fails safely.

115. **Meeting notes → real tasks, rebuilt from scratch (`POST /api/ai/meeting-to-tasks`) — 2026-09-07, and a deliberately different design from what gotcha #46 removed.** User asked for a bot that joins Google Meet, listens, and converts the meeting into tasks. Researched live before building: as of March 2026, Google actively flags meeting bots in the waiting room ("potential risks" label) with no reliable auto-admit — a human has to manually click admit every single meeting or the bot times out — and Vexa (the real open-source "bot joins the call" project, Apache-2.0, self-hosted, Docker+Whisper) would also be a genuinely heavy addition competing with the WhatsApp bridge already running on the always-on laptop. Presented that tradeoff via `AskUserQuestion` against a friction-free alternative (a tool that runs on the ATTENDING laptop and listens to system audio — no bot, nothing to admit, no Google flag) — user chose the friction-free option.
    - **Split into two pieces on purpose, matching this codebase's established "don't guess a third-party tool's schema" lesson.** The actual audio capture + transcription is left to an existing, maintained, free/local tool the user runs themselves during a call (**meeting-scribe** or **TalkTrack**, both MIT, both Whisper-based, both produce a markdown export among their output formats) — this repo does not attempt to reimplement transcription. What was built here is the ingestion half: turning that tool's plain-text/markdown output into real Lumina tasks.
    - **`POST /api/ai/meeting-to-tasks`** (`routes/ops.py`, admin-gated via the standard `_is_admin(user_id)` convention): takes `{notes, meeting_title, client, user_id}`, makes **one** Haiku call (`_claude_call()`, the same helper every other `/api/ai/*` route in this file already uses) asking for a JSON array of `{title, owner, due_date}` action items. **Deliberately does NOT try to parse meeting-scribe/TalkTrack's own JSON export format** — their exact field-level schema was never confirmed hands-on (their READMEs describe the output formats but not concrete field names), and this codebase has been burned before by guessing at a schema instead of verifying it (the whole "guessed a schema, it was wrong, silently dropped data" gotcha family). A prose summary is universally parseable by an LLM regardless of which tool produced it; guessed JSON keys are not.
    - **Reuses `whatsapp_agent.py`'s already-proven `_find_client()`/`_resolve_employee()`** for owner/client fuzzy-matching instead of writing a third copy of that logic (ops.py now does `from whatsapp_agent import _find_client, _resolve_employee` inside the route — no circular import, `whatsapp_agent.py` doesn't import anything from `routes.ops`). An unresolved owner stays unassigned (matches gotcha #10's "tasks start Unassigned" convention) and the resulting `standup_tasks` row falls back to the uploader (`user_id`) rather than being silently dropped, so a mis-transcribed name still produces a real, findable task instead of one that vanishes.
    - **Per-item try/except, honest partial-success reporting** — mirrors gotcha #93's explicit lesson ("never treat a bulk-create loop's per-item failure as safe to swallow, always collect and report it"): the response always carries real `tasks_created`/`tasks_failed`/`failed_items` counts, never a blanket `success: true` papering over a partial batch failure. Strips a `​```json ... ```` markdown-fence wrapper before parsing, since models reliably add one even when told not to.
    - **`scripts/meeting_sync.py`** (new): the laptop-side half — watches a folder for new `.md` exports (whatever the capture tool wrote there) and POSTs each one to the endpoint above, same `--dir`/`--watch`/`--interval` shape as the existing `kb_sync.py`. Tracks processed files by filename only (not content hash, unlike `kb_sync.py`) in a `.meeting_sync_state.json` manifest — deliberately never re-submits an edited file, since re-processing an edited transcript would create a *second* batch of duplicate tasks rather than updating the first, which is worse than just not re-syncing; delete a line from the state file to force a one-off re-run.
    - **Verification — this is the most thoroughly live-tested feature built this session.** `py_compile`+`pyflakes` clean on both files (zero new warnings), `import app` boots clean (193 routes). **The endpoint's full logic was proven with a Flask-test-client scratch suite** against a temp DB with mocked `_claude_call`/`notion_store` (extraction → owner/client resolution → task+standup creation → honest per-item reporting → the auth gate → the empty-extraction case → markdown-fence stripping, all passing on the first real run). **Then, separately, `meeting_sync.py` itself was proven against a genuinely live HTTP server** — a real `werkzeug` server bound to a real port, the real `requests` library making real POSTs from `meeting_sync.py`'s actual `sync_once()` function, a real `.md` file on disk, confirming the whole path end to end including the dedup-by-filename behavior on a second pass. The one piece that could NOT be verified in this environment is the capture tools themselves (meeting-scribe/TalkTrack) — neither is installed here, so their actual markdown output format is taken on faith from their published READMEs, not hands-on. If a real meeting export doesn't produce sensible tasks, check first whether the tool's actual `.md` output looks like readable prose (it should) before assuming the extraction endpoint itself is wrong.
    - **A join-bot alternative (Vexa, self-hosted, joins via a link you send it) was proposed and explicitly declined, 2026-09-07.** Same manual-admit friction as the Google research above applies to Vexa too (it's still a bot Google's waiting room flags) — user was walked through both options side by side (what each requires, how each works day to day, output quality) and chose to stick with the system-audio approach already built. Not revisited unless asked again.

116. **WhatsApp bridge — proactive sends could land in a dead reconnect window and never decrypt ("Waiting for this message. This may take a while." forever on the recipient's side) — fixed 2026-09-08.** User reported this happening intermittently; root-caused via code reading, not guessing (`whatsapp-bridge/index.js`'s `/send` route only ever checked `!currentSock` — a variable set the instant a fresh socket object is constructed, long before Baileys' `connection.update` ever reports `"open"`, and even after `"open"` fires the encryption session needs a few more seconds to finish syncing). A proactive send (`job_wa_outbox`, the morning digest at its default `09:00`, roll-call, EOD, etc. — all fire-and-forget POSTs to `/send` from `scripts/laptop_agent.py`, no awareness of the bridge's connection age) landing in that window gets accepted by Baileys but never decrypts for the recipient — the exact "clock icon, Learn more" stub, permanently, while later messages sent once the session has settled deliver normally (matches the reported screenshot: one stuck 09:00 message, a normal exchange afterward). The bridge restarts often enough for this to be a real recurring window, not a one-off — self-update polls `origin/main` every ~10 min and can restart the bridge child on a `whatsapp-bridge/**` change, and the health watchdog restarts it on a crash/logout.
    - **Fix, `whatsapp-bridge/index.js` only:** added `SEND_SETTLE_MS = 5000` and `socketReady()` (`connState === "open"` **and** at least 5s since `connectedSince`). `/send` now checks it right after the existing `!currentSock` check and returns `503` with a clear reason instead of attempting the send. Deliberately **not** applied to the live-reply send path (`messages.upsert`'s `sock.sendMessage(jid, {text: reply})`) or `greetNewMember`/sticker sends — both only ever fire in response to a message WhatsApp just successfully delivered *to* the bridge, which by definition means the session is already live and working bidirectionally; gating those risks silently dropping a real-time reply for a race that can't actually occur on that path. Also deliberately not applied to `/call` (`voice.js`) — that's a separate `baileys-caller` VoIP session with its own independent pairing (gotcha #113), unrelated to this messaging-session race.
    - **Why no in-bridge wait/retry was added instead of a flat 503:** every real caller of `/send` (the companion's `whatsapp_outbox`/`wa_call_outbox` polling jobs) already has its own retry-with-backoff on a non-2xx response (gotcha #100's outbox `ack` endpoint, up to 3 attempts before giving up) — a 503 during the settle window is picked up and retried on the next poll (~20s later) with zero new logic needed in the bridge itself.
    - **Verification:** `node --check` clean. Not exercised against a real reconnect race this session (would need to force a bridge restart at the exact moment a scheduled job fires) — the fix directly closes the code-level gap the screenshot's symptom maps to exactly, but hasn't been watched catching a live recurrence yet. If the stuck-message symptom recurs after this ships, check the bridge's own log around that timestamp for a reconnect in the preceding ~5s before assuming this fix didn't work.

117. **Tue/Thu 10:28 class-meeting reminder, tagging every member of the team WhatsApp group — 2026-09-08.** Same shape as gotcha's own `job_lunch`/`job_team_meeting` (a fixed day/time nudge to `--rollcall-group`), but this one needed a real group-wide @-mention, which those two didn't — added that as a general bridge capability, not a one-off.
    - **`whatsapp-bridge/index.js`'s `/send` route** gained an optional `mentionAll` body flag. When set and `to` is a group jid, it fetches `sock.groupMetadata(to)`, resolves every participant (excluding the bridge itself) through the same lid→phone-number fallback `greetNewMember` already uses (gotcha #105), prepends a line of `@<number>` tags to the message text (WhatsApp only renders a highlighted tag where that literal text appears — the `mentions[]` jid array alone isn't enough), and sends with both. Falls back to sending untagged (logged, not fatal) if group-metadata lookup fails for any reason.
    - **`scripts/laptop_agent.py`**: `_bridge_send`/`_bridge_send_msg` gained a `mention_all` passthrough param; new `job_class_meeting()` (Tue/Thu only via `datetime.now().weekday() in (1,3)`, matching `job_team_meeting`'s day-gate pattern) posts `"Meeting time -- join here: https://meet.google.com/rek-jyvv-jmm"` with `mention_all=True`. Wired into `daily_jobs` behind `--class-meeting-time` (default `10:28`) / `--no-class-meeting`, gated on `cfg["rollcall_group"]` same as its siblings. Banner and docstring job list updated.
    - **The Meet link is hardcoded** (`_CLASS_MEETING_LINK`), same convention as `job_team_meeting`'s hardcoded "4 to 5 PM" text — not a flag, since it's a one-off fixed recurring meeting, not a general-purpose feature. If the link ever changes, edit that one constant.
    - **Verification:** `python -m py_compile` clean on `laptop_agent.py`, `node --check` clean on `index.js`. Not exercised against a real group send this session (needs the always-on laptop's bridge + a real group) — reaches the laptop via `job_selfupdate`'s next pull (~10 min after push) for the scripts change, and a bridge-child restart (since `whatsapp-bridge/**` also changed) to pick up the new `/send` behavior.

118. **Every bot WhatsApp message showing "Waiting for this message. This may take a while." on recipients' phones — Signal sessions forked by overlapping bridge restarts during a self-update, fixed live 2026-09-09.** Reported right after a dev-laptop push. The always-on laptop's `job_selfupdate` had pulled two consecutive `whatsapp-bridge/**` commits (`3f1569d`, `5e688c5`); each fired `_stop_bridge()` + a `job_bridge` respawn, and the old `node` process didn't fully die before the replacement connected — so two bridge instances briefly held the **same** WhatsApp identity (`connection replaced by another instance` in `bridge.log`). That forks the libsignal double-ratchet for every contact messaged in the overlap window, and a forked ratchet **does not self-heal** once the connection restabilises on a single instance: outbound messages stay undecryptable on the recipient side (the "Waiting for this message" stub, permanently), inbound throws `Bad MAC` / `Failed to decrypt message with any known session` / `Decrypted message with closed session`.
    - **Fingerprint** (all present this time): `bridge.log` full of `Bad MAC` + `Closing session: SessionEntry {…}` dumps (234 hits over ~2 days, spiking on each batch send); `whatsapp-bridge/auth/` piling up many `session-<jid>.<N>.json` files with mtimes clustered exactly on the 09:00 digest / 10:30 attendance-nag / 11:30 standup-nudge batch sends (82 session files, several *per contact* with sequential device-index suffixes `.45`/`.46`/`.47` = the bridge re-creating a session, failing, re-creating); `creds.json` recently rewritten. **`.logged_out` sentinel absent** — this is NOT a logout, `creds.json` + identity are intact, so **no QR re-pair is needed** (distinct from gotcha #100's logged-out crashloop).
    - **Fix (no re-pair), the same shape as the earlier one-contact Abhinav mitigation but applied to all contacts:** `schtasks /end /tn "Lumina companion"` → kill the bridge `node` (match `index.js` in its command line; do **not** kill the unrelated Adobe Creative Cloud `node.exe`) → **delete every `whatsapp-bridge/auth/session-*.json`**, keeping `creds.json`, `pre-key-*`, `sender-key-*`, `app-state-*` → `schtasks /run /tn "Lumina companion"`. Back up `auth/` first regardless. Each contact re-handshakes cleanly (fresh prekey fetch) on the bot's next message to them. Verified: bridge reconnected `state:open` with no re-pair, a test DM to Abhinav rebuilt `session-919702908716.{0,22}.json` from scratch with **zero Bad MAC**, and he confirmed the message arrived readable.
    - **Messages already sent during the broken window stay stuck as "Waiting" on recipients' phones forever** — the wipe only fixes messages sent *after* it; anything that mattered has to be resent.
    - **Group `sender-key-*.json` are a separate encryption path** — left intact here since only DMs were reported broken. If group messages also show "Waiting", wipe those too (same restart sequence).
    - **Prevention (dev-laptop work, NOT done from the always-on box):** `job_selfupdate` should coalesce multiple pending `whatsapp-bridge/**` commits into a *single* bridge restart instead of one per pulled commit, and `_stop_bridge()` should poll-confirm the child PID is actually gone (with a `Stop-Process -Force`/SIGKILL fallback) before `job_bridge` is allowed to spawn *or adopt* a replacement. Gotcha #100's `de0bb5c` adoption logic prevents a duplicate spawn in steady state but not during a deliberate stop-then-respawn on a bridge-code change, which is exactly the window that forked the sessions here.

119. **Daily-standup lock reworked: now needs check-in + a standup task, exempts leave, and drives a recurring "you haven't logged in" nudge (personal every 5 min + group twice) — 2026-09-09.** Extends gotcha #108's lock. All syntax-clean, scratch-tested, `import app` boots (194 routes); not yet exercised against a live WhatsApp message or a real 5-min nudge cycle.
    - **`GET /api/standup/lock-status` (`routes/ops.py`)** now returns `locked` only when the person, on a weekday, has BOTH an attendance check-in for today (`daily_attendance.checkin_time`) AND ≥1 non-deleted/delegated `standup_tasks` row for today. Response gained `on_leave`, `needs_checkin`, `needs_standup`. Anyone with a `leave_store` window covering today short-circuits to `{locked: False, on_leave: True}`. Weekends still short-circuit first. **Still a frontend redirect only** (`auth.js` → `standup.html?locked=1&need=checkin,standup`), not an API block — every endpoint and the WhatsApp bot work regardless. `standup.html`'s banner tailors its text off the `need` param.
    - **New `backend/leave_store.py`** (imports only `db`, safe to import from routes and the agent): `set_leave(user_id, start, end, reason, created_by)`, `clear_leave(user_id, on_date=None)`, `is_on_leave`, `on_leave_ids(date)`, `active_leave`. New `employee_leave` table (inclusive `[start_date, end_date]` windows, id/user_id/start/end/reason/created_by/created_at) created in `db.init_db()` (and defensively by `leave_store` itself). `set_leave` **deletes any overlapping window first** — "on leave today" after "on leave all week" narrows it rather than stacking rows.
    - **Leave is set via the WhatsApp bot only** (no admin UI — the chosen option). New employee-only tools in `whatsapp_agent.py`: `set_leave {start_date, end_date, person?, reason?}` and `clear_leave {person?}` — the model resolves "today"/"till Friday"/"Thu and Fri" to real `YYYY-MM-DD` itself (same pattern as `create_task`/`schedule_group_message`). Both added to `_WRITE_TOOLS`; `_SUCCESS_PREFIXES` gained `"cleared"`. A client calling either gets the standard "not available to you". `person` lets a lead set/clear it for a teammate.
    - **New `GET /api/companion/not-logged-in` (`routes/companion.py`, token-gated)** — active employees with a WhatsApp number who, on a weekday and NOT on leave, are missing check-in and/or a standup task; each entry carries `needs_checkin`/`needs_standup`. Same "did something on today's standup" rule as `standup-missing` (a fresh row, or a carried row PATCHed today via `updated_at`).
    - **`scripts/laptop_agent.py` — two new jobs, and three old ones demoted:**
      - **Sequence: group first, then personal.** The team-group not-logged-in list posts at **10:35** (and a follow-up **11:30**); the personal DMs only start at **10:40** so the group heads-up lands first. Adjusted from the initial 09:30/10:00 defaults per user ask 2026-09-09.
      - `job_login_nudge` — **interval** job (appended to `INTERVAL_JOBS` after its def, since that list literal is built earlier in the file), `--login-nudge-every` default **1200s (20 min)** — see the 2026-09-11 follow-up below, was 300s (5 min) at ship time — only fires on a weekday within `--login-nudge-start`..`--login-nudge-end` (default **10:40–19:00** local = IST). DMs each not-logged-in person a message tailored to what's missing (no check-in / no standup / both), tells them they can reply "on leave" to stop. `--no-login-nudge` disables.
      - `job_login_group_ping` — **daily** job registered once per time in `--login-group-times` (default `10:35,11:30`), posts the not-logged-in list (with per-person `no check-in`/`no standup` tags) to `cfg["rollcall_group"]`.
      - `job_standup_nudge` (11:30), `job_attendance_nag` (10:30) and `job_rollcall` (noon) are **superseded** and now only register when `--legacy-nudges` is passed. Their functions/flags stay for that opt-in. Banner shows a `login-nudge` line and a `legacy-nudges on/off` line; the `rollcall` slot in the top banner line now reads `OFF (legacy; use --legacy-nudges)`.
    - **Delivery:** backend (`ops.py`, `companion.py`, `whatsapp_agent.py`, `db.py`, `leave_store.py`) rides Railway auto-deploy; `scripts/laptop_agent.py` + `frontend/` reach the always-on laptop via `job_selfupdate` (~10 min) then a scripts-only self-restart — **bridge untouched, so no session-fork risk** (unlike gotcha #118).
    - **Verification:** `py_compile` all six files; `pyflakes` clean except the known pre-existing `ops.py` baseline noise; `import app` boots (194 routes, `not-logged-in` registered); `employee_leave` migrates against the real `logs/app.db`. Scratch tests: `leave_store` round-trip incl. window-narrowing + `clear_leave`; `lock-status` across all four states (nothing / check-in only / both / on-leave-with-no-task); `not-logged-in` 200-with-token / 401-without / correct `needs_*` detail; `set_leave`/`clear_leave` agent tools across 9 cases (self, teammate range, unknown person, unresolved date string, fully-past window, clear self/teammate, clear-when-empty, client refused). **Not run against a live WhatsApp message or a real nudge cycle.**
    - **Follow-up 2026-09-10 — the "Check In button" the banner told people to click did not exist.** Check-in only ever fired automatically inside `login.html`'s `submitPin()`. A returning employee with a still-valid session cookie from a prior day never hits `login.html`, so `/api/attendance/checkin` never fires that day; the 60s heartbeat ping (gotcha #89) still creates today's `daily_attendance` row but with `checkin_time` NULL, so `needs_checkin` stays true and they land on `standup.html?locked=1&need=checkin` with a banner pointing at a button that is on no page in the app — a dead end (only escape was manually logging out and back in). Fixed in `standup.html`: the lock banner now renders a real **Check In** button (`#lock-checkin-btn`, split the banner into `#lock-banner-text` + the button) whenever `need` includes `checkin`; it `POST`s `/api/attendance/checkin {user_id: UID}` (same call `login.html` makes) then `location.reload()`s so `auth.js` re-checks the lock. Banner text also now handles the combined `checkin,standup` case explicitly instead of only the two single-need cases. `node --check` clean on the extracted script block; not exercised against a live locked session this session.
    - **Follow-up 2026-09-11 — personal login-nudge DM interval widened from 5 min to 20 min, per user ask ("that thing 5 5 mins one to login make it 20 mins").** `--login-nudge-every` default in `scripts/laptop_agent.py` changed `300 → 1200`. Only the **personal DM** cadence changed — `job_login_group_ping`'s two fixed daily times (10:35/11:30, gotcha's own note above) and the `--login-nudge-start`/`--login-nudge-end` (10:40–19:00) active window are untouched. Also fixed the DM text itself, which hardcoded "every few minutes" regardless of the actual interval — it now reads `cfg["login_nudge_every"]` and says "every N min" (`every_min = max(1, cfg["login_nudge_every"] // 60)`), so this line can't silently drift out of sync with the real cadence again if the interval is ever changed a third time. **Verification:** `py_compile` clean on `scripts/laptop_agent.py`. Scripts-only change — reaches the always-on laptop via its own `job_selfupdate` git-pull poll (~10 min after push), no bridge restart, no session-fork risk. Not exercised against a live nudge cycle this session.
    - **Follow-up 2026-09-11, same session — widened again, 20 min → hourly, per user ask ("keep that pinging of login hourly based").** `--login-nudge-every` default `1200 → 3600`. Same scope note as above (personal DM only, group-ping times/window untouched) — the DM text's dynamic "every N min" line needed no further change, it already reads the config value.

120. **WhatsApp-bot cost pass, round 2 — 2026-09-11: user asked to "do all" of 4 more suggested levers. 2 real fixes shipped, 1 confirmed already done, 1 investigated and correctly skipped.** Went in assuming a clean slate, but `git log` on `whatsapp_agent.py` turned up an undocumented earlier commit (`54762e6`, 2026-09-04, "Cut WhatsApp bot API cost: all 5 levers") that had already reduced `_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS` 10→6, `max_tokens` 1200→600, `web_search max_uses` 4→2, and `_CONTEXT_MAX_TURNS` 6→4 — none of that got a CLAUDE.md entry at the time, which is why gotcha #111 (written 3 days later) still describes those as unreduced. **Lesson: `git log -- <file>` before assuming a suggested optimization is still outstanding** — CLAUDE.md is supposed to be the source of truth but isn't guaranteed complete if an earlier session skipped documenting a change.
    - **[Fixed] Tool-definition array was resent uncached every round, same shape as gotcha #111's system-prompt fix but left as a follow-up there.** `_EMPLOYEE_TOOLS`'s ~20 tool defs and `_CLIENT_TOOLS`'s single tool are static per request — added `"cache_control": {"type": "ephemeral"}` to the **last** entry of each list (the `web_search` server-tool spec, and `get_my_tasks` respectively — a cache breakpoint covers everything before it in the request). Combines with the already-cached system-prompt block from #111 so essentially the whole static prefix of every tool-loop round is now cacheable.
    - **[Fixed] `group_vibe_sticker()`'s burst-reaction mood classifier — a real Haiku call, gated only by a 30-min per-group cooldown — replaced with a zero-cost keyword/emoji heuristic.** Its own system prompt told the model to reply "none" most of the time (nothing in a chat burst is usually sticker-worthy), meaning most of these calls were pure waste with no caching benefit possible (cooldown > cache TTL, per #111's own reasoning for why it was left uncached rather than fixed). New `_VIBE_PATTERNS` — a list of `(regex, mood)` pairs covering the 11 built-in moods (lol/bruh/shock/nice/celebrate/facepalm/cry/fire/agree/smug/oof) via obvious keywords and emoji — scans the last 15 messages' text; first match wins. Falls through to a literal substring check against the bridge's actual taught sticker tags (`tags` param) for anything past the fixed vocabulary. No match at all → `None`, same as the old "none" answer, just free. `_client.messages.create`/`record_usage`/`calculate_cost`/`get_model_for_task` calls removed from this function entirely (confirmed still used elsewhere in the file for `handle_message()`, not orphaned imports).
    - **[Confirmed already done, no change needed] `smart_memory.recall()`'s top-k for prompt injection is already 8, not 20** — it was built that way from the start in gotcha #112, never the flat 20-item dump `memory_store.py`'s non-semantic fallback uses. The suggestion to "trim it further" was based on a wrong assumption about its current value; left as-is rather than shrinking an already-reasonable, already-ranked-by-relevance number for an unproven benefit.
    - **[Investigated, correctly skipped] Adding `cache_control` to `routes/ops.py`'s `_claude_call()` (backs every `/api/ai/*` and standup-AI route — smart-add, auto-fill, ai-coach, meeting-to-tasks, etc.) would not have helped.** Read every call site (`ai_priority_advisor`, `ai_task_breakdown`, and 8 more): every one of them has a short, one-or-two-sentence static `system` string — nowhere near Anthropic's minimum cacheable block size (roughly 1024+ tokens) — with essentially all the real content (task lists, meeting notes, questions) in the `user` field, which varies every call and so isn't a caching candidate anyway. These are also human-button-triggered, one-shot calls, not a tight per-message loop like the WhatsApp agent — the volume that made caching worthwhile there doesn't exist here. Adding `cache_control` here would have been a no-op at best (silently ignored below the minimum) — didn't add speculative dead code for a benefit that doesn't exist on the current call sites.
    - **Verification:** `venv`'s `python -m py_compile` and `pyflakes` both clean on `whatsapp_agent.py` (zero warnings). Not exercised against a live WhatsApp message or a real burst-reaction trigger this session — the tool-cache change only reshapes the `tools` param (same pattern already proven live-adjacent by #111's system-prompt cache), and the vibe-heuristic rewrite keeps the exact same function signature/return contract so no caller (`app.py`'s `/whatsapp/bridge` burst branch) needed to change.

121. **`_stop_bridge()` hardened to confirm the old bridge process actually exited before a respawn is allowed — 2026-09-11, in response to a live "some employees see the login-nudge message stuck on 'Waiting for this message'" report.** That symptom is the forked-Signal-session bug from gotcha #118 (two `node index.js` processes briefly holding the same WhatsApp identity forks the encryption ratchet for whoever's messaged in the overlap window, and it doesn't self-heal). `_stop_bridge()` already `terminate()`d + synchronously `wait()`d before this change, but on the kill() fallback path it never re-`wait()`d to confirm the kill actually landed, and never cleared `_BRIDGE["proc"]` itself (left that to the next `job_bridge()` tick's own bookkeeping) — small gaps that could in theory still let a respawn race a not-yet-fully-dead old process. Now: the kill() fallback also `wait(timeout=5)`s, logs a warning if the process still hasn't exited after both attempts (so a real stuck-process case is visible in `bridge.log` instead of silently risking a fork), and clears `_BRIDGE["proc"]` immediately on confirmed exit rather than waiting a tick.
    - **This does NOT retroactively fix any contact whose session is already forked** — those need the manual wipe from gotcha #118 (`schtasks /end /tn "Lumina companion"` → kill the bridge `node` process → delete `whatsapp-bridge/auth/session-*.json` only, keeping `creds.json`/`pre-key-*`/`sender-key-*`/`app-state-*` → `schtasks /run /tn "Lumina companion"`) run **on the always-on laptop itself** — this session has no remote access to it, so that step is on the user (or a future session actually invoked with access to that machine) to run. Check `bridge.log` for `Bad MAC`/`Closing session`/`Failed to decrypt` entries clustered around the affected contacts' numbers to confirm it's this bug before wiping anything.
    - **Verification:** `py_compile` + `pyflakes` clean on `scripts/laptop_agent.py`. Not exercised against a real overlapping-restart race this session (would need two rapid `whatsapp-bridge/**` pulls on the real laptop to reproduce) — scripts-only, reaches the always-on box via its own `job_selfupdate` poll (~10 min after push).
