122. **A THIRD, previously-undocumented root cause of "Waiting for this message" found and fixed live, 2026-09-15 — this one is NOT the restart-overlap bug (#118/#121), and it also explains a real bridge crash.** User reported the group symptom again; investigated fresh (per systematic-debugging discipline) instead of assuming a repeat of #118, since #121's hardening should already prevent that specific overlap. `bridge.log` gave an exact, unambiguous timeline: the bridge restarted at 12:19:08 to load that day's Baileys library bump (`^6.7.9` → `^7.0.0-rc11`, needed for voice calling — see gotcha #113), sent a post-upgrade confirmation message at 12:20:07, and the **very next log line** was a mass `Closing session`/`Bad MAC` cascade — one continuous process, no overlap, starting the instant the new library version first touched the pre-existing (v6.7.9-created) session files on disk. The bridge then crashed at 12:35:19 with an unusual native-looking exit code (`4294967295` / `0xFFFFFFFF`, not a normal JS exception's small positive code) — and the timing lines up with baileys-caller's WASM VoIP module printing its own "Blocking on the main thread is very dangerous" emscripten warning right around several failed call-placement retries (the `#activeCall` bug from gotcha #113, not yet fixed at that point in the day). The crash left **zero diagnostic info** in the log (no stack trace at all) — `index.js` had no `uncaughtException`/`unhandledRejection` handler, so whatever actually threw is unconfirmed, only strongly implicated.
    - **Root cause #1 (the fork): a Baileys MAJOR version bump is not a safe hot-swap over existing session files.** Old libsignal session state written under 6.7.9 was evidently misinterpreted (or its MAC no longer verified correctly) once the new 7.0.0-rc11/rc14 library loaded it — cascading into "Closing session"/rebuild-from-prekey for essentially every contact and the team group's sender-key sessions, exactly the "Waiting for this message" symptom. **Not a bug in this repo's own code** — it's an inherent risk of any future Baileys/library major-version upgrade. **Fixed for now** by the live session wipe (below); **the durable fix is procedural**: any future `@whiskeysockets/baileys` major-version bump MUST include a full `auth/session-*.json` + `auth/sender-key-*.json` wipe as a planned step of the upgrade, not an afterthought discovered after messages start getting stuck. `creds.json`/`pre-key-*`/`app-state-*` are identity material, not session state — never wipe those, no re-pair needed.
    - **Root cause #2 (the crash): the voice-calling WASM library shared a process with the production text bridge.** A fault in a young, single-maintainer, WASM-based library placing real-time calls should never be able to take down the live WhatsApp text-messaging session — but until this fix, it could, and the timeline above is the leading evidence that it did. **Fixed**: `whatsapp-bridge/voice-runner.js` (new) places exactly one call and exits; `index.js`'s `/call` route now spawns it via `execFile` in a **separate OS process** (`runVoiceCallIsolated()`) instead of `import()`-ing `voice.js` in-process — writes `{to, message}` to the child's stdin, reads one JSON line back from stdout, and treats ANY crash/timeout/malformed-output from that child as an ordinary clean `{ok:false, error}` result. A future crash in voice calling can now only ever kill that one short-lived child; the text bridge and the live WhatsApp session are structurally unaffected.
    - **Also fixed, defense-in-depth for "crashing" generally:** `index.js` gained top-level `uncaughtException`/`unhandledRejection` handlers that log the full stack trace before exiting — so a future crash (from this or any other cause) is actually diagnosable instead of showing up as a bare unexplained exit code, which is exactly what made root-causing today's incident harder than it should have been. `scripts/laptop_agent.py`'s `job_bridge()` crash-handling branch also gained a 15s minimum backoff floor for a crash after a long healthy run (previously: `up >= 120s` reset `fails` to 0, giving a **0-second** backoff — confirmed live today, the replacement respawned just 17s after the crash). This doesn't address root cause #1 (that corruption happened within one continuously-running process, no overlap involved) but is cheap, safe insurance against any *other* future crash where the old process's teardown genuinely isn't instant from WhatsApp's side.
    - **Live fix applied the same way as gotcha #118**: backed up `auth/` (`C:\lumina-backups\auth-backup-groupfix-<stamp>`), stopped the task, killed the bridge's `node index.js` process specifically (verified via `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process` command-line match, not just process name — the always-on laptop also runs an unrelated Adobe `node.exe`), deleted `session-*.json` (28) and `sender-key-*.json` (39, including the team group's), restarted, confirmed reconnect needed **no re-pair** (`creds.json` untouched), sent a real test message to the group, and the user confirmed receipt on a real phone.
    - **Verification of the code changes:** `node --check` clean on `index.js`/`voice-runner.js`, `py_compile` clean on `scripts/laptop_agent.py`. The isolation wrapper's plumbing (spawn, stdin write, stdout parse, timeout/crash handling) is code-reviewed and structurally sound but **not exercised against a real call this session** — deliberately avoided re-triggering the voice-call/VoIP-connect path again so soon after today's incident without a concrete reason to. `voice.js` itself (the actual call-placement logic now running inside the isolated child) is unchanged from what was already live-verified working earlier today (gotcha #113's real successful call + the "A call is already active" fix) — only the process boundary around it changed.
    - **If "Waiting for this message" recurs again:** check `bridge.log` for whether it started **immediately after a library/dependency version change and restart** (this cause) vs. **two restart timestamps close together** (#118/#121's overlap) vs. something new — the fingerprint (`Bad MAC`/`Closing session`/`Failed to decrypt`) is identical for all three, but the trigger and the correct fix differ. Don't assume it's a repeat of the most recently-documented cause without checking the actual timeline first, the way this entry did.

123. **Initiative engine, standing instructions, and a memory that spans the week — 2026-09-23.** User asked for the bot to be more like [Instinct](https://instinct.com/) and "a complete assistant." Researched Instinct first: a consumer personal assistant you text or call, whose defining traits are (a) one continuous thread forever, (b) a persistent machine that keeps working between your messages, (c) standing instructions, (d) real-world execution via browser automation + cached logins. Mapped against Lumina, the gap was **never tool count** (the bot already had 33 tools, denser than Instinct for an office context) — it was four structural things: no initiative, a 6-hour memory, no persistent rules, and a walled garden. User chose to build the first three; email/calendar reach was deferred (needs Gmail/Google Calendar connector OAuth) and browser automation was not pursued. Autonomy scope chosen: **act freely inside Lumina, still confirm before anything that reaches another person** — so none of this changes the existing confirm-before-broadcast gates.
    - **`backend/standing_rules.py` (new)** — "from now on always…" rules, table `standing_rules`. Two halves on purpose: every active rule is injected into that person's system prompt on every message (so it's obeyed conversationally), and a rule may *additionally* carry `remind_day` + `remind_time` so recurring ones actually fire. That narrow weekday+clock hook is deliberate — a natural-language cron parser was rejected as overreach when `remind_me`/`schedule_group_message` already own one-off timed things, and anything more exotic is still honoured via the prompt. Restating an existing rule updates it in place instead of duplicating; active rules are capped at `MAX_RULES_PER_USER` (25, oldest deactivated) so the prompt can't grow without bound.
    - **`backend/followups.py` (new)** — the initiative engine. Table `followup_log` for dedup. **Deliberately non-overlapping with the five proactive jobs that already exist**: `job_login_nudge`, `job_login_group_ping`, `job_eod_group`/`job_eod_personal` and `check_overdue_tasks`'s 08:00 escalation already cover "you haven't checked in", "no standup yet", "today's pending list" and "this task is overdue". Re-reporting any of that would have made this the *sixth* thing DMing someone the same fact. What nothing covered before, and what this watches: `stale_task` (a standup task that's been rolling over for ≥3 days, read off `carried_from`, which is set once and never updated — the same property gotcha #77 relies on), `assigned_stalled` (work you put on a teammate that hasn't moved in ≥2 days, **reported to the assigner** — nobody was ever told their delegation went nowhere), `approval` (stuck awaiting sign-off ≥2 days), and `rule` (a recurring standing instruction coming due).
    - **The agent composes the nudge, not a format string.** `whatsapp_agent.compose_followup(employee, items)` is a separate entry point from `handle_message` — no inbound message, no tool loop, one short call that turns already-detected loose ends into something reading like the assistant noticed. It reuses the full `_system_prompt` so the persona holds (reverent to Abhinav, dry to everyone else), and is told explicitly not to invent any task/date/name not in the supplied list. **Returns `""` on any failure and `followups._fallback_text()` sends a deterministic message instead** — a proactive nudge must never depend on an LLM call succeeding, so the feature still works at zero LLM spend with the budget exhausted, it just sounds like a cron job.
    - **Rails, because an engine that messages people unprompted is only useful if it stays quiet:** IST weekday + 10:00–19:00 window; skips anyone inactive, on leave (`leave_store`), or with no WhatsApp number; a 20h per-`(person, kind, ref)` cooldown so one stalled task is mentioned once a day at most; a hard 2-messages-per-person-per-day cap; items sorted longest-stalled-first so the cap trims the least important. `was_recently_sent`/`sent_today_count` both **fail closed** — a DB hiccup makes the bot stay quiet rather than risk spamming.
    - **Runs on Railway's APScheduler, not the laptop** (`task_scheduler._run_followup_sweep`, `interval`, 90 min). Deliberately an *interval* job, not `cron`: `sweep()` checks the IST window itself, so it doesn't matter what timezone the container clock is in (the pre-existing `hour=8` cron is whatever the host calls 08:00 — an ambiguity left alone, not silently "fixed"). Delivery is `wa_outbox`, which the laptop poller already drains — **so this needed zero companion-side change and no bridge restart, i.e. no session-fork risk (gotchas #118/#121/#122).**
    - **Four new employee-only tools**: `set_standing_rule`, `list_standing_rules`, `remove_standing_rule`, `get_followups` (the on-demand half of the same engine — "what am I forgetting", "what's slipping"). The two writes are in `_WRITE_TOOLS`; their replies were phrased to start with "Added"/"Cleared" so the existing `_SUCCESS_PREFIXES` audit gate picks them up with no new entries, same trick as `remind_me` (gotcha's own note). All four refuse a `client` identity.
    - **Memory**: `_CONTEXT_TTL_HOURS` 6 → 168 (a week), `_CONTEXT_MAX_TURNS` 4 → 10. The 6h TTL was the real amnesia culprit — a morning conversation was already forgotten by the afternoon. **Raising the TTL costs nothing per message** (`MAX_TURNS` is what bounds how much history is actually resent); only the turn count is a real token lever, hence the modest bump there. This partially reverses commit `54762e6`'s cost trim, consciously — gotchas #111/#120 since added prompt+tool caching, which paid for it.
    - **Caching note**: the standing-rules block goes *inside* the cached system-prompt block (it changes only when someone sets a rule, so invalidation is rare and the cheap path is right), unlike `smart_memory` recall which varies per message and is appended as a separate uncached block (gotcha #111's reasoning).
    - **Two new companion routes**, both token-gated: `GET /api/companion/followups` (dry run — sends and records nothing, the inspection window into a feature that otherwise only shows itself by messaging people) and `POST /api/companion/followups/run` (force a sweep; still obeys every rail, so it can't be used to spam).
    - **Real bug caught in my own code before testing, worth remembering:** `_stalled_delegations` first queried `WHERE delegated_from = <user_id>`. Every writer of that column — `assign_task`, `delegate_my_task`, `create_task` in `whatsapp_agent.py` and `delegate_task` in `routes/ops.py` — stores the assigner's **display name** (`identity["name"]`), never their employee id, so the query would have silently matched nothing forever. Fixed to a `LOWER(delegated_from)=LOWER(name)` match. **If adding anything else that reads `delegated_from`, it is a name.**
    - **Verification**: `py_compile` + `pyflakes` clean on all six touched files (zero new warnings; the two `task_scheduler` datetime/timedelta ones are the documented pre-existing baseline), `import app` boots at 197 routes with `_run_followup_sweep` registered, and the `standing_rules`/`followup_log` migrations run against the real `logs/app.db`. An **82-check functional test** against a temp DB (never `logs/app.db`, no real Anthropic calls) covers: rule add/list/restate-in-place/remove-by-number/remove-by-text/cap/`rules_text`/`due_reminders` day+time matching; every detector's positive AND negative case (1-day carry not flagged, task typed today not flagged, done-but-stale not flagged, fresh delegation not chased, completed delegation not chased, assignee doesn't get an `assigned_stalled` for their own work); the dedup log incl. normalization and cooldown expiry; all five working-window cases; the sweep end-to-end (outside-window no-op, dry run writes nothing, real run enqueues to the right number with the composed text, `left` employee skipped, on-leave skipped, second sweep re-sends nothing, compose-failure falls back); every new tool incl. malformed day/time, day-without-time, time-without-day, empty rule, unknown person, and client lockout on all four; and the prompt injection being present for the rule-holder and absent for everyone else. Prior scratch tests (`remind_me`, future-standup-date, `message_multiple`) re-run clean — zero regressions.
    - **NOT live-verified**: no real WhatsApp message and no real sweep firing against production data this session. `compose_followup`'s actual model call is stubbed in the test (the fallback path is exercised for real); `_stale_approvals` is the one detector never exercised against live Notion, since the test runs with `notion_store.is_configured()` False. **First thing to check if the bot goes quiet or noisy**: `GET /api/companion/followups` returns exactly what the engine currently sees without sending anything.

124. **Railway volume filled to 100%, production went fully down (502 on every route including `/api/health`) — 2026-09-28. Root-caused and fixed live, in production, without paying for more storage (user explicitly declined the upgrade).** User saw "Volume Is Full" on `web-volume`'s metrics tab. Full incident, several rounds:
    - **Diagnosis tooling added first, since neither Railway CLI nor an MCP connection was available this session to exec into the container.** `GET /api/companion/storage-report` (new, token-gated like every other `/api/companion/*` route) reports a byte/file-count breakdown of everything under `logs/` plus real `shutil.disk_usage()` totals. First read: `app.db` 350MB, `hf_cache` (semantic-KB's downloaded embedding model, gotcha #98) 123MB, `uploads` 31MB, volume total only ~454MB, 97.8% used.
    - **`POST /api/companion/storage-cleanup?targets=hf_cache,vacuum`** (new): `hf_cache` is fully disposable (semantic_kb.py already lazily re-downloads it on next use) so deleting it is zero-risk; `vacuum` runs SQLite `VACUUM` to reclaim space left behind by old deletes (confirmed live: `db.py::get_connection()` opens with no explicit transaction wrapper, so VACUUM runs cleanly on a fresh connection). **Order matters and is enforced in the docstring**: VACUUM needs free space to rebuild the file and fails cleanly (never touches the live DB) if there isn't any — so `hf_cache` must be freed first.
    - **First real attempt: `hf_cache` cleanup succeeded (freed ~61.5MB of *real* disk, confirmed via before/after `shutil.disk_usage` — the reported "122.84MB freed" was inflated ~2x, see the symlink bug below), then VACUUM failed with "database or disk is full."** Root cause: **`hf_cache` silently regrew** (bigger than before — 183MB not 123MB) during the gap between the two calls, racing VACUUM and refilling the disk before it could use the space. Not a VACUUM bug — VACUUM aborted exactly as designed, zero data touched.
    - **Root cause of the regrowth, found by tracing the code, not guessing: `GET /api/kb/semantic` and `GET /api/memory/smart` (`routes/system.py`) had ZERO auth, and both call `stats()`, which unconditionally calls `available()` — which LOADS (and downloads, if missing) the shared embedding model just to answer a status check, regardless of whether the feature is even enabled.** Any request to either URL — from anyone, or any automated check — could force that download. This is what raced against every cleanup attempt. **Fixed**: both GET routes now require the same admin-tier `user_id` every POST route in this file already required (gotcha #60's `bool(user_id)` convention) — closing the open trigger.
    - **`storage-report`'s own reported hf_cache size was inflated ~2x — a real bug in the diagnostic itself, found and fixed.** `huggingface_hub`'s cache layout stores model weights once under `blobs/<hash>` and symlinks to it from `snapshots/<rev>/<filename>`; the original `_du()` walked both without skipping the symlink, and `pathlib`'s default symlink-following `is_file()`/`stat()` counted the same physical bytes twice. Fixed by checking `is_symlink()` before ever following one, both for a bare-file root and inside the `rglob` walk. Confirmed via the real disk-usage delta from the actual `hf_cache` deletion (61.5MB real vs. 122.84MB reported) — not a hypothetical.
    - **Then the actual outage: `/api/health` itself started returning 502 "Application failed to respond."** `db.py` calls `init_db()` (opens a real sqlite3 connection, runs migrations) the instant it's *imported* — and several modules `app.py` imports transitively (`kb_retriever`, `whatsapp_agent`, `notion_store`, ...) pull that in before the file ever reaches its own logs-directory setup. A disk at 0% free can kill the app **during import**, before any request-time `try/except` ever gets a chance to help — this is why the app couldn't even serve `/api/health`.
    - **The fix, free, no CLI/shell access needed: a boot-time disk guard, added as the very first executable code in `app.py`, before any local module import.** Checks free space via `shutil.disk_usage`; only if critically low (<50MB) does it delete `hf_cache` — needs **zero free space to succeed**, since removing files never requires headroom the way writing or downloading one does. On a healthy volume it's a silent no-op, preserving the actual point of pointing `HF_HOME` at the persistent volume (avoid re-downloading on every redeploy). **Live-verified**: the very next deploy self-healed the outage with no manual Railway-dashboard action and no payment — `/api/health` returned `{"status":"ok"}` afterward, disk at 84.2% used / 61.6MB free.
    - **User explicitly declined paying for a bigger volume ("i dont want to upgrade is there some other wy") when the app was still down** — the boot guard above was built specifically as the free alternative, and worked.
    - **Durable fix, also free, user explicitly approved via `AskUserQuestion` after the acute crisis was resolved: `backend/data_retention.py` (new)** — nothing in this codebase had ever deleted an old row from its append-only log tables (`usage_logs`, `wa_action_log`, `wa_call_outbox`), so the database would keep growing back toward the ceiling from pure normal use, independent of any bug. New daily cron (`task_scheduler.py`, 03:30 IST) prunes: `usage_logs` (180d — **verified first, not assumed**, that every dashboard aggregate lives in a separately-persisted `budget` table row unaffected by this; the one live-computed exception, the WhatsApp all-time cost/calls/tokens stat, is preserved by folding pruned rows' totals into a persisted `app_settings` counter that `budget_tracker.get_usage_summary()` now adds back in — a row with no timestamp at all is never touched, no guessing), `wa_action_log` (180d, pure audit trail), `wa_call_outbox` (30d, **only ever-terminal rows** — a `pending` row is never deleted regardless of age). **Deliberately NOT touched**: `sheet_edit_log` (backs the user-facing Version History/Restore feature, gotchas #74/#75 — pruning it is a real feature-loss decision for a human, not an automatic one), `standup_tasks` (actively queried across arbitrary past date ranges by the history picker and velocity chart — needs its own careful pass), `conversations` (real chat history, never auto-deleted, period), `followup_log` (already has its own `prune_log(days=30)` in `followups.py`, not duplicated). New routes: `GET /api/companion/db-table-sizes` (per-table byte breakdown of `app.db` via SQLite's `dbstat` virtual table, so "which table is actually big" has a real answer instead of `app.db` being one opaque blob), `GET /api/companion/retention` (dry run), `POST /api/companion/retention/run` (force a sweep now).
    - **Verification, this whole incident**: every file `py_compile`+`pyflakes` clean against the documented baseline (zero new warnings). `import app` boots clean throughout, ending at 202 routes. Real functional tests at every step, run against temp DBs/directories (never `logs/app.db`): 13+17 checks for storage-report/cleanup, a symlink-counting test (the actual symlink-creation branch couldn't run in this session's Windows sandbox without admin rights — noted honestly, not faked; the fix is a straightforward guard that applies identically on Railway's Linux container), 4 checks proving the boot guard triggers under critical disk pressure and stays silent otherwise and never crashes even if `disk_usage()` itself fails, 10 checks proving the two auth fixes 403 without a `user_id` and 200 with one, and — the one that matters most for correctness, not just "does it delete rows" — a 19-check test proving the WhatsApp all-time stat is **byte-for-byte identical before and after a prune**, and that running the sweep twice doesn't double-count the folded total. **Live-verified in production**: the storage-report/cleanup routes against the real volume (multiple rounds, including catching the regrowth mid-incident), and the boot guard's actual recovery of the real outage. **Not yet live-verified**: the data-retention cron itself (will run for the first time at the next 03:30 IST tick, or on-demand via the new `/run` route).
    - **If the volume creeps back up again**: check `GET /api/companion/storage-report` first (what's using space) and `GET /api/companion/db-table-sizes` second (which table inside `app.db` specifically) before assuming it's the same cause — `sheet_edit_log`/`standup_tasks`/`conversations` were deliberately left unpruned and are the most likely next growth source if this recurs.

125. **"My Attendance" manual Check In / Check Out widget on `dashboard.html` — 2026-09-30.** User wanted explicit dashboard buttons: click Check In → capture location → start office time; click Check Out → capture location → end it. Backend was **already fully built** for this (`_attendance_checkin`/`_attendance_checkout` in `routes/attendance.py` already accept optional `lat`/`lng` and the endpoints already had no other gate beyond `user_id` — see the 2026-09-28 geolocation work referenced inside gotcha #124's neighborhood) — it just had no manual UI entry point, only two automatic ones: `login.html`'s `submitPin()` fires checkin on every login, `auth.js`'s `authLogout()` fires checkout on every logout, both already grabbing `navigator.geolocation` via a shared `getAttendanceLocation()` helper (duplicated in both files, `window.getAttendanceLocation` in `auth.js`).
    - **New `#my-attendance-panel`** in `dashboard.html`, above the existing admin Live Attendance grid: a status line + Check In / Check Out buttons, visible to every employee (not admin-gated — it's each person's own attendance). Reuses `window.getAttendanceLocation()` from `auth.js` (already loaded on this page) rather than a third copy of the geolocation helper.
    - **`renderMyAttendance(myRow, isViewingToday)`** is called from inside the existing `loadAttendance()` (which already fetches `/api/attendance/today` and builds `allRows` for the admin grid) — no second network round-trip, just reads the current user's own row out of data already being fetched. Button state: no checkin today → show Check In only; checked in, no checkout → show Check Out only; both present → still show Check Out (correctable — re-clicking just updates `checkout_time` to "latest wins", matching the endpoint's existing UPSERT semantics, gotcha #94). Buttons hidden entirely when viewing a past date via the date picker — checking in/out for a day that's already over makes no sense.
    - **Composes with the existing automatic paths, doesn't replace them**: clicking Check In after an auto-checkin-on-login already fired is a harmless no-op (`checkin_time` only ever fills once, per gotcha #94's `WHERE checkin_time IS NULL` fix) — the button just reflects/re-asserts what already happened, or is the *first* thing that happens if someone's session was still valid from a prior day and they never hit `login.html` fresh (exactly the scenario gotcha #119's follow-up flagged as needing a real Check In affordance, which `standup.html`'s lock banner already got — this gives the same action a permanent, always-visible home on the dashboard too, not just inside the lock banner).
    - **No backend changes needed at all** — this is a frontend-only addition, same two existing endpoints, same request shape (`{user_id, lat?, lng?}`) `login.html`/`auth.js` already send.
    - **Verification**: `node --check` clean on the extracted `<script>` block. Not exercised against a live browser/real geolocation prompt this session — the button wiring is a direct rewire of the exact `fetch`/body-shape pattern already proven live in `login.html`'s auto-checkin (same endpoint, same optional-lat/lng contract), so the main untested surface is the button-state logic (`renderMyAttendance`'s three branches) rather than anything server-side.

126. **Full leave management system — 2026-09-30, built via subagent-driven-development (9 tasks + a 3-round final-review fix wave), merged to `main` at `158ba5b`.** User asked for: a 15-day annual leave pool per employee; overtime beyond 9hrs/day accumulating and every 24 cumulative hours converting into +1 leave day; a calendar UI to apply for/cancel leave with colored dots (green=full day worked, yellow=half day worked, red=approved leave, orange=pending request); HR approval (hardcoded to `emp009`/Noorish, same allowlist convention as the bet feature — see gotcha #102); a leave column in the downloadable attendance Excel. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-30-leave-management-design.md`. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-30-leave-management.md`.
    - **Reused, not forked: `employee_leave`/`leave_store.py` already existed (gotcha #119)**, built narrowly for WhatsApp-only instant self-exemption from the standup lock. This feature extends that table (new `status`/`leave_type`/`approved_by`/`approved_at` columns, `status` defaulting `'approved'` so every pre-existing WhatsApp-granted row stays valid) rather than building a parallel system.
    - **Behavior change from gotcha #119, deliberate and explicitly accepted:** WhatsApp's `set_leave`/`clear_leave` tools no longer apply instantly — `set_leave` now creates a `pending` request awaiting HR approval, and `is_on_leave()`/`on_leave_ids()` (which gate the standup lock, gotcha #108/#119, and the login-nudge suppression) now only ever honour `status='approved'` rows. A same-day WhatsApp leave request no longer instantly unlocks standup — it waits for Noorish to approve it. This closes the self-approval loophole a real balance system would otherwise have.
    - **Discovery mid-plan: `routes/attendance.py::attendance_export_sheets()` already had most of the Excel-export infrastructure** (`_FULL_DAY_HOURS=9.0`, `_overtime_hours()`, `_hours_and_day_type()`, a full multi-sheet xlsx export) from an earlier, undocumented session's geolocation work. The plan's spec had envisioned a brand-new separate export endpoint; this was corrected mid-build to extend the existing function instead — a weekday with no checkin covered by an approved leave now labels `"Leave (Approved)"` (its own fill color, excluded from the attendance-% denominator like "In Progress" already is) instead of plain `"Leave"`, and the Summary sheet gained `Leave Used (Formal)`/`Comp-Off Earned`/`Leave Remaining` columns.
    - **New:** `backend/routes/leave.py` (`GET /api/leave/balance`, `GET /api/leave/calendar`, `POST /api/leave/apply`, `POST /api/leave/<id>/cancel` self-service; `GET /api/leave/pending`, `POST /api/leave/<id>/approve`, `POST /api/leave/<id>/reject` HR-only), `frontend/leave.html` (balance strip, month calendar, click-to-apply modal, click-a-pending-day-to-cancel, HR pending-approvals panel), a nightly `task_scheduler.py` cron (02:00 IST) running `leave_store.run_overtime_conversion_sweep()`, and a new WhatsApp tool `get_leave_balance`.
    - **Final whole-branch review (opus) found and fixed 3 Critical + 4 Important bugs in the seams between code paths — exactly the class of bug a per-task review structurally cannot see, since each task passed its own review individually:**
      1. **`/api/leave/*` had no real authentication at all** — the HR gate compared a client-supplied `user_id` body param against `"emp009"`; combined with this app's `CORS(origins="*")`, anyone could approve any leave, read everyone's pending requests/reasons, or file leave in a colleague's name. Fixed with a real `session_token` cookie check (`_session_user_id()`, mirroring `routes/attendance.py::_verified_admin()`'s exact pattern) as the sole source of identity for every route.
      2. **`get_balance()` deducted 1.0 day per approved leave ROW, not per DAY** — a 5-weekday WhatsApp leave request only cost 1.0 day, not 5.0. Fixed to count actual weekdays in the window (`_deduction_in_year()`), splitting a deduction across a Dec31/Jan1 year boundary, and made `comp_off_ledger`'s year-bucketing UTC→IST consistent (the column is stored UTC, the "which year did this accrue in" question is IST).
      3. **WhatsApp's `clear_leave` tool hard-DELETEd approved leave for ANY employee** (via a `person` argument), with no audit trail, silently refunding the balance — including mid-already-taken-leave. Fixed at both layers: `leave_store.clear_leave()` is now a thin compatibility alias to a new `cancel_pending_for_user()` (status transition to `'cancelled'`, pending-only, row preserved — never a hard delete), and the WhatsApp tool's `person` argument was dropped entirely (an employee can only ever cancel their own still-pending request, never touch anyone else's).
      4. **The approved/pending overlap-rejection guard only ever existed on `apply_leave()` (the web path), not `set_leave()` (WhatsApp)** — a chat request could double-book an approved day, and two pending requests for the same day could both be approved, double-deducting. Fixed with a shared `_overlapping_status()` helper used by both writers, plus a live re-check inside `approve_leave()` itself for the TOCTOU gap between request-time and approval-time.
      5. Plus: HR panel showed raw employee ids with no name resolution (`shared-config.js` was never included); WhatsApp blocked backdated leave, contradicting the spec's explicit "backdated leave is allowed" decision; `created_by` was written as an id by one path and a display name by the other.
    - **Two findings surfaced BY the fix wave itself, verified real by an independent scoped re-review, deliberately left open (parked, not fixed) per the process's "no second fix wave" rule at this stage:**
      - **`POST /api/leave/<id>/approve` has no try/except around the new live-overlap re-check** added to `approve_leave()` — if it fires (two near-simultaneous overlapping approval-worthy requests for the same person), HR gets a raw 500 instead of a clean 400. No data corruption (the double-booking is still correctly blocked before any write) — just an ungraceful error. **The fix, if picked up later, is a one-line copy of the try/except ValueError pattern `leave_apply()` already uses two functions above it in the same file.**
      - **WhatsApp's rewritten `clear_leave` success reply ("Cancelled your pending...") no longer starts with a word in `whatsapp_agent.py`'s `_SUCCESS_PREFIXES`** (which has `"cleared"`, not `"cancelled"`) — a successful self-cancel silently stops being written to the `wa_action_log` audit trail. Functionally harmless (cancellation still works, self-only, no security impact) — just an audit-coverage gap. **Fix: add `"cancelled"` to `_SUCCESS_PREFIXES`, or reword the reply back to start with "Cleared".**
    - **Also flagged, not a defect:** the balance-per-day fix (`_deduction_in_year()`) has zero automated regression coverage — every existing test uses single-day leave windows only, none exercises a multi-day window's actual deduction math. Verified correct by two independent code traces (the original implementer + the re-reviewer), but a real multi-day test would close this gap if anyone's back in this file.
    - **Verification:** all 7 new test files (`test_leave_schema/store/routes/overtime_job/lock_regression/whatsapp_tools/export`) pass, `import app` boots clean (213 routes), pyflakes clean on every touched backend file, `node --check` clean on `leave.html`. **Not exercised against a live browser, a real geolocation-adjacent flow, or real employees/Noorish actually using it** — every verification in this feature is test-suite-level or direct-code-trace-level, same honest caveat as most features in this codebase's history. If leave balance, the calendar dots, or HR approval look wrong in production, check the specific finding numbers above first — most of the subtle logic (day-counting, year-splitting, the approval-time re-check) was added during the fix wave, not the original 9-task build, and is the least battle-tested part of this feature.

127. **HR Office Calendar + shared Google-Calendar-style component — 2026-10-05, commits `89aee1c`/`9b1638e`.** User asked for a page where Noorish (HR, `emp009`) sets public holidays and work-from-home / in-person office days, shown on everyone's leave calendar, with the same Google Calendar look everywhere.
    - **Backend:** table `office_calendar` (`kind` = `holiday|wfh|office`, `title`, inclusive `start_date`/`end_date`, `created_by`) in `db.py`; `routes/office_calendar.py` — `GET /api/office-calendar?start=&end=` (any logged-in session), `POST` / `PUT /<id>` / `DELETE /<id>` HR-only. **Auth reuses `routes.leave._require_session/_require_hr` (real `session_token` cookie), never a body `user_id`** — same rule as gotcha #126's fix wave. A holiday must have a name; wfh/office default their titles client-side.
    - **Frontend:** `frontend/gcal.js` is the one shared calendar (Month/Week/Day/4 days/Year/Schedule, D/W/M/Y/A/X shortcuts, mini-month + per-layer checkboxes in the sidebar, view and hidden layers remembered in `localStorage` under the caller's `storageKey`). All events are all-day; an event is `{source,title,start,end,style:'fill'|'dot'|'ring',color}`. Used by `office-calendar.html` (HR editor; anyone else is redirected to `leave.html`) and `leave.html` (read-only layers + the existing apply/cancel flow).
    - **CSS-collision bug, found live:** `gcal.js` chips originally used bare class names `dot`/`fill`/`ring`, and `leave.html`'s own legacy `.dot` rule (an 8px circle) squashed every non-HR user's "Work from home"/"In-person office"/attendance chips into dots, while HR's page (no such rule) looked fine. Fixed by namespacing the chip classes `gc-dot`/`gc-fill`/`gc-ring` and deleting the dead `.cal-*`/`.dot`/`.legend` CSS from `leave.html`. **When adding any shared widget CSS to a page with older inline styles, prefix the widget's classes** — and a hard refresh (Ctrl+Shift+R) is needed after a deploy that changes cached `gcal.js`.
    - **Display only:** holidays/WFH don't affect leave balance, apply validation or the attendance Excel export. Not built: a holiday blocking leave applications, or marking holidays in the export.

128. **Leave page renamed "Calendar" in the UI — 2026-10-07.** `leave.html` title/heading and the nav links in `dashboard.html`, `standup.html`, `office-calendar.html` now say "Calendar". The URL is deliberately unchanged (`/leave.html`) so bookmarks, the HR approval link in emails and WhatsApp links keep working.

129. **Leave-request email to HR (`backend/mailer.py`) — built 2026-10-07, INACTIVE until SMTP env vars are set (see the OPEN TODO at the top of this file).**
    - `mailer.py`: stdlib `smtplib` (STARTTLS on 587, SSL on 465), configured only by `SMTP_HOST/SMTP_PORT/SMTP_USER/SMTP_PASS` (+ optional `SMTP_FROM_NAME`). Unconfigured = logged no-op, never an error. Sends on a daemon thread so a slow mail server never delays an HTTP request. `email_for(user_id)` = `<first name lowercased>@mmga.agency` unless `employees.json` gives that person an explicit `"email"` field (add one if a first name ever collides or differs).
    - `notify_leave_request(row)`: To = `noorish@mmga.agency` (`HR_USER_ID`), Reply-To = the applicant, From display name = "`<Name>` via Lumina" (the *address* is always the one `SMTP_USER` mailbox — Lumina has no employees' mailbox passwords, so it can't truly send "from" each person). Body has dates, full/half, reason and a link to `/leave.html`.
    - **Hooked in `leave_store.py` after the row is committed**, in both `apply_leave()` (web) and `set_leave()` (WhatsApp tool), via `_email_hr()`, and only for `status == 'pending'`. A mail failure is swallowed so it can never fail or roll back the request. Approve/reject do **not** email the applicant (unasked follow-up).
    - **Check it:** `GET /api/companion/email-status?token=<STORAGE_SYNC_TOKEN>` → `{configured, smtp_host, sender, hr_email}`; add `&test=1` to send a real test mail to HR synchronously (`test_sent: true/false`). Production currently reports `configured:false`.
    - Verified with a mocked SMTP in a temp-DB scratch test (13 checks: addresses, no-op when unconfigured, web + WhatsApp paths both email, approved rows don't, send failure doesn't break the request). **No real email has ever been sent.**

130. **"Random tasks appear in my daily standup" — root cause found, 2026-10-05, commit `c9f7f6d`.** Reported by Abhinav, Lakshmi (`emp006`) and Charulata (`emp007`). `auto_fill_standup()` (the **Sync All Tasks** button, `routes/ops.py`) decided each Notion task's owner with a **hardcoded name→id dict** that still mapped `"Mohit"→emp006`, `"Palak"→emp007`, `"Om"→emp010` after those ids were reassigned (gotchas #102/#80), so every old Mohit/Palak task in Notion landed in Lakshmi's/Charulata's standup. Sync All also inserts for **everyone**, not just the clicker, and pulls anything `in_progress` regardless of due date — so one person pressing it re-populates everyone's standup with weeks-old tasks (matches Abhinav's stale tasks, which have a `notion_id` but no `carried_from`). Fix: the dict is now built from the live active employee list (`status` not in the inactive set). **Same family as gotcha #63 — never hardcode a name→id map.** Not changed (offered, not asked): making Sync All skip overdue tasks or confirm before filling everyone. A second automatic route exists: editing a task's assignee in Sheets/Kanban inserts it into that person's standup (`notion_update_task`, gotcha #42).

131. **Project-board "Daily Standup Tasks" group showed 266 tasks — now only tasks open in someone's current standup, 2026-10-05, commit `12b459c`.** `/api/notion/dashboard` kept an unassigned task if *any* `standup_tasks` row (any date, any status incl. `deleted`/`delegated`) existed for it and the last-seen row wasn't `done`, so finished/abandoned rows accumulated. Now, for the `notion_id == "unassigned"` group only: take each person's **most recent standup date** (`MAX(date)` per user, not strictly today, so someone who hasn't opened the app yet still counts), build `standup_open` (status not in done/deleted/delegated) and `standup_done`, and show a task only if it's in `standup_open` and not in `standup_done` — i.e. it vanishes the moment **anyone** ticks it. Live count 266 → 81. Real client groups are untouched. The standup ✗ (delete) still archives non-social Notion pages and is cross-off-only for social-media ones (gotcha #104); both now also drop off the board because deleted rows aren't "open". **Shared-task caveat:** if two people hold a task and one ticks it, it leaves the board for both — matches what the user asked, easy to change in `standup_done`.

132. **Overtime on work-from-home days, public holidays AND weekends (Sat/Sun) no longer counts toward comp-off leave — 2026-10-07/08.** Weekends were added 2026-10-08 on request: _NOT_WFH_SQL also requires `strftime('%w', date) NOT IN ('0','6')` (carry rows exempt via `date LIKE 'carry-%'`, since strftime on them is NULL). Weekend overtime is hidden everywhere the pending figure is read, but the rows stay in the ledger. User: a day HR marked WFH (they meant 6 Oct) or as a public holiday must not add overtime toward the 24h->1-leave-day conversion, for everyone. `leave_store._NOT_WFH_SQL` (`AND NOT EXISTS (... office_calendar kind='wfh' AND overtime_ledger.date BETWEEN start_date AND end_date)`) is appended to every "unconverted overtime" query: the pending-hours figure on the Calendar page (`get_balance`/`get_unconverted_ot_hours`), `convert_overtime_now`, and the row selection inside `_convert_overtime_for_user` (so WFH rows are never consumed). It is a **live filter, not a data change**: nothing is deleted, adding/removing a WFH day in HR's calendar instantly re-includes/excludes those hours retroactively, and `office` days still count (only `kind='wfh'` and `kind='holiday'` are excluded). Already-converted days are untouched (never revert granted balance). `carry-<uuid>` carry rows can't match the BETWEEN (non-date string sorts after digits). **Not changed:** the attendance Excel export still shows raw overtime hours per day (it computes from `daily_attendance`, not the ledger) and `/api/companion/ot-ledger-summary` still lists WFH-day rows (diagnostic only). The rule depends on HR actually marking the day on the Office Calendar. Verified in a temp-DB test (9 checks: WFH excluded, balance strip agrees, range + office day, un-marking restores, conversion leaves WFH hours unconsumed) plus the three earlier OT test files, all passing. At ship time `comp_off_ledger` was empty in production, so nothing already granted needed correcting.

133. **Hours are shown as "10h 31m", never decimals — 2026-10-08.** User: decimal hours (10.52) are confusing. Calendar page (`leave.html::fmtHM`): "OT Hours Pending", "N more needed", the convert toast and the "Full/Half day worked (…)" chips. WhatsApp `get_leave_balance` now also reports pending overtime via `leave_store.fmt_hm()`. **Excel export** (`routes/attendance.py::_hm`/`_HM_FMT`): Hours Worked, Overtime, monthly/total hours and the Summary sheet now store each duration as an Excel time value (hours/24, rounded to the minute) with number format `[h]"h" mm"m"` — still real numbers, so sums/filters work, and `[h]` keeps >24h from wrapping. Comp-off/leave *days* stay decimal (they're days, not hours). If a new screen shows a duration, use `fmtHM`/`fmt_hm`/`_hm`.
134. **11 PM "please check out" DM — 2026-10-08.** User: ping people personally at 11 PM, only those who checked in today but haven't checked out, only ONE message, never Vidit or Kshitij. Backend: `GET /api/companion/checkout-missing` (token-gated, `routes/companion.py`) lists active employees with a WhatsApp number who have a `daily_attendance` row today with `checkin_time` set and `checkout_time` empty. `emp001`/`emp004` are hard-excluded in code; `app_settings` key `checkout_nudge_exclude_ids` can only ADD more ids, never un-exclude them. Someone who never checked in is not listed. No weekend skip (the rule is purely "checked in and not out"). Laptop: `scripts/laptop_agent.py::job_checkout_nudge`, a daily job at `--checkout-nudge-time` (default 23:00; `--no-checkout-nudge` disables), DMs via `_bridge_send` with no retry; "one message" is guaranteed by the persisted `_daily_fired` state (a restart can't re-fire) and the job's 40-min window ends before midnight. The text tells them to check out or reply "out" (handled by the existing `set_attendance` tool). Note the heartbeat sweep (gotcha #89) already auto-checks-out people whose tab went quiet, so at 11 PM this mostly catches people with a tab still open. Scripts-only change on the laptop side: it reaches the always-on laptop via `job_selfupdate` (~10 min) with a scripts-only agent restart (bridge untouched, no session-fork risk). Verified with a temp-DB Flask test + a fake-bridge run of the job (list rules, config, exactly one DM, once-per-day gate, nothing before 23:00); not yet seen on a real phone at 11 PM.
135. **Timed events on HR's calendar, announced to everyone by email + WhatsApp — 2026-10-08.** HR's Office Calendar gained a fourth type, **Event** (`office_calendar.kind='event'`), with a required start time, optional end time and a "Notify everyone" switch (default on). New `office_calendar` columns `start_time`, `end_time` (HH:MM), `notify`, `reminded_at` (db.py ALTERs). `backend/office_events.py`: `announce(ev, 'new'|'updated'|'cancelled')` emails every active employee (one mail, `mailer.email_for` addresses) and queues a WhatsApp DM to each via `wa_outbox` (delivered by the laptop's outbox poller, so no bridge change). `routes/office_calendar.py` calls it on create, on a PUT that changes title/date/time (also resets `reminded_at`), and on delete — only for future, notify-on events; holidays/WFH/office never notify. `reminder_sweep()` runs every 5 min (`task_scheduler._run_event_reminders`), reminds once ~25–30 min before start (claims the row via `UPDATE ... WHERE reminded_at IS NULL` first, so overlapping sweeps can't double-send), using the IST clock itself. Calendar page shows events as "3:00 PM Title" chips in an "Events" layer. **Email part is inert until SMTP is configured (OPEN TODO at top); WhatsApp works now.** Verified with a temp-DB test (23 checks: validation, HR-only, announce on create/change/delete, no re-announce on unchanged save, notify-off/holiday/past silent, one reminder in the window, fresh reminder after a move) with mailer/outbox mocked; not yet seen on a real phone or inbox.
136. **Email goes through Brevo's HTTPS API, SMTP is the fallback — 2026-10-08.** Gmail SMTP from Railway failed with `Network is unreachable`/timeouts: Railway blocks outbound SMTP on this plan (`email-status?diag=1` shows ports 587 and 465 time out; the server's IPv6 route is also unreachable). `backend/mailer.py` now picks a transport automatically: `BREVO_API_KEY` set -> Brevo (`POST https://api.brevo.com/v3/smtp/email`, header `api-key`, one request per recipient so addresses stay private and reply-all can't spray everyone; sender = `BREVO_SENDER` or `SMTP_USER` or `marketing@mmga.agency`, Reply-To preserved); else the old SMTP path. `mailer.provider()` returns `brevo|smtp|""`; `mailer.last_error` keeps the last failure text. `GET /api/companion/email-status?token=…` reports `provider`, `&test=1` sends a real test to HR and returns `test_sent` plus an `error` string on failure, `&diag=1` probes outbound ports 587/465/2525/443 over IPv4/IPv6. Brevo's sender must be verified (and ideally SPF/DKIM set for `mmga.agency` for inbox delivery; unverified-domain mail may land in spam). Tested with the HTTP call mocked (11 checks) plus the leave-email and event tests; not yet sent for real. **LIVE as of 2026-10-08:** the Brevo key is set on Railway (`BREVO_API_KEY`), `marketing@mmga.agency` is a verified Brevo sender, and a real test mail to noorish@mmga.agency returned `test_sent: true`. **Gotcha found live:** Brevo's *Security > Authorised IPs* blocked Railway's address (`401 unrecognised IP address`); it had to be switched off with **Deactivate for API keys** (Railway's egress IPs change, so allow-listing one doesn't hold). If email ever returns 401 again, check that setting first (`email-status?test=1` shows the error). Deliverability: Brevo reported the sender's DKIM as `Default` (warning) while DMARC is configured; authenticating the `mmga.agency` domain in Brevo (Senders, Domains & IPs > Domains) would improve inbox placement if mails land in spam.
137. **Applicants are told when HR approves/rejects their leave — 2026-10-08.** `leave_store.approve_leave()`/`reject_leave()` now, after the DB update commits and only if a pending row was actually changed, call `_notify_applicant()`: `mailer.notify_leave_decision()` emails the applicant at their `@mmga.agency` address (Reply-To = whoever decided, From name "<HR> via Lumina") and a WhatsApp DM is queued via `wa_outbox`. Rejections can carry a reason: `leave.html::decide()` now `prompt()`s Noorish for an optional one (Cancel aborts the rejection) and `routes/leave.py::leave_reject` already passed `reason` through; the reason is only sent in the notifications, **not stored** (no column). Notifications are best-effort in their own try/excepts, so a mail/WhatsApp failure never undoes HR's decision; a missing/already-decided request notifies nobody. Verified with a temp-DB test (13 checks incl. reason/no-reason, approve vs reject recipients, failure tolerance); email goes via Brevo (#136), WhatsApp via the laptop outbox.
138. **Standup lock banner no longer keeps showing "Check In" after you've checked in — 2026-10-08.** Cause: `standup.html` built the banner from the `?locked=1&need=checkin` query string `auth.js` redirected with, and the Check In button did `location.reload()` — which kept the same stale query string, so the banner and button reappeared even though check-in had succeeded. Fix: new `refreshLockBanner()` always asks the server (`/api/standup/lock-status`) for the current state: unlocked -> hide the banner and `history.replaceState` away the query string; still locked -> show text for what's actually missing and show the Check In button only if `needs_checkin`. The Check In button now calls it after a successful check-in instead of reloading, and adding a task calls it too (replacing the old unconditional hide). Frontend-only; logic exercised against a stubbed DOM/fetch in node (locked -> check in -> button gone -> unlocked -> banner + URL cleaned).
139. **Calendar shows what you applied for and what was rejected — 2026-10-08.** `leave_store.calendar_days()` now also reads `status='rejected'` rows. A day with a rejected request (and no live pending/approved one) keeps its normal status (full/half/none) and gains `"rejected": true`; a day with a pending/approved request still wins (so re-applying a rejected day shows the new pending request). Leave days also carry `leave_type`. `leave.html` renders: approved = solid red "On leave (approved)" / "Half-day leave (approved)"; pending = orange ring "Leave applied — awaiting approval" (half-day variants); rejected = grey ring "Leave request rejected" in a new "Rejected requests" layer (clicking it explains and invites re-applying; clicking the empty part of the day still opens the apply form). Rejected requests are only ever visible to the person who applied (the endpoint is per-session). Rejection *reasons* are still not stored (they go out by email/WhatsApp only, #137). Tested with a temp DB (8 checks).
140. **Apply-for-leave box is a composed email — 2026-10-08.** `leave.html`'s Apply modal now shows From (you, `<first name>@mmga.agency`), To (Noorish), type, from/until dates, a short reason, an editable **Subject** and an editable **Message** that are prefilled from the form (`rebuildMail()`; a field stops auto-updating once the person edits it by hand). On submit the page sends `email_subject`/`email_body` (+ `end_date`) to `POST /api/leave/apply` (capped at 150/3000 chars); `leave_store.apply_leave(..., email_subject, email_body)` passes them to `mailer.notify_leave_request(row, subject, message)`, which sends **exactly that subject and message** to noorish@mmga.agency (Reply-To = applicant) with only a short "Approve or reject: <link>" footer appended. With no custom text (e.g. the WhatsApp `set_leave` path) the old standard summary email is used. Reason stored in the DB is still the short Reason field, not the email body. Tested end-to-end through the Flask route with a mocked sender (7 checks) and the template logic in node.
141. **Hardening pass on the features the boss's PDF ("MMGA OS x LUMINA AI CRM") counts as built — 2026-10-09.** Scope: daily work by chat (§17), follow-ups (§21), meeting to tasks (§47), standup tick to "Need for approval", Sheets sync, plus the WhatsApp reply/CC changes the same day. Each fix below came from a failing hostile-input test, not from reading code.
    - **Matching a task by words no longer guesses (`whatsapp_agent._match_standup_task`).** Several tasks fitting "reel" used to mean the FIRST one was silently marked done / delegated / given a blocker. Now `prefer="open"/"done"` narrows a tie to tasks that make sense for the action, and a real tie returns `_which_one()` ("More than one task fits: 1. ...; 7. ... Ask which one") from `update_standup_task`, `delegate_my_task` and `set_task_meta`. Callers that pass no `ambiguous=` list keep the old first-match behaviour.
    - `add_standup_task` skips an identical title already on that day's list ("Already on the list ..."), collapses whitespace/newlines, accepts non-string input. `update_standup_task` rejects an unknown status instead of coercing it to done. `set_task_meta` and `_resolve_standup_date` now parse real calendar dates (`2026-13-45` matched the shape check, sorted after today, and was accepted as a standup date nobody would ever see) and cap a standup date at one year ahead.
    - **Un-ticking a standup task now pushes Notion back to `in_progress`** (`update_my_task`, only when the body carries `status:"pending"`, which only `toggleTask` sends). Before, Notion kept "Done"/"Need for approval" for a reopened task.
    - **`/api/ai/meeting-to-tasks`**: the model's JSON is taken from the outermost `[...]` if wrapped in prose; non-object items are skipped (a list of strings used to 500 the request mid-loop); dates are validated as real dates (Notion rejects the whole create on a bad one); the same notes sent twice are a no-op (sha256 of whitespace/case-normalised notes stored in `app_settings` as `meeting_notes_<hash>`, only recorded once something was actually created, so an all-failed run can be retried; `force:true` overrides); response includes `truncated` when notes exceed the 12000-char model window.
    - **Follow-up engine fallback speaks to the person** (`followups._second_person`): item texts are written about someone ("they gave Noorish ...") for the model composer, and the deterministic fallback used to send them as-is TO that person. Also `compose_followup`'s output is cleaned of leaked tool-call narration (`_clean_followup`, commit `9996baa`); a rejected draft falls back to the template.
    - **Burst stickers** (`group_vibe_sticker`) only count short (<=60 chars), non-question messages, so "is it true that the fire safety deck is due?" no longer triggers an "agree"/"fire" sticker.
    - **Sheets sync, Sheet->Lumina (`google_sheets_store.py`)**: (1) a fully blank row used to create a junk "[Post] New Idea" task, because `_row_to_fields()` defaults Type to "Post" and so `any(row_fields.values())` was never false; the Apps Script sends every row in the data range, so an empty row inserted mid-calendar triggered it. Blankness is now decided on the raw cells (`_row_is_blank`). (2) `_normalize_date` accepted `2026-13-45` (ISO shape) and passed it to Notion; now parsed for real. (3) A `None` cell became the text "None". (4) **Proportional mass-delete guard**: a delete pass removing >=5 tasks AND more than half of the client's tasks (`MASS_DELETE_MIN`, `MASS_DELETE_FRACTION`) is skipped and reported as `deletes_skipped_safety`, in both single-tab and multi-tab reconcile. This catches a truncated payload that recognises SOME tasks, which the older "recognised zero" guard did not (gotcha #93 incident class). Trade-off: deleting most of a calendar's rows on purpose will not mirror to Lumina; delete those tasks in Lumina instead.
    - **Verification**: new scratch suites `test_hardening.py` (55 checks), `test_sheets_reconcile.py` (28), plus the older suites repaired for changes made on purpose since they were written (system prompt is now a list of blocks; `_pop_pending_broadcast` returns a dict; leave needs approval and `clear_leave` only cancels pending; EOD text format; burst stickers are a keyword heuristic; follow-up test clock). 50 scratch/repo suites green. Still failing and NOT touched: `test_leave_store` (fails identically before these changes; predates the weekend-OT rule #132), the four `test_attendance_sheets*`, `test_overtime_export` and `test_laptop_agent` (old export/format assumptions). `test_standup` and `test_quickwins` end with only a Windows temp-file delete error after passing. **Not live-verified**: nothing here was run against production Notion, a real Google Sheet or a real WhatsApp message.
142. **WhatsApp agent can run on Qwen via OpenRouter instead of Claude -- 2026-10-09, `backend/llm_openrouter.py`.** Chosen for cost (Qwen3.8 27B is $0.045 in / $2.30 out per 1M tokens on its cheapest host vs Haiku about $1 / $5; real bot messages measured at about $0.0005 to $0.001 each, about 5x cheaper than Qwen3.6 27B). Qwen Cloud direct sign-up is not reliable from India, so it goes through OpenRouter (one key, OpenAI-style API).
    - **Switch:** on when `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` is set on Railway AND `WHATSAPP_LLM` (env) / `app_settings.whatsapp_llm` is not `claude`. Set `WHATSAPP_LLM=claude` to go straight back to Haiku. `OPENROUTER_MODEL` overrides the model (default `qwen/qwen3.8-27b`).
    - **Same loop, new transport:** `llm_openrouter.create()` returns an Anthropic-shaped response (blocks with `.type` text/tool_use, `.stop_reason`, `.usage`), so `handle_message`'s tool loop and `compose_followup` did not change; both call `whatsapp_agent._llm_create()`. If the OpenRouter call raises, that one call falls back to Claude (`to_anthropic_messages` converts our `Block` objects back to dicts). Only the WhatsApp agent uses this; the web chat, standup AI routes and everything else stay on Claude.
    - **Differences on Qwen:** Anthropic's hosted `web_search` tool cannot run, so it is dropped (no web search while on Qwen); prompt caching on Qwen is OPT-IN per OpenRouter's docs: the static system block is sent with `cache_control` only when the model is one OpenRouter documents as supporting it (`qwen3.6-plus`, `qwen3-max`, `qwen-plus`, the coder models; list in `llm_openrouter._CACHE_MODELS`, extend via `OPENROUTER_CACHE_MODELS`). The default `qwen3.8-27b` is NOT on that list (its host Wafer does list a cache-read price, but OpenRouter's explicit-marker support for it is undocumented, so we send no markers), so it gets no explicit caching; set `OPENROUTER_MODEL=qwen/qwen3.6-plus` to use it (about $0.325 in / $1.95 out; cache-read rate not confirmed) -- and set `OPENROUTER_PRICE_IN` / `OPENROUTER_PRICE_OUT` to its rates so the cost dashboard stays accurate; `<think>` text is stripped; reasoning is requested off. Usage is billed per provider (`model_tier` `qwen`, rate in `model_router.MODEL_COSTS`), so the cost dashboard counts it under neither haiku nor sonnet call counts.
    - **Check it:** `GET /api/companion/llm-status?token=<STORAGE_SYNC_TOKEN>` shows which model is live; add `&test=1` for one real call that also forces a tool call (the bot depends on tool calling) and returns the exact error if it fails.
    - **Verified:** 22 checks with OpenRouter's HTTP faked (translation both ways, a full tool-call loop, Claude fallback, per-provider billing, followup without tools) plus the existing agent suites. **Never run against the real OpenRouter** (no key was available): whether the host honours `reasoning: {enabled:false}` and `provider.require_parameters`, and how well Qwen picks tools on our ~35-tool list, are unproven. Watch real replies for wrong-tool or invented-task mistakes and flip `WHATSAPP_LLM=claude` if they show up.
    - **First real use, 2026-10-12 (live on OpenRouter, ~$0.0003 per call, ~9K input tokens per call because system prompt + tool list go every time):** Qwen worked for reads but failed on writes. For "add X to my daily standup" it twice replied "Added to your standup" (no tool called, nothing written) and then listed tasks copied from chat history instead of the database. Chat history (now a week, 10 turns) lets a cheap model imitate its own earlier answers. Three fixes in `whatsapp_agent.py`: (1) **no-tool guard** -- if a message matches `_TASK_REQUEST` (task/standup/add/done/show...) and the model answers with no tool call, the turn is sent back once with a "[system] call a tool" message; (2) **direct fast path** -- `_ADD_STANDUP` regex handles a plain "add X to my daily standup" in code with no model call (messages with a date word or "assign" still go to the model); (3) **replies show the full list** -- `add_standup_task` (today) and `update_standup_task` now return the updated numbered list, and `_format_standup` marks each line with the same ✅ / ❌ as the 5 PM group post (was a "done: " prefix; prompt tells the model to copy the list exactly). If another write tool misbehaves on Qwen, give it the same direct path or set `WHATSAPP_LLM=claude`.
143. **Group chat memory -- 2026-10-12, `backend/group_memory.py`.** The bot now remembers what is said in the team WhatsApp group instead of only seeing messages addressed to it. Bridge (`whatsapp-bridge/index.js`): `refreshObserveAllow()` reads `GET /api/whatsapp/groups` (`effective`, refreshed every 5 min) and `observeGroupMessage()` queues every text message of an **allow-listed group only** (nothing from other groups leaves the laptop), `flushObserved()` posts batches every 10 s (or at 25 msgs) to `POST /whatsapp/bridge {observe:true, group_id, group_name, messages:[{from,name,text,ts,id}]}`; sender is resolved from `participantPn` / the LID map like everywhere else. Backend: the observe branch stores via `group_memory.record()` only if `_group_allowed()`; **no AI call, so it costs nothing**. Table `wa_group_log` (dedup on `(group_id, wa_id)`, sender mapped to the employee name by phone, else the WhatsApp push name); `data_retention` prunes it after **30 days**. The agent: in a group, the last 40 messages (max ~3000 chars, 48 h) go in as an uncached system block each time it is addressed; new employee-only tool `read_group_chat {query?, hours?}` searches the last 30 days (works from a DM too, answers about the allow-listed groups). Bot replies and DMs are not logged. **The bridge change needs a bridge restart on the laptop (self-update does it, ~10 min); until then nothing is recorded.** If the bot says the group has nothing, check `bridge.log` for `group observe flush failed` and that the group id is in `GET /api/whatsapp/groups`. Verified with a temp-DB test (batch store, non-allowed group dropped, dedup, name mapping, search, tool, client refused, prune, context reaches the model); not seen on a real group yet. `test_group.py` (scratch) is out of date since the no-tool guard (expects 1 model call).
144. **Group intelligence: the bot tracks promises made in the group and chases them -- 2026-10-12, `backend/group_intel.py`.** Builds on #143. Researched first: no ready repo does "group chat -> commitments -> follow-up" (closest is the Follow-Through MCP server: extracts promises from meeting transcripts, checks Slack/email/Linear for completion, nudges and escalates; also the extract-then-verify pattern and Zep/Graphiti's "close, don't delete" status model), so the pieces are borrowed, not installed. **Flow:** (1) `task_scheduler._run_group_intel` every 8 min -> `group_intel.extract_pending()`: for each group with unprocessed `wa_group_log` rows (`processed` column), one model call (WhatsApp model, i.e. Qwen) gets today's date, the roster, the OPEN commitments, 12 context lines and up to 60 new messages and returns JSON `new / done / dropped / moved`. **Every item must carry a quote that is actually in the messages (`_grounded`) or it is thrown away**, so it cannot invent a promise; the owner must match a roster name, else `team` (never nudged); duplicates of an open item are skipped. No model call when nothing is new; a tiny batch is skipped only if nothing is open (a short "posted" can close a promise). Backlog older than 24 h is marked, not analysed. (2) Table `group_commitments` (owner, action, due, quote, status open/done/dropped, evidence, nudge_count). (3) **End-of-day check** via the existing follow-up engine: `followups.loose_ends_for` now calls `group_intel.commitment_items`, which uses `nudge_ready` (due today -> from 16:30; overdue -> any time; no due date -> from 17:00 the same day, or next day) and first looks for evidence: a standup task of the owner ticked done with similar words, or a Notion task Posted/Final/Done with similar title -> auto-closes silently. Only if nothing shows it is done does it surface "you said X in the group, is it done or what's holding it up?" as a `group_commitment` item; the engine's rails still apply (10-19 IST weekday, leave skipped, 20 h cooldown, 2 messages/person/day), delivery is a DM through `wa_outbox` (no bridge change), max 3 nudges per item (`MAX_NUDGES`). Not posted in the group on purpose (public pressure). (4) Agent tools `get_group_commitments {person?, status?}` and `resolve_group_commitment {which, status}` (id or words), so an owner can reply "done" to the DM and it closes. (5) Inspection: `GET /api/companion/group-commitments`, `POST /api/companion/group-intel/run` (token-gated). Closed items are pruned after 90 days. **Cost:** about one small call per active 8-minute window, none when the group is quiet. Verified with a temp-DB test using a fake model: real promise stored while an invented quote is rejected, no call when nothing new, not nagged at noon, asked at end of day, one DM queued and counted, closed when he later says "posted it", closed by a ticked standup task with no nag, tools work and a client is refused. **Not verified against real Qwen output or a real group**: how well it picks out promises in Hinglish chatter is unproven; look at `GET /api/companion/group-commitments` after a day and tune `_SYSTEM`. `mark_sent` previously counted nudges while another connection held a write; fixed by counting after it closes.
145. **Personal-assistant layer -- 2026-10-12, six additions on top of #143/#144.** (1) **Voice notes and images** (`backend/media_ai.py`): the bridge (`observeGroupMedia`) downloads audio/image messages of allow-listed groups (caps 4 MB / 5 MB) and posts `{observe_media, kind, mime, data_b64, ...}` to `/whatsapp/bridge`; the server turns them into `[voice note] <transcript>` / `[image] <description>` via an OpenRouter audio+vision model (`OPENROUTER_MEDIA_MODEL`, default `google/gemini-2.5-flash`, needs `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`) and stores them as normal group messages, so a promise in a voice note is tracked. A meme/selfie returns "skip" and is dropped. All failures return "" (media is a bonus). (2) **Decisions and client preferences**: the extractor also returns `facts` (kind decision|preference, client, text, quote; same quote-must-match check) into `group_facts`; tool `recall_group_facts {client?, query?}`. (3) **Boss morning brief** (`backend/pa_brief.py`): `gather()` collects facts in code (calendar, group promises due/overdue, decisions since yesterday, posts going live today not yet posted, approvals waiting, no standup yet, on leave); `build_brief()` has the model write it up in the assistant's voice with a plain-text fallback; `run_daily()` (scheduler job every 10 min, `task_scheduler._run_pa_brief`) sends once per weekday 09:00-11:00 IST to `alert_recipient_ids` (default emp003) via `wa_outbox`, claimed first with an `app_settings` key `pa_brief_sent_<date>`; on demand via tool `get_my_brief`; preview at `GET /api/companion/pa-brief?token=&user_id=` (`&write=1` also writes it). (4) **Corrections**: tools `reject_group_commitment {which, why}` (closes it and stores the example in `group_feedback`; the last 8 examples are appended to the extractor instructions as "LEARNED FROM CORRECTIONS") and `add_group_commitment {owner, action, due}` for a missed promise. (5) **Google Calendar, read-only** (`backend/gcal.py`): reuses the Sheets service account with the `calendar.readonly` scope; **you must share each calendar with the service account's email (shown by `GET /api/companion/calendar-status?token=`, `&test=1` fetches today) and put the calendar ids (a person's main calendar id is their email) in `app_settings` key `gcal_calendar_ids` or env `GOOGLE_CALENDAR_IDS`, and enable the Calendar API in that Google Cloud project.** Tool `get_calendar {days}`; also feeds the brief. **Email is NOT built**: reading Gmail with a service account needs Google Workspace domain-wide delegation set up by an admin; the Gmail connector on your Claude account is not reachable from the bot. (6) **Smarter extraction model**: `GROUP_INTEL_LLM=claude` (env or `app_settings` `group_intel_llm`) runs only the promise-extraction step on Claude Haiku instead of Qwen; try it if Qwen misjudges real Hinglish chatter. Verified with a temp-DB test using fakes (media endpoint incl. non-allowed group ignored, promise from a voice note, invented fact rejected, recall, reject then fed back, manual add, calendar via a fake API, brief facts and plain fallback when the model is down, client refused on every tool). **Not verified live:** the media model slug/audio format acceptance (WhatsApp sends ogg/opus; if the provider rejects it, convert to mp3 with ffmpeg on the laptop), Calendar access, and real Qwen quality. The bridge change needs the laptop to self-update.
    - **Calendar and email are PARKED (user decision, 2026-10-12).** `gcal.py`, the `get_calendar` tool and the calendar part of the brief are in the code but inert until a calendar is shared with the service account and `GOOGLE_CALENDAR_IDS` / `gcal_calendar_ids` is set; the brief just skips it and the tool says the calendar isn't connected. Email is not built. Don't revisit unless asked.
    - **Polish pass, same day:** (a) the bridge converts voice notes to a small mono mp3 with ffmpeg before sending (`toMp3`, falls back to the original ogg if ffmpeg is missing) so the speech model's format support is not a risk; (b) **fast paths in `handle_message`** (`_fast_standup_command`): plain "add X to my standup", "show my tasks / what are my tasks / my standup", "mark X done / X is done / I finished X" run the tool directly with no model (exact ✅/❌ list, instant, free); anything with a date word, another person, "all/everything", or a long message still goes to the model; add/mark-done replies show the updated full list; (c) `group_intel` gives up on a batch after `MAX_FAILS` (3) consecutive model failures instead of retrying and billing forever; (d) Hinglish examples in the extraction instructions (commitment / completed / vague / dropped phrasings); (e) `GET /api/companion/group-commitments` now also returns `status` (`group_intel.stats()`: messages total / last 24 h / waiting to be read, last message time, commitments by status, facts, corrections learned, extraction model) so you can see whether it is reading at all.

146. **Leave feature scenario audit and fixes -- 2026-10-12.** Triggered by "what if Vidit/Kshitij (CC'd on the leave mail) reply, how does it update?". **Answer: before this, it didn't.** The request mail went To Noorish with Vidit and Kshitij only CC'd, Reply-To = the applicant, and Lumina never reads inbound mail, so a reply from anyone changed nothing; only Noorish could decide, in the app. Probing 12 scenarios against the code found real gaps, all fixed: (1) **approvers are now HR + both founders** (`leave_store.APPROVER_IDS = emp009, emp001, emp004`, `approvers_for()`): first decision wins, a later one is told who decided (409 / "Already approved by X"), the other approvers get a WhatsApp saying who decided; (2) **nobody can decide their own request** (`PermissionError`; was possible: Noorish could approve her own leave) and when HR applies, only the founders are asked; (3) **each approver gets their own email with one-click Approve / Reject links (links are in the EMAIL ONLY, per the user; the WhatsApp heads-up has no links, just "approve from the email or reply here")** (`action_url`, HMAC `action_token` per request+approver+action, `GET /api/leave/act` shows a confirm page and never decides on GET because mail scanners prefetch links, the button POSTs; a tampered or reused-as-someone-else token is refused), replacing "CC"; replying to the email still only reaches the applicant, **inbound email is not parsed on purpose** (needs MX/inbound-parse setup and is unreliable to read), use the link, the app, or tell the bot; (4) new bot tools `get_pending_leave` and `decide_leave {which, decision, reason}` for approvers ("approve Nupur's leave"); (5) **validation** (`_validate_window`): a weekend-only or holiday-only request is refused, max 60 days per request, not more than 60 days in the past or 366 ahead (was accepted: 2029, a year-long request); (6) **public holidays (HR's office calendar) cost no leave** in `get_balance` and `working_days` (a leave over Diwali charged the holiday); (7) **over-balance requests are flagged**, not blocked: `shortfall` (days beyond remaining minus other pending requests) is in the approvers' email/WhatsApp as "unpaid"; (8) **approved leave can be cancelled** (`cancel_approved`, `POST /api/leave/<id>/cancel`): the owner before it starts (balance refunded), an approver until it ends, the row kept as `cancelled`; the Calendar page offers it when you click your own future approved day; (9) a still-**pending** request for today stops the login nudges (`on_leave_or_pending_ids`, used only by `GET /api/companion/not-logged-in`; it does NOT unlock the standup, which still needs approval); (10) the leave tool now reports the real refusal reason ("those dates are only weekend or public holiday") instead of "couldn't save"; (11) `/api/leave/pending` is for approvers and hides your own request; the Calendar approvals panel shows for HR and both founders. **Who can do what (user decision, same day):** Noorish (emp009), Vidit (emp001) and Kshitij (emp004) approve/reject leave AND edit the HR office calendar (`/office-calendar.html`, holidays / WFH / office / events; the office-calendar write routes now use `routes.leave._require_approver`); **Abhinav (emp003, `leave_store.VIEWER_IDS`) can only SEE**: the pending-requests panel on the Calendar page (`/api/leave/pending` returns every pending request with `can_decide:false`, buttons replaced by "view only"; the bot's `get_pending_leave` works for him) and the office calendar (no Create button, clicking shows details); his approve/reject/edit calls get 403. Everyone else is redirected away from the office-calendar page. **Not changed, by choice:** half-day on a day you also clocked in is still charged; a left employee's pending request stays listed; the 24h overtime rule is untouched. Verified with `test_leave_scen.py` (about 45 checks incl. the link page, forged/tampered tokens, session routes, tools) plus the existing leave/OT/office suites (`test_leave_email` updated for per-approver mails); **not run against real email or WhatsApp delivery**, and the links only work once `PUBLIC_BASE_URL` is right (it is on Railway).

147. **Sunday-night office schedule + calendar-change alerts -- 2026-10-12, `backend/week_digest.py`.** What Noorish puts on the office calendar (holiday, WFH, in-person office, events) now reaches everyone without opening the app. (1) **Every Sunday 21:00-23:00 IST** (`task_scheduler._run_week_digest`, interval 10 min; `run_weekly` acts only in that window, once per week via `app_settings` key `week_digest_sent_<monday>`) it sends the coming Mon-Sun: an **HTML email** (emoji, coloured pills per day, week picture and month picture inline, plain-text fallback) to every active employee, and a **WhatsApp DM to each person** (week text + week picture, then a second message with the month picture, current week highlighted). Weekend rows show only if something is on them. (2) **Mid-week changes are held, then sent (now 30 minutes after the last edit, see the second pass below; originally 60)** (`CHANGE_HOLD_MIN`; user's choice so a mistaken edit that is corrected doesn't message everyone twice): adding, editing or deleting a holiday / WFH / office day calls `queue_change`, which merges per calendar entry in table `cal_change_pending` (keeps the first "before", latest "after", restarts the timer; add-then-delete or edit-then-revert sends nothing). `flush_changes` (called by the same 10-min job, so the message lands 60-70 min after the last edit) sends everyone except the editor a WhatsApp about that date only: what changed, "Was:" on an edit, the day's full schedule. Past dates never alert. Timed events keep their own immediate announce() in `office_events.py`. (3) **Pictures** drawn with Pillow (`Pillow>=10.1` added; DejaVu/Arial fonts if present else Pillow's built-in), stored in table `wa_images` (30-day prune on write) and served unauthenticated at `/api/public/cal-image/<token>.png` (random token is the only secret). (4) **Image delivery to WhatsApp**: `whatsapp_outbox.image_token`; `GET /api/companion/whatsapp-outbox` returns `image_url`; `scripts/laptop_agent.py::job_wa_outbox` downloads it and sends `imageB64` to the bridge `/send`, which sends the picture with the text as caption (over 1000 chars goes as a separate text). **Needs the laptop to self-update and the bridge to restart (`whatsapp-bridge/index.js` changed).** A row whose picture can't be fetched is retried, not sent bare. (5) `mailer.send` gained optional `html=`. (6) Testing: `GET /api/companion/week-digest?token=` previews the text; `POST ...?to=emp003` force-sends the digest to just those ids (refuses without `to`). Verified with temp-DB suites (window/once-only, recipients, left employee skipped, outbox rows with pictures, HTML, change alert, 60-min hold/merge cases, image route) and both pictures viewed; **not yet seen on a real phone or inbox**.
